# 7.0.0

## @rjsf/antd

- **BREAKING CHANGE** Dropped support for `antd` version 5; the peer dependency range is now `^6.3.6`. `ErrorList` and `CyclicSchemaExpandTemplate` use of `Alert` always passing `title` prop now. The `6.3.6` floor isn't arbitrary: antd 6.0.0-6.3.5 crash `@rjsf/antd` forms on first render (`getRealHeight` destructuring a `null` node), fixed upstream in [ant-design/ant-design#57636](https://github.com/ant-design/ant-design/pull/57636)
- `DateWidget`/`DateTimeWidget` format a `format: "iso-date-time"` value as a naive local date-time string instead of converting it to a UTC ISO string, since that format's timezone is optional. A stored value that happens to carry an offset anyway is stripped before being parsed, so it displays as the naive wall-clock time it represents instead of being converted to the browser's local zone ([#3930](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3930))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Fixed `BaseInputTemplate` never calling `onChangeOverride` for a numeric field: its `InputNumber` branch was wired straight to `onChange` and reports only the parsed value, never an event. A numeric field with an `onChangeOverride` now renders the `Input` branch (still `type="number"`) so the override receives the `ChangeEvent`, as it does in every other theme
- Added `<optgroup>`-equivalent support to `SelectWidget` via `ui:options.optgroups`, grouping options using rc-select's nested `options` shape
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` renders an additional property's key as a `SelectWidget` of the names the parent schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows, instead of a free-text input, and takes the key from the new `keyName` prop rather than from the decorated `label`, so a `deprecated` additional property no longer offers its decoration as a key name, nor renames itself to it ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The default `Form` export is a `ThemedForm` (see `@rjsf/core`): generic over the form data, so `<Form formData={data} />` infers `T` and `<Form<MyData> />` names it. The `Theme`, `Templates` and `Widgets` exports keep each component's own generic type instead of being instantiated at the default `T` (`any` in 6.x), so `withTheme<MyData>(Theme)` and `{ ...Templates, FieldTemplate: MyFieldTemplate }` typecheck for any `T`. `generateForm()` returns a `ThemedForm<T, S, F>`; `generateTheme()`, `generateTemplates()` and `generateWidgets()` keep their signatures, apart from the new `unknown`/`FormContextType` defaults, and still build a fresh object on every call
- `FieldTemplate` renders a field's validation errors alongside its `ui:help` text instead of replacing them with it. `Form.Item`'s `help` prop is antd's only slot for either, and the old precedence meant a field with `ui:help` showed no error message at all ([#5326](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5326))
- `FieldTemplate` renders a `ui:help` passed as a React element, which it previously dropped along with the field's errors, since it gated both on the string-only `rawHelp` (see `rawHelp` under `@rjsf/utils`). A string `ui:help` was never affected, from the field's uiSchema or from `ui:globalOptions` ([#5326](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5326))
- `FieldHelpTemplate` is registered in the theme's templates and restates antd's description color on help text when the field has errors. antd tints its whole `help` slot as an error in that case, and help shares the very element that carries the error text, so the tint would render help as a second error message. This is deliberately the opposite of the `hasErrors`-keyed danger tint `@rjsf/react-bootstrap` and `@rjsf/shadcn` apply, where help is its own element below the field. It is an inline style because it has to beat the inherited tint, so a consumer restyling `.help-block`'s color needs `!important` to take the error case back ([#5326](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5326))
- Fixed object options in `RadioWidget` and object/array selects in `FieldTemplate`, and `SelectWidget` reports its form data value on focus and blur ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309), [#5317](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5317))
- Fixed `realValue` options whose `String()` matches, such as `1` and `'1'`, sharing a DOM value ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- Fixed `AltDateWidget` never passing `time`, `disabled` and `readonly` to `useAltDateWidgetProps()`, so `alt-datetime` rendered no hour, minute or second selects and emitted a date-only value, and the Now and Clear buttons still changed a `readonly` or `disabled` field ([#5410](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5410))
- `TextareaWidget` no longer replaces a `ui:options.rows` of `0` with the default of 4 rows. React leaves a `rows` of `0` off the textarea, so it gets the browser's default height of 2 rows, as with `@rjsf/core`'s `TextareaWidget` ([#5387](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5387))
- `FieldErrorTemplate` keys a React element error by its own `key` and any other error by its position, rather than giving every element error the same `...-[object Object]` key and a repeated string error the same key twice ([#5382](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5382))
- Fixed `ObjectFieldTemplate` rendering a property with no column span when the `colSpan` map in `formContext` has no usable entry for its widget or field name. The lookup now falls through to the schema type and then to the default span, skipping entries that are not a whole number of columns from 0 to 24 and a `ui:field` that names no registered component. The keys are the ones `SchemaField` renders the property by, after resolving a `$ref` with its `ui:definitions` entry and merging an `allOf`, except for the type `SchemaField` infers from the constants of a `oneOf`/`anyOf` with no `type`, and the select it infers for a boolean with labelled options ([#5516](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5516)). The type is read with `getFieldTypeForWidget()`, so a nullable type, a type inferred from `const`, `enum` or `properties`, or a type list whose `ui:widget` picks its field, matches its entry in the map, and an object declared without `type` gets the full-width default. The default span counts only visible properties. A string entry such as `{ string: '8' }`, outside the declared `Record<string, number>`, is now ignored, and a plain `colSpan` number that is not a whole number from 0 to 24, such as `25` or `12.5`, gets the default span instead of being passed to `Col` ([#5450](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5450))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `AltDateWidget`'s date parts having no accessible name: each select is named by the field's label and the translated part, such as `When, year`, even when the label is hidden, and described by the field's description, help and errors. `SelectWidget` gives the `aria-label` and `aria-describedby` it is passed to its control through `widgetAriaProps()` ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- Fixed `CheckboxesWidget` and `RadioWidget` rendering options enabled on a field whose group is disabled: every checkbox of a `disabled` field, where a click still changed the form data, and every checkbox and radio of a `readonly` one under `readonlyAsDisabled`. An option's own `disabled` overrides its group's in antd, and each option passed `false` unless `ui:enumDisabled` listed it ([#5487](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5487))
- Fixed `CheckboxesWidget` and `RadioWidget` staying enabled, and still changing the form data, under an antd `DisabledContext` such as `<ConfigProvider componentDisabled>`. The group and each option passed `disabled={false}` for an enabled field, which antd reads ahead of the context; they now leave `disabled` unset ([#5487](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5487))
- Declares its presentation: its template draws every field's description, its checkbox's included, through `fieldDefault`, so `ui:label: false` now hides the description as in every other theme. `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` labels the key through `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldHelpTemplate` renders under the help id of its field's plan, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's own `ui:help` no longer repeats the field's id ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `TitleField` carries the `titleId()` it is handed as an id, which an object's option selector is named by, rather than as a `for` naming nothing; the click handler that focused that missing target is gone ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `CheckboxWidget` carries the native `required` attribute only where its schema demands `true`, so a required `boolean` left unchecked submits as `false` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` hands `SelectedOptionDescription` its `hideLabel` and plan, so the selected option's description renders under the id the select is described by ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The widgets put their field's plan on the element a user focuses, through `getControlAttributes()`: `BaseInputTemplate`, `PasswordWidget`, `TextareaWidget` and `DateWidget` are `aria-invalid` while their errors show and described only by the elements that render, rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()`, and `CheckboxWidget`, already described that way, is `aria-invalid` too; `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget`, through `getGroupAttributes()`, name their group by the template's label through `aria-labelledby`, which its `for` can't reach, and describe each option, and `RadioWidget` marks its `radiogroup` `aria-invalid` rather than each radio; `RangeWidget` names its slider's handle by that label, or by the text of one `ui:label: false` hides, and is declared `labelledBy: 'aria-labelledby'`. The handle takes no `aria-describedby` or `aria-invalid`, so its errors are announced only through the template's ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldTemplate` marks its label required from the plan's `label.requiredMarker`, falling back to `required` without a plan, so a label it draws for a widget declared `uncheckedIsFalse` is marked only where the schema demands `true` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- With `descriptionLocation: 'tooltip'`, `FieldTemplate` renders the tooltip's popup from the start, kept hidden until it opens, so the description a control's `aria-describedby` names exists, and a field whose template draws no label, such as a checkbox, which antd shows no tooltip for, shows its description below rather than losing it. The tooltip is kept out of a `ConfigProvider`'s shared tooltip (`tooltip={{ unique: true }}`), which renders its popup only while it is open. The tooltip's icon is hidden from the name of a control the label names through its `for` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `TitleField` puts the `titleId()` it is handed on a `<span>` around the title's text, inside the `<label>` whose required class draws antd's `*` as generated content, so an object's option selector named by that id isn't named "* Pet" ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws only the heading `ArrayField` hands it as `title`, the one the array draws, as core's does, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option repeating its field's `ui:title` no longer draws it a second time, and lays out an optional array's data control with its items where it draws no heading, rather than dropping it with the heading ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `MultiSchemaFieldTemplate` lays out no row for a selector it isn't given, as while an optional `anyOf`/`oneOf` field has no data, rather than an empty `<div>` ([#5401](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5401), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out the optional data control with the properties where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading, as core's does, and renders no empty column where there is no control ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate` gives `Form.Item` the `htmlFor` that `templateLabelFor()` gives, as core's does, so a radio, checkboxes or range field's label has no `for` naming the group's wrapper or the slider's root, which a `for` can't label; the group and the slider's handle are still named by the label's `fieldLabelId()`. A selector aimed at `label[for=…]` for such a field should aim at `label:has(#root_color__label)` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `RadioWidget` focuses its checked radio under `ui:autofocus`, where Tab into the group lands, or the first a user can choose where none is checked or `ui:enumDisabled` disables it, and `CheckboxesWidget` the first checkbox a user can choose, where both took the first option, which takes no focus where `ui:enumDisabled` disables it, and `AltDateWidget`'s row of date parts is a `group` carrying the field's id, named by the template's label, as `@rjsf/core`'s is, since core declares the alt-date widgets `labelledBy: 'aria-labelledby'`, so the label draws no `for`, which named an id no date part carried, and it hands `DateElement` its plan, so each date part is described by the field's parts that render and is `aria-invalid` while its errors show. Its Now and Clear buttons are disabled with the field, so they aren't left in the tab order doing nothing. A selector aimed at `label[for="root_when"]` should aim at `label:has(#root_when__label)` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490), [#5542](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5542))
- `DateWidget` and `PasswordWidget` take `htmlName` as their input's `name`, as the other widgets rendering a named input do, so a form given a `nameGenerator` submits them under the generated name rather than the field's id ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` names an `additionalProperties` entry's key input by the key's label through `aria-label` where `ui:label: false` leaves no label drawn, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, and `SelectWidget` is `aria-required` while its field is required, since antd's select takes no `required` of its own, while `PasswordWidget`, `TextareaWidget`, `DateWidget` and each of `RadioWidget`'s radios are `required` while their field is, as `@rjsf/core`'s are, a radio of a read-only field excepted, where they never forwarded it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate` puts the label id its plan gives it on a `<span>` around the label's text, as `TitleField` puts a heading's, apart from the `*` and `:` antd draws in the label as generated content, so a group, slider handle or alt-date group named by it through `aria-labelledby` is named `Color` rather than `* Color:` in a browser. The `<span>` carries the label's `title`, and its text has a trailing colon stripped, as antd does for a string label, where `formContext.colon` isn't `false`, as `TitleField` decides it. Inside an antd `Form` given `colon={false}` or `layout='vertical'`, where antd keeps a string label's colon, the label's colon is still stripped ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `BaseInputTemplate`, `PasswordWidget`, `TextareaWidget` and `DateWidget` mark a `ui:readonly` field's input read-only where `formContext.readonlyAsDisabled: false` leaves it enabled, so the browser no longer applies `required` to it and an empty read-only text, number, password, textarea or date field doesn't block the submit; a number input there no longer keeps what is typed into it. `DateWidget` opens no popup there, and `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate`'s key input is read-only too. `RadioWidget`'s radios take no `required` while the field is read-only, disabled or not, since a radio has no read-only state to exempt it, and its group is controlled even with no value, so a click on a read-only group `readonlyAsDisabled: false` leaves enabled checks nothing ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The theme declares `block` as its `globalUiOptionsKeys`, typed by the new `AntdGlobalUISchemaOptions` and the new `UiSchema` type, which takes it inline, so a global `block` reaches `RemoveButton` outside a `LayoutGridField` too ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607))
- `ObjectFieldTemplate` keys a property's column span by the property's own `ui:widget` and `ui:field` only, since RJSF no longer reads either from `ui:globalOptions` ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607))

## @rjsf/chakra-ui

- Converted the internal `forwardRef`-wrapped UI primitives (`Field`, `Slider`, `Alert`, `NumberInputRoot`, `Checkbox`, `Radio`, `CloseButton`, and the `Select` family) to plain function components that accept `ref` as a regular prop, now that React 19 supports this natively
- `SelectRoot` is now a plain generic function (`function SelectRoot<T extends CollectionItem>(...)`) instead of a non-generic arrow function cast to `ChakraSelect.RootComponent`, removing the cast and the wrapping parens it required
- Fixed `getChakra()` deleting the non-forwardable keys straight out of `uiSchema['ui:options'].chakra`, mutating the caller's own uiSchema as a side effect of rendering. It now returns a filtered copy
- Fixed `ChakraUiSchema`, which used `Omit<UiSchema, 'ui:options'>`. `UiSchema` has a string index signature, so `keyof UiSchema` includes `string` and `Omit` erased every named member, leaving a type that accepted anything. It now intersects instead
- `BaseInputTemplate` renders a `number`/`integer` field as `<input type="text" inputMode="decimal|numeric" pattern="..." title="...">` instead of `<input type="number">`, and no longer puts `step`, `min` or `max` on it, so the rendered markup and any snapshot of a numeric field change; see the `getInputProps()` entry under `@rjsf/utils` for the reasoning and the ways back to a native number input ([#4038](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4038))
- Added `<optgroup>`-equivalent support to `SelectWidget` via `ui:options.optgroups`, using `Select.ItemGroup`/`Select.ItemGroupLabel`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `chakra-react-select` from `peerDependencies`; the package no longer uses it, so it no longer needs to be installed alongside `@rjsf/chakra-ui`
- Fixed `NativeSelectWidget` ignoring `ui:enumDisabled`: it computed each option's disabled state but never applied it to the rendered `<option>`, so every listed option stayed selectable
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` renders an additional property's key as a `SelectWidget` of the names the parent schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows, instead of a free-text input, and takes the key from the new `keyName` prop rather than from the decorated `label`, so a `deprecated` additional property no longer offers its decoration as a key name, nor renames itself to it ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `BaseInputTemplate`, `CheckboxesWidget`, `NativeSelectWidget`, `SelectWidget`, `TextareaWidget` and `UpDownWidget` marking themselves `invalid` from `rawErrors` alone, so a field stayed flagged as invalid under `ui:hideError` ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `CheckboxesWidget` marking its inner `CheckboxGroup` `invalid` whenever the field was required and empty, which flagged it before validation had found anything wrong and could not be suppressed by `ui:hideError`. It now derives that state from the field's visible errors, the same as the surrounding `FieldsetRoot`. A required, empty field is no longer flagged until validation reports an error for it, and a field with visible errors now marks every checkbox `aria-invalid`, where a non-required one used to mark only the fieldset ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The default `Form` export is a `ThemedForm` (see `@rjsf/core`): generic over the form data, so `<Form formData={data} />` infers `T` and `<Form<MyData> />` names it. The `Theme`, `Templates` and `Widgets` exports keep each component's own generic type instead of being instantiated at the default `T` (`any` in 6.x), so `withTheme<MyData>(Theme)` and `{ ...Templates, FieldTemplate: MyFieldTemplate }` typecheck for any `T`. `generateForm()` returns a `ThemedForm<T, S, F>`; `generateTheme()`, `generateTemplates()` and `generateWidgets()` keep their signatures, apart from the new `unknown`/`FormContextType` defaults, and still build a fresh object on every call
- Fixed object options in `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget`, and `SelectWidget` reports its form data value on focus and blur and offers an empty string option in the `realValue` format ([#5317](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5317))
- Fixed `realValue` options whose `String()` matches, such as `1` and `'1'`, sharing a DOM value ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- Fixed `AltDateWidget` never passing `time`, `disabled` and `readonly` to `useAltDateWidgetProps()`, so `alt-datetime` rendered no hour, minute or second selects and emitted a date-only value, and the Now and Clear buttons still changed a `readonly` field, or a `disabled` one rendered by a custom `FieldTemplate` ([#5410](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5410))
- `UpDownWidget` reports `undefined` to `onFocus`/`onBlur` when the focused element is an increment or decrement trigger, where it reported the trigger's `''` ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `AltDateWidget`'s date parts having no accessible name: each select is named by the field's label and the translated part, such as `When, year`, even when the label is hidden, and described by the field's description, help and errors. `SelectWidget` gives the `aria-label` and `aria-describedby` it is passed, or its own `aria-describedby`, to its trigger rather than its root, through `widgetAriaProps()` ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- `getChakra()` returns `JsxStyleProps`, the style props its filter keeps, instead of `Field.RootProps`, so `Fieldset.Root` and `Field.Root` both accept its result without a cast. Only a read of a non-style `Field.RootProps` member off the result, which was always `undefined`, stops compiling ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Fixed the `chakra` option never reaching a widget: every widget handed `getChakra()` its `uiSchema` wrapped as `{ uiSchema }`, which holds no `ui:options`, so the style props were dropped. `getChakra()` now takes the widget's merged `options` instead of a `uiSchema`, so a `chakra` set through `ui:globalOptions` or the `ui:chakra` shorthand applies as one in `ui:options` does. A form whose uiSchema carries a `chakra` option is now styled by it. See the upgrade guide ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** The `chakra` option of the theme's `UiSchema` type is `JsxStyleProps` instead of `Field.RootProps`. A non-style prop such as `invalid`, `required`, `label` or `onClick`, which `getChakra()` has always filtered out, is now a compile error in the `ui:options` of the uiSchema's root. A nested field's `ui:options`, `ui:chakra` and `ui:globalOptions` are not typed for it, so it still compiles there and is dropped ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- `getChakra()` keeps every Chakra style prop, where it dropped the ones that share a name with an HTML attribute, such as `color`, `width`, `height` and `display`, and `@emotion/is-prop-valid` is no longer a dependency ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- Declares its presentation: its widgets draw their own label, its checkbox its description too, and an `anyOf`/`oneOf` field is named by its option selector. `FieldTemplate` renders the description it is handed under a plan, whatever `displayLabel` says, and without one, through `templateDrawsDescription()`, only where `displayLabel` lets it draw the label, as before the plan, `CheckboxWidget` draws the description its plan gives it and is described by the ids its plan renders, and `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` labels the key through `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldHelpTemplate` renders under the help id of its field's plan, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's own `ui:help` no longer repeats the field's id ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Declares `FallbackField` as drawing its own label, so the fallback UI's value is labelled by its own widget, which no template label did before ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `CheckboxWidget` carries the native `required` attribute only where its schema demands `true`, so a required `boolean` left unchecked submits as `false` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget`'s trigger takes the plan's `aria-labelledby`, which `SelectTrigger` used to leave on the control around it, so an object's option selector is named by the object's heading ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `BaseInputTemplate`, `CheckboxesWidget`, `NativeSelectWidget`, `RadioWidget`, `RangeWidget`, `SelectWidget`, `TextareaWidget` and `UpDownWidget` take their error state from `hasWidgetErrors()`, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's control is styled invalid while the field above draws its errors, and `CheckboxWidget`, whose box was never styled invalid, takes it from there too ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The widgets put their field's plan on the element a user focuses, through `getControlAttributes()`, so each is `aria-invalid` while its errors show and described only by the elements that render, rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()`: `BaseInputTemplate` and `TextareaWidget` on their input; `UpDownWidget` on its input and `RangeWidget` on its slider's thumb, rather than on the root around them; `CheckboxWidget` on its input rather than on Ark's root `<label>`; `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` on each option through `getGroupAttributes()`, and `RadioWidget` on its `radiogroup` too; and `NativeSelectWidget` through `widgetAriaProps()`, as `SelectWidget` does, so it is described by its selected option's description too. `RadioWidget` takes `ui:autofocus`, which it ignored, as core's does, focusing its checked radio, where Tab into the group lands, or the first a user can choose where none is checked or `ui:enumDisabled` disables it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RadioWidget` names its `radiogroup` by the label it draws, under the id its plan gives it, `fieldLabelId()`, or `optionLabelId()` beneath a label a field above draws, by the label text `ui:label: false` hides through the `aria-label` its plan gives it, and leaves it unnamed where it has no label, drawing no label for a required field with no label text, which would hold only its `*` and name the group by it, where Ark named it by the legend of the template's fieldset, which is the field's description, or by an id no element had where the description wasn't shown ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldTemplate` draws the field's label where its plan gives the template the label, as for a custom widget declared `label: 'template'`, under the id its plan gives it, `fieldLabelId()`, or `optionLabelId()` beneath a label a field above draws, with the `for` that `templateLabelFor()` gives, which is none for a radio or checkboxes group named by it through `aria-labelledby`, dims it for a disabled or read-only field, as each widget dims its own, and marks it required from the plan's `label.requiredMarker`, so a widget declared `uncheckedIsFalse` is marked only where the schema demands `true`. It drew no label whatever the plan said, so such a field had none. The theme declares `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget` and its own `AltDateWidget` and `AltDateTimeWidget` `label: 'template'`, since none of them draws a label, so those fields are labelled by the template, a rating's `radiogroup` named by that label, where under `default: { label: 'self' }` they had no label at all. A widget of your own registered under one of those keys takes that declaration, so one that draws its own label should be declared `label: 'self'` under its key, as the upgrade guide's "Group labels" entry says ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `TitleField` puts the `titleId()` it is handed on a `<span>` around the title's text rather than on the box around the heading, its optional data control and its separator, and marks a required object's or array's heading `*`, which it didn't, outside that `<span>`, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, so an object's option selector named by that id is named "Pet" rather than "Pet Remove data for optional field" or "Pet *". A selector or style targeting `#…__title` now targets the `<span>` inside the `<h5>` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws only the heading `ArrayField` hands it as `title`, the one the array draws, as core's does, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option repeating its field's `ui:title` no longer draws it a second time, and lays out an optional array's data control with its items where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `MultiSchemaFieldTemplate` lays out no row for a selector it isn't given, as while an optional `anyOf`/`oneOf` field has no data, rather than an empty `<div>` ([#5401](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5401), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldErrorTemplate` renders a field's errors in one element under `errorId()`, each message on its own line, where it rendered each message under that same id, so a field with two errors repeated it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out the optional data control with the properties where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading, as core's does, and renders no empty grid item where there is no control ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` renders its select's control once: it wrapped `SelectTrigger`, which renders a `Select.Control` of its own, in another, so `select:<id>:control` appeared twice on the page ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldTemplate`'s fieldset no longer points `aria-labelledby` at a legend that renders only for a description: it points at nothing, so a field with a description is still named by its legend, and the control or group the template's label names, such as a rating's `radiogroup` or the date parts' fieldset, isn't announced inside a fieldset of the same name ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Every widget's control is named by its label and carries the field's id on the element a user focuses. The text input's, textarea's and an additional property key input's label had a `for` naming an id Ark generated rather than the input's, so the input had no accessible name, and the slider's thumb was labelled by an element that didn't exist. The checkbox's and number input's `<input>`, the select's trigger and the slider's thumb now carry the field's id, the radio and checkboxes groups carry it and each radio's or checkbox's `<input>` its `optionId()`, as core's do, so `focusOnFirstError` reaches them. A radio group's or slider's label is drawn with no `for`, which can't name either, and a slider or select that draws no label points its thumb, or its trigger and listbox, at none. Selectors aimed at Ark's generated ids, such as `select:root_color:trigger`, a radio's `radio-group:…:radio:input:0` or a checkbox's `checkbox:root_color-0:input`, should aim at the field's id. The slider's thumb takes focus under `ui:autofocus` as it mounts, as React's `autoFocus` does for a form control but doesn't for the thumb, which isn't one ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RangeWidget` marks its label `*` when the field is required, dims it when the field is disabled or read-only, and marks it invalid while the field's errors show, as `UpDownWidget`'s does; its `Field` was given none of them ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `CheckboxesWidget`'s fieldset no longer points `aria-labelledby` at a legend it draws only for a label, so a group drawing no label, as under `ui:label: false`, names nothing rather than an id no element has, and the group inside it, which carries the field's id, is named by the label it draws, under its plan's label id. That label is styled as the fieldset's legend but drawn outside a `<legend>`, which would name the fieldset too, so the group's name is announced once rather than twice ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `AltDateWidget` and `AltDateTimeWidget` are named by reference, through the `labelledBy: 'aria-labelledby'` `@rjsf/core` declares for them, so the label the template draws for them has no `for`, which named an id no element had, and the fieldset around the date parts is named by it, or by the `aria-label` its plan gives it under `ui:label: false`, rather than pointing at a legend that never renders. Its date parts are described by the field's parts that render and are `aria-invalid` while its errors show, through the plan `DateElement` now hands them ([#5542](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5542)). `CheckboxWidget`'s input drawing no label, as with no title or under `ui:label: false`, no longer points `aria-labelledby` at a label that isn't rendered ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RangeWidget` and `UpDownWidget` take `htmlName` as the `name` their form value is submitted under, as the other widgets do, where it was the field's id; `CheckboxesWidget` focuses the first checkbox a user can choose under `ui:autofocus`, which it ignored; and `TitleField`'s required marker carries `chakra-field__requiredIndicator` and takes its style from the `field` recipe, as the marker in a field's label does ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` and `RangeWidget` draw their own label under the id their plan gives it, as `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` do, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's select or slider beneath a radio selector, which draws the field's label under `fieldLabelId()`, takes `optionLabelId()` rather than repeating that id, and an option selector drawn as a select takes the field's label id; `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate`'s `propertyNames` key select and free-text key input are named by the key's label through `aria-label` where `ui:label: false` hides it, rather than left unnamed; and `NativeSelectWidget` takes `htmlName` as its `name`, where it had none ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget`'s trigger, where no label names it, as for an untitled root `anyOf`/`oneOf` selector, and its listbox are named by the field's label, its placeholder or the translated select prompt through `getControlAriaLabel()`, as `@rjsf/daisyui`'s and `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s are, rather than by nothing, and Ark's hidden native select there points at no label, where it pointed at one that isn't rendered. `SelectWidget` and `RangeWidget` take their label's id from `getLabelId()`. `SelectWidget`'s label has no `for`: one naming the trigger made a click on the label click the trigger, which opened the listbox. The label names the trigger through `aria-labelledby`, and a click on it focuses the trigger, as zag's own select label does. `CheckboxWidget` falls back to the schema `description` for an empty `ui:description`, as every field's description does, where it used to hide it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RadioWidget`'s group is `required` while its field is, so the `radiogroup` is `aria-required` and each radio `required`, and `CheckboxesWidget`'s label marks a required field with a `*`, as `RadioWidget`'s does. A click on the label `RadioWidget` or `RangeWidget` draws with no `for` focuses the checked radio, else the first a user can choose, or the slider's thumb, as zag's own labels do. `SelectWidget`, `UpDownWidget`, `CheckboxWidget` and `AltDateWidget`'s first date part take focus under `ui:autofocus`, which they ignored, and `RangeWidget`'s thumb takes it on mount only, not again each time an `<Activity>` around the form is shown. `AltDateWidget`'s Now and Clear buttons are disabled with the field ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `UpDownWidget` no longer writes the input's value from an Effect: Chakra's `NumberInput` already shows the `value` it is handed, a value set from outside the field included, so a stored `0` shows as `0`, where the input was left empty. A missing value is handed to it as `''`, so a value the form clears or resets away is cleared from the number input's own state as well as from the input, which showed the old number again once it lost the focus, and stepped on from it ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- `UpDownWidget` hands Chakra's `NumberInput` a stored number as its text, so the field no longer reports a change when it loses the focus or takes Enter without being edited, which ran `onChange`, and live validation, on a form whose data hadn't changed ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- `UpDownWidget` no longer clamps a number past ±`Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, stored or typed, to that bound when the input loses the focus, which reported a change through `onChange` for a stored number nobody edited. The widget gives Chakra's `NumberInput` no `min` or `max`, so the safe integers were its only bounds, applied on blur but not on Enter; a schema's own bounds are validated by the form ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- Declares where its widgets draw their own label: above the control, `labelPlacement: 'above'`, for every widget, the `anyOf`/`oneOf` option selector and the fallback UI's type selector, and beside it, `'beside'`, for the checkbox, so a custom `ArrayFieldItemTemplate` is handed `displayLabel: true` for an item whose widget draws its label above it
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `AltDateWidget` hands each date part its `elements[].value` as `useAltDateWidgetProps()` returns it, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, rather than mapping an unset part to `''`, which the hook now gives as `undefined` (see `@rjsf/utils`), so a custom `SelectWidget`, `onFocus` and `onBlur` see it as `undefined` ([#5378](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5378))
- The theme declares `chakra` as its `globalUiOptionsKeys`, so a `chakra` in `ui:globalOptions` reaches every widget's `options`, a custom widget's included, now that the registry keeps only the global options a form declares. The theme's `UiSchema` type types `ui:globalOptions` with `chakra` and takes a `UserExtensions` parameter, and `ChakraGlobalUISchemaOptions` is exported ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607))

## @rjsf/core

- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `GridType` and `Operators` are now `as const` objects with same-named union types instead of `enum`s, so the source is valid under Node's type stripping. Values and `GridType.ROW`-style member access are unchanged; code that used a member as a type must write `typeof GridType.ROW` instead of `GridType.ROW` ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `withTheme()` now returns a plain function component instead of a `forwardRef`-wrapped one, and `FormProps['onSubmit']`'s event parameter is now typed as `SubmitEvent<HTMLFormElement>` instead of the deprecated `FormEvent<any>`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed deprecated `Form` APIs: the `getUsedFormData()` and `getFieldNames()` instance methods (no direct replacement), the `omitExtraData()` instance method (use `SchemaUtils.omitExtraData(schema, formData)` instead), the `removeEmptyOptionalObjects` prop (already a no-op; use `omitExtraData`, which now prunes empty optional objects itself), and `boolean` values for the `liveValidate`/`liveOmit` props (use `'onChange'` in place of `true`, or omit the prop in place of `false`). Also removed the `ui:rootFieldId` uiSchema directive; use the `Form.idPrefix` prop instead
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Replaced the `extraErrorsBlockSubmit` prop with `extraErrorsAreWarnings`, inverting the default: `extraErrors` now block form submission unless `extraErrorsAreWarnings` is set to `true`, fixing [#4964](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4964)
- Fixed `validateFormWithFormData()` (used by form submission and `validateForm()`) silently dropping a `customError` raised by a field/widget's `onChange` on submit; it's now merged in and blocks submission the same way schema and (non-warning) `extraErrors` do
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed the private `_internalFormWrapper` prop from `FormProps` and `ThemeProps`; its only consumer was the removed `@rjsf/semantic-ui` theme. Use `tagName` to render a different element in place of `<form>`
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Dropped the `experimental_` prefix from `FormProps`, now that these features are no longer experimental: `experimental_defaultFormStateBehavior` → `defaultFormStateBehavior`, `experimental_customMergeAllOf` → `customMergeAllOf`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getTestRegistry()` moved to `@rjsf/core/testing` as a named export; `@rjsf/validator-ajv8`, which it needs, is an optional peer dependency that only this entry point requires. Its positional arguments remain supported. Fixed three theme test helpers that previously passed an options object instead of positional arguments
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `TimeWidget`/`DateTimeWidget` pad missing seconds and append the browser's local UTC offset to a `format: "time"`/`format: "date-time"` value whenever it's changed (e.g. `14:30:00-04:00` instead of `14:30`), so the value is compliant with those JSON Schema formats per RFC 3339 — both require seconds, and a timezone offset is required for `time`/`date-time` specifically, matching the stricter `ajv-formats` 3 upgrade in `@rjsf/validator-ajv8`. The offset is always stripped back off for display in the native time/datetime-local input, since it can't render one. `format: "iso-time"`/`"iso-date-time"` are mapped to the same widgets and still get seconds padded, but no offset added, since their timezone is optional ([#3930](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3930))
- Added `generateTheme()`, `generateFields()`, `generateWidgets()`, `generateTemplates()`, `generateButtonTemplates()` and individual component exports. **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getDefaultRegistry()` has been removed; use `generateTheme()`, one of the individual generators, or a direct component import instead ([#5266](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5266))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The `Form.getRegistry()` static is now a standalone `buildRegistry()` export from `@rjsf/core`, taking the same arguments; most callers should use the `registry` prop they already receive, or `getTestRegistry()` from `@rjsf/core/testing` ([#5266](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5266))
- `submit()` and `focusOnError()` reach the form-only members (`requestSubmit`, `elements`) behind an `instanceof HTMLFormElement` check, so they no longer throw when `tagName` renders something other than a `<form>` ([#5266](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5266))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The `data` parameter of the `onBlur` and `onFocus` props is now typed as `unknown` instead of `any`, since a field's value can be any shape. Handlers that used the value without narrowing it (e.g. `(id, value: string) => ...`) need a type guard or an explicit cast ([#5266](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5266))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Markdown requires an explicitly registered `templates.MarkdownTemplate`; core defaults to plain text. Import the renderer from `@rjsf/core/markdown`; `markdown-to-jsx` is now an optional peer dependency that only that entry point needs. Existing description/help flags remain independent and default to false. Raw HTML parsing is disabled, including file information. `FileWidget`'s file information rendered markdown unconditionally before, which is what made the file name bold; it now respects `ui:enableMarkdownInDescription` like every other translatable string, and the English `FilesInfo` string is plain text. ([#5263](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5263))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `RichDescription.TEST_IDS` and `RichHelp.TEST_IDS`, since neither component renders markdown itself any more; use `MarkdownTemplate.TEST_IDS.markdown` from `@rjsf/core/markdown` instead. Added the default `MarkdownTemplate` to the individual component exports, so it can be imported from `@rjsf/core` like every other template ([#5263](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5263))
- Fixed `ui:placeholder` being dropped for multi-select arrays, file arrays, custom array widgets and boolean fields. They read `placeholder` from props, which nothing passes, so it stayed `undefined` while the value sat unused in `options`. They now read it from `getUiOptions()`, as `StringField` does
- Fixed a `ui:FieldTemplate` or `ui:FieldErrorTemplate` override being ignored on a `LayoutMultiSchemaField`, which resolved both templates by passing `options` — the anyOf/oneOf option schemas — where `getTemplate()` expects the UI options
- `FileWidget` ignores a `change` event whose `FileList` is empty instead of forwarding it to `useFileWidgetProps`, which now reads an empty selection as a cleared value. Some pickers re-fire `change` with an empty list when the dialog is cancelled, which would have wiped the existing selection
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fields, widgets and templates identify a field by two strings instead of the `fieldPathId` object: `fieldPath` (a `FieldPath` such as `friends[0].firstName`, where the data lives) and `id` (the DOM id). `FieldProps`/`FieldTemplateProps` receive both; the other templates receive `id`. `FieldProps['onChange']` carries the `FieldPath` string instead of a `FieldPathList`, and the `childFieldPathId` compensation prop is gone. Ids are unchanged from v6, except in two cases where v6 produced a duplicate: the optional-data controls of an object or array that is itself an `oneOf`/`anyOf` schema now carry the `XxxOf`-suffixed id of the field rendering them, where v6 gave the outer and the selected option's controls the same id; and a schema property named `''`, which v6 gave its parent's id, now has one of its own at every level — directly under the root included, where v6 wrote its value over the whole `formData`. `IChangeEvent` and `FormState` no longer carry `fieldPathId` either, so an `onChange(e)` handler reading `e.fieldPathId` must be updated. Primitive props are reference-stable across renders, so `React.memo` boundaries work without deep comparison ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `IChangeEvent` is a standalone interface rather than a `Pick` of `FormState`, so the form's state can change shape without changing the public event, and every member is `readonly`. Members and types are unchanged apart from `formData`, which is now `EventFormData<T>` (see below); assign the event's `formData` into your own state rather than assigning into the event
- `Form` passes its `uiSchema` through to `getDefaultFormState()` so `ui:emptyValue` and `ui:initialValue` are applied — including to a new array row added via `ArrayField`'s "Add" button (using `uiSchema.additionalItems` for a row added past a fixed/tuple `items` schema, and `uiSchema.additionalProperties` for an object's dynamically-added property, matching the uiSchema each already renders with) — and merges a `ui:required: true` field's validation into the result via `@rjsf/utils`'s new `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()`, rather than rewriting the schema handed to the validator. That utility walks the form the same way it's rendered — resolving each node's schema (`$ref`, `allOf`, `dependencies`, `if`/`then`/`else`, the selected `oneOf`/`anyOf` branch) and uiSchema (including a `ui:definitions` fragment) against the current `formData` — so `ui:required` is enforced everywhere a field can appear, identically on the submit and `liveValidate` paths, and by precompiled validators, since the schema they were built from is never touched ([#4983](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4983))
- `SchemaField` and `LayoutGridField` resolve a field's effective required state from `ui:required` when present, falling back to the schema otherwise. `ui:required` is resolved from a field's own uiSchema only: it is not honored via `ui:globalOptions` or a `LayoutGridField` grid cell's own config object for a schema-backed field, since a form-wide or grid-level default would make the required indicator and schema validation disagree; a grid cell that instead renders an arbitrary custom `render` component is unaffected by this, since its `uiProps` (including any `required`) are plain component props passed through untouched, not schema-connected uiSchema. `SchemaField` warns when `ui:required: false` is set on a schema-required field with neither `ui:initialValue` nor `ui:emptyValue` set on that same field's own uiSchema, since the UI would show it as optional while validation still rejects an empty value. The warning is logged once per page for a given field id, through `logOnce()` (see the `@rjsf/core` entry below) ([#4983](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4983))
- Added `CoreUiOptionsChecks`, the `UiOptionsCheck` union describing `@rjsf/core`'s own built-in widgets, fields and `ui:options`. Pass it as the `Checks` type argument of `@rjsf/utils`'s new `StrictUiSchema` type (see the `@rjsf/utils` entry below) to type-check a uiSchema against `@rjsf/core`'s real vocabulary ([#4916](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4916))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed the `experimental_componentUpdateStrategy` prop. `Form` and `SchemaField` are memoized with the default shallow comparison, so props holding functions (`customValidate`, `transformErrors`, templates, widgets, fields, `formContext`, ...) must be stable references; a changed one now takes effect at once, where v6 ignored it until another prop changed ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The `formData` passed to `onChange` and the other callbacks shares unchanged subtrees with the previous value, the `formData` you passed included; treat it as immutable ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- Every state `Form` sets is shared with the previous state through `replaceEqualDeep()`, and `LayoutGridField` keeps each cell's `uiSchema` reference, so typing in one field re-renders no sibling; `renderStability.test.tsx` pins it for forms seeded with `initialFormData` and passed `formData`, nested objects, arrays, layout grids, live validation on change and on blur, submit, and a parent re-render with rebuilt but equal props ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- Fixed a `oneOf`/`anyOf` whose options are all constants (`const` or a single-value `enum`) rendering as an unsupported field when the schema omits `type`. `SchemaField` now infers the `type` from the constant values so the select renders, including for an option nested behind another `oneOf`/`anyOf`. Boolean constants keep their `boolean` type and default `ui:widget` to `select` instead, so a custom field, template or widget still sees the real type; constants that mix types, or are only `null`, use `string`, since neither has a type whose field can show every option ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
- Fixed `BooleanField` ignoring a constant `anyOf` and falling back to the generic `Yes`/`No` options, dropping the option titles, even though `optionsList()` reads `anyOf` before `oneOf` everywhere else ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
  - **Potentially breaking change:** a `type: 'boolean'` schema that carries constant options under _both_ keywords previously rendered the `oneOf` titles and now renders the `anyOf` ones. Give the two keywords the same titles, or drop the redundant one, to keep the previous labels
- Fixed `BooleanField` labelling every non-`true` constant with the `No` label, so a `oneOf` of `null`, `true` and `false` without titles rendered `null` and `false` identically; only boolean constants get a `Yes`/`No` label now ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
- `NumberField` keeps a leading sign while it is typed into the text input a `number`/`integer` field now defaults to, so `-0.5`, `-0` and `+5` are no longer rewritten to `0.5`, `0` and `5` mid-typing. A pending `-.` was rewritten to `-`, swallowing the separator rather than the sign, so `-.5` ended up as `-5`; it now reads as the `-0.5` it spells. One consequence: a `-.` left on its own resolves to `-0` where it used to leave the string `-` in `formData`, so an abandoned half-typed entry is now a submittable 0, as a lone `.` already was. A native number input never had either problem ([#4038](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4038))
- `BaseInputTemplate` renders a `number`/`integer` field as `<input type="text" inputMode="decimal|numeric" pattern="..." title="...">` instead of `<input type="number">`, and no longer puts `step`, `min` or `max` on it, so the rendered markup and any snapshot of a numeric field change; see the `getInputProps()` entry under `@rjsf/utils` for the reasoning and the ways back to a native number input. A `pattern` or `inputMode` the calling widget passes still wins over the derived one, as it did when neither was derived, and replacing the `pattern` drops the derived `title` with it rather than leaving it to describe a rule no longer in force ([#4038](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4038))
- Added `<optgroup>` support to `SelectWidget` via `ui:options.optgroups`, fixing [#1813](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/1813) and [#580](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/580). Grouping only affects how the options are displayed: a multiple select still reports its values in enum order, so adding `optgroups` never changes the order of the array written to form data
- An object whose `propertyNames` enumerates the allowed key names now renders each `additionalProperties`/`patternProperties` key as a dropdown of those names rather than a free-text input, leaving out the names its siblings already hold; the add button also creates the new property under the first allowed name that is still free, and is hidden once they are all taken rather than adding a property under a name the schema rejects. `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` receives the names as a new `propertyNamesEnum` prop, which `SchemaField` and `FieldTemplate` pass along ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- `ObjectField` prefers a name matching one of `patternProperties`' patterns when adding a property to an object that names no `additionalProperties`. A name matching none has no subschema of its own, so `retrieveSchema()` stubs it as the unusable `{ type: 'null' }`, where a matching name gives a field the user can fill in. It stays a preference: the schema allows every name it enumerates ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- `SchemaField` passes the new `keyName` prop to `FieldTemplate`, and `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` takes an additional property's key from it rather than from the decorated `label` ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- Fixed `MultiSchemaField` never passing `hideError` to its `oneOf`/`anyOf` selector widget, and `ArrayField` never passing it to the widget rendering a multi-select array or an array of files, so those widgets kept rendering their error state under `ui:hideError`; the custom-widget array path already passed it. `ArrayField` and `ObjectField` now also set the `hideError` that `ArrayFieldTemplateProps` and `ObjectFieldTemplateProps` declare, so a custom template can tell its errors are hidden ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `LayoutMultiSchemaField` passing its `FieldTemplate` neither `rawErrors` nor `hideError`, so a field rendered through a layout grid showed its error messages while the label and the control around them stayed unstyled, and a custom `FieldTemplate` could not tell that errors were hidden. Those two props now follow the same contract they do from `SchemaField` ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327)) ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- `SchemaField` now passes its `FieldTemplate` the `errorSchema` for the field, which `FieldTemplateProps` newly declares and which the container templates already declared. It carries every error whatever `hideError` says, so a template that renders the errors itself — the case `ui:hideError` exists for — can reach the ones the directive withholds, which blanking `rawErrors` left it no way to do ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- Fixed `ArrayField` never setting the `errorSchema` that `ArrayFieldTemplateProps` declares for an ordinary array, so a custom `ArrayFieldTemplate` rendering its items' errors itself saw `undefined` for `{ "type": "array", "items": { ... } }` while the same template worked for a fixed/tuple `items` array, which did receive it ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- A schema that allows more than one type (`type: ['string', 'number']`) rendered as a field for the first of them, with no way to reach the others, and a property the schema puts no constraint on — an `additionalProperties: true` entry — rendered as a field for whatever type its data happened to hold, with the same one-way door. With `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType` turned on, `SchemaField` now routes both to `FallbackField`, whose type selector offers exactly the types the schema allows (every JSON Schema type for the unconstrained property) and swaps in the field for the chosen one. A nullable `['string', 'null']` still renders as the plain `string` field it always has, as does a union whose value an `enum` or `const` already pins, and with the prop off the first type still wins, as before. A type list naming an unrecognized type alongside recognized ones offers only the recognized ones rather than every type, `null` included: a `['foo', 'null']` offers the `null` it names and nothing else, the way a plain `['null']` renders as the `null` it is, rather than treating the unrecognized name as leaving the property free to hold anything ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- `FallbackField`'s type selector offers `integer` and `null` alongside the five types it already did, and casts the form data into the chosen type for all seven: an `object` or `array` starts empty rather than keeping a value of the type it replaces, which the field then rendered as if it belonged, and a value with no text form of its own (`null`, an object, an array) clears instead of putting the literal `null` or `[object Object]` into the input. It starts on the type the current form data has, `null` included, rather than showing `object` for a `null` value, and on the schema's own first type when there is no data to match, except that a schema listing `null` first starts on the first type that can hold a value, since starting on `null` would write a `null` into the form data for a field nobody has touched. Casting to `number` or `integer` leaves the field empty for a value with no numeric reading of its own rather than writing the `0` or `1` that `Number(null)`, `Number([])`, a boolean, blank text and a `NaN` used to collapse to, since unlike the empty string a `0` satisfies `required` and `minimum` as though the user had entered it, and casting to `integer` leaves it empty for a fractional value rather than rounding it, which rewrote the data the user entered and left the original unrecoverable, since switching back showed the rounded value. Text is read as a number only in the notation the number field itself accepts, so `0x10`, `0b11` and `0o17` leave the field empty rather than coming back as the `16`, `3` and `15` nobody typed, as does text naming an infinity — `Infinity`, or a `1e999` that overflows to one — which is no JSON number at all and which `JSON.stringify()` writes out as `null`. Casting to `boolean` leaves the field empty for a value that is not there, for text a user has cleared, and for an object or array, which `Boolean()` reads as a definite `true`, rather than the definite `false` that `Boolean(undefined)` and blank text produce and `required` accepts, and reads the text of a boolean as the boolean it spells, so a `false` sent through `string` and back comes back `false` rather than the `true` that `Boolean('false')` produces, and so does text a user typed as `False`, `no` or `off`, whatever its case or surrounding whitespace. Changing the type also clears the errors raised against the value it replaced, when it had any, rather than re-asserting them against the cast value or handing the form an empty error schema it keeps as a custom error of its own and merges into every later validation ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- `FallbackField` renders the value with the schema it was given, its `type` pinned to the one chosen, rather than with a schema built from that type alone, so every other keyword still applies: a union's `properties` render for the `object` type, and `items`, `format`, `minimum` and the rest are no longer dropped. The `$id` is left out, since the value schema is not the schema that identifier names — its `type` is pinned to one of the several the schema allows — and two schemas sharing one identity also share the base URI a relative `$ref` resolves against, as is the `ui:help` of the `uiSchema` in either spelling, which the field around the value renders whether or not it renders a label. Everything else the field around it presents is turned off on the value field with a `ui:options.label` of `false`, which the templates already read for both the title and the description, rather than stripped keyword by keyword, so nothing renders twice under the same DOM id or labels the same input twice. It also gives way to the types the schema currently allows, so a `dependencies` or `oneOf` branch switch that replaces them can't leave the selector reading one type while the field below renders another, and to the shape of form data replaced from outside the form, which never passed through the selector: an object arriving where `string` is selected shows the object's own fields rather than `[object Object]` in a text input. A selection that gives way is replaced rather than merely overridden for the one render, so emptying the value afterwards no longer brings back the type the data used to hold and swaps the field out from under the user. The textual types (`string`, `number`, `integer`) still stand in for one another so that a `number` mid-decimal, which `asNumber()` holds as the string `'3.'` until the next digit, doesn't send the selector back to `string` between keystrokes. The selector is no longer marked required along with the field, since it always has a type chosen and its marker read as a second required field within the one the label already marks. It also no longer remounts on every form data change, which was dropping the keyboard focus of the user changing types. A `ui:widget` the chosen type has no widget for — `textarea`, valid for a union's `string` but not for its `boolean` — is dropped for that type rather than passed on to throw, which took the whole form down, including one a `ui:definitions` entry supplies for the `$ref` the schema was resolved from ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- A schema pairing multiple types with an `anyOf`/`oneOf` renders both selectors, the type selector wrapping the option selector, so every member of the union is reachable from within an option rather than the option being stuck on the first type. `FallbackField` keeps the options on the value schema it builds, so the option selector renders within it for whichever type is chosen, and `getFieldComponent()` excludes `FallbackField` from the rule that hands a schema carrying an `anyOf`/`oneOf` to `XxxOfField` alone, the way it already excluded `ObjectField` for the properties shared by every option. Choosing `null` drops the option selector along with the value field, since a `null` is the whole of the value and leaves an option nothing to say about it. An `anyOf`/`oneOf` whose options are all constants still gets no type selector, since those options pin the value the way an `enum` does, and with `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType` off such a schema stays one-way, exactly as in v6 ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- A union whose type resolves to one whose field renders no label of its own — an `object` or a `boolean` first in the list — renders its own `title` and `description` on the value field rather than nowhere at all, since the field around the value renders neither and the value field replaced them with the translated `Value`. Exactly one of the two fields presents the schema either way: the field around the value when it labels it, the value field when it does not. The value field no longer takes the translated `Value` as its title in that second case, which renamed every untitled multi-type property the moment the fallback UI was turned on, since that title stood where the property's own name would otherwise show
- A `ui:widget` given as a component is left to render the union itself rather than wrapped in a type selector, the way a `ui:field` already is: a control written for that schema handles the types it allows, and wrapping it pinned the schema to one of them and cast the value on every switch. A widget named by string still renders within the type selector, since it is a theme's control for one type, which is the choice the selector is there to make
- An `additionalProperties` entry whose value the user clears can still be given any type. Clearing one stores the empty string so its key survives, which the type selector read as a `string` the selection had to give way to, putting `boolean`, `number` and `integer` out of reach of a cleared value and storing the empty string under them when they were reached from a value that had no reading in the chosen type. Only such an entry stores a cleared value that way, and only for a type that empties to nothing, so an empty string arriving anywhere else — or under an `object` or `array` selection, which starts out as the empty one of its own — is still data of the `string` type that the selector gives way to. An input clearing to the value its `ui:emptyValue` names is read the same way, whatever that value is rather than only a `null`, so a `ui:emptyValue` of `false` or `0` no longer takes the selector to `boolean` or `number` the moment the user empties the field. Both readings are confined to a type that empties to nothing, since an `object` and an `array` start out as the empty one of their own and render no widget a `ui:emptyValue` belongs to, so a value matching either convention under one of them is data from outside the form that the selector gives way to
- `FallbackField` builds the schemas, the `uiSchema` and the `getDisplayLabel()` call behind them only when `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType` is on, rather than for every typeless or unknown-type field in a form that renders the unsupported-field template instead
- A union carrying an `anyOf`/`oneOf` that `shouldRenderOptionalField()` renders as an optional data control hides its label until there is form data, the way the same `anyOf` without the union already did. The label named an option selector withheld until the data arrives, and the value field the fallback UI renders that selector within is labelled `false`, which left the outer field the only place it could have come from
- The type selector renders no label of its own when a `ui:options.label` of `false` turns off the label of the field it sits in, rather than leaving a `Type` label standing over a field whose own label the caller asked for none of
- A `ui:widget` given as a component no longer withholds the type selector from a schema with no type of its own — an unrecognized `type`, or an `additionalProperties` entry left unconstrained — the way it does from a union. Such a schema lists no types for a control written against it to handle, since the type its field renders was guessed from the data rather than named by the schema, so the selector stands and the widget renders within it for whichever type is chosen
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Clearing a custom error a field raised through `onChange(value, path, errorSchema)` now also removes it from the error list the `ErrorListTemplate` displays, where it used to stay until the next validation ([ownership RFC](https://github.com/jimmycallin/react-jsonschema-form/blob/rfc/form-state-ownership/rfcs/form-state-ownership-spec.md), lane A2/A3)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `noValidate` now also turns off live validation on blur; `liveValidate='onBlur'` used to validate and display errors regardless of it, where `liveValidate='onChange'` and submission both honored it
- `Form` derives its state and applies each change through pure functions. The internal `getStateFromProps()` method is gone with that; it was never part of `FormRef` ([ownership RFC](https://github.com/jimmycallin/react-jsonschema-form/blob/rfc/form-state-ownership/rfcs/form-state-ownership-spec.md), lane A2/A3)
- Fixed `extraErrors` and a field's own `customErrors` being appended to the displayed error list again on every change that raises a custom error while schema validation errors are already on display; the re-merge now starts from the validator's own error list, matching the `errorSchema` it already started from ([#5041](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5041))
- A schema change clears `schemaValidationErrors`/`schemaValidationErrorSchema` along with the displayed errors, so a later `extraErrors` change no longer brings the previous schema's errors back; `noValidate` clears the two the same way, where it already cleared the displayed errors
- Live validation of data replaced from outside, or re-run for a changed validation callback, validates with the schema resolved for the data whenever the schema utilities are unchanged, as an edit does, so a replaced value shows the same live errors typing it would; the errors of a derivation are reconciled by one `reconcileErrors()` function shared by every derivation
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FormRef.validate()` takes only the form data: `validate(formData)`. Its optional `schema`, `altSchemaUtils` and `retrievedSchema` parameters existed for the form's own validation passes, which now call the derivation pipeline directly; to validate against another schema or validator, build the utilities for it and call `schemaUtils.getValidator().validateFormData(...)`. With them go the `FormRef` type's `S` and `F` type parameters, which nothing on the handle used any more: it is `FormRef<T>` ([ownership RFC](https://github.com/jimmycallin/react-jsonschema-form/blob/rfc/form-state-ownership/rfcs/form-state-ownership-spec.md), lane A2/A3)
- Fixed the changed-field error clearing that deriving state from new props does without live validation reaching the field but not the error list, so the `ErrorList` and the `onChange` payload kept an error the field no longer showed ([#5341](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5341))
- Fixed an `ErrorSchema` a field raises through `onChange`, on a path that already carried a validator error, being lost from the error list and on the next state derivation. A raise at any path, the root included, now replaces the validator's errors below it in both the display and the stored result (an `ArrayField` reorder, remove or copy included), and with no validator error there a root raise replaces the custom errors the same way, so `{}` or `{ __errors: [] }` clears them and its nested errors are kept; what the field hands back beyond the validator's own errors is taken for `extraErrors`/`customErrors` and not stored, so they neither list twice nor outlive the parent clearing them; an emptied path is removed rather than left as an empty entry; and a validator error whose message is still raised keeps its place, `name`, `params` and `schemaPath` ([#5341](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5341))
- Fixed an `ArrayField` add, copy, move or remove dropping the array's own errors, such as `minItems`, from the field ([#5341](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5341))
- Fixed an additional property added from outside the form after mount not rendering
- Fixed a field edit being dropped when the form data root is `null` or `undefined`; it now creates the object or array the field lives in
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `edit` from `IChangeEvent`. It only restated the props passed to the form (whether `formData` is defined, or whether `initialFormData` was passed); check your own props instead
- Fixed a throw from a consumer's callback taking the form down. A `customValidate` that throws under `liveValidate: 'onChange'` no longer blocks later edits, and a throwing `onChange`, `onSubmit` or `onError` no longer unmounts the form: the error comes out of the `FormRef` call or event that caused it, or is reported as an uncaught error when a field's own change, blur or focus caused it
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `T` defaults to `unknown` and `F` to `FormContextType` throughout, following `@rjsf/utils`. `onChange`/`onSubmit` handlers written without a type argument receive `unknown` form data; pass a type to `Form` (or to `IChangeEvent`) to get it back, or pass `formData` and let `Form` infer it. `FormProps` infers `T` from `formData`/`initialFormData`, and from its configuration props and handlers only when no data is passed, so an unannotated `UiSchema`, `CustomValidator`, `IChangeEvent` handler or widget next to typed data neither fails nor widens `T`; an unannotated `createRef<FormRef>()` still makes it `unknown`. See the v7 upgrade guide. The `F` default is a rename only: `FormContextType` still aliases `Record<string, any>`, so `formContext` reads are unchanged until it is retyped in a follow-up
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `withTheme()` returns a `ThemedForm<T, S, F>` (a new exported type) instead of a `ComponentType<FormProps<T, S, F>>`. A `ThemedForm` is a function component that is itself generic over the form data, so `<ThemedForm formData={data} />` infers `T` from `formData` and `<ThemedForm<MyData> />` names it, the same as `Form`; the `T` given to `withTheme()` is the widest data the theme's components accept rather than the one type the result renders. An annotation of the return value with the old `ComponentType<FormProps<T, S, F>>` type still compiles but fixes `T` again, losing that inference, and component statics such as `displayName` are no longer part of the returned type
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `IChangeEvent.formData` is a required member typed `EventFormData<T>` (a new exported type), and `FormRef.getFormData()` returns the same: `T` when the form data is an object or array, which no change below the root replaces with `undefined`, and `T | undefined` for a scalar root, which clearing its field leaves `undefined`. A write at the root itself, `setFieldValue('', undefined)` or a root-level custom field or widget calling `onChange(undefined, ROOT_FIELD_PATH)`, does leave an object root `undefined`, which the type does not reflect. An object form's `onChange={({ formData }) => setData(formData)}` now type-checks against `useState(initial)`; a hand-built `IChangeEvent` has to include `formData`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `withTheme()` no longer spreads the theme object onto `<Form>` as props: only its `fields`, `widgets` and `templates` are used. A non-theme prop such as `noHtml5Validate` in the object passed to `withTheme()`, which `ThemeProps` never allowed, is now ignored; pass it to the themed `Form` instead
- `FormRef.setFieldValue()` takes the field's value as `unknown` rather than `T`, the whole form's data type, so a typed form's ref can set a field
- `SchemaField` and `MultiSchemaField` log their uiSchema misconfiguration warnings through `logOnce()`, so each is logged once per page rather than on every re-render. Those warnings now name the field by its id followed by its `fieldPath` (e.g. `"root_shipping_street" (shipping.street)`, or just `"root"` at the root) rather than by its `name` or title, so two misconfigured fields that share a name are each still reported. Both are needed: ids join their segments with `idSeparator`, so a property named `a_b` and a nested `a`/`b` share one id, while the `FieldPath` grammar can't collide but carries no `idPrefix` and so can't tell two forms on a page apart ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
