- `SchemaField` passes the `ui:help` it resolved to `FieldTemplate` as `rawHelp`, rather than only passing it when it is a string, so a `FieldTemplate` that gates on `rawHelp` to decide whether to render `help` no longer drops a `ui:help` given as a React element ([#5326](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5326))
- A `ui:help` on a layout grid's discriminated `oneOf`/`anyOf` cell renders, which `LayoutMultiSchemaField` dropped entirely: it passed `errors` and `rawErrors` to its `FieldTemplate` but never `help` or `rawHelp`, so the cell produced no help text in any theme ([#5326](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5326))
- `ObjectField` and `ArrayField` fall back to one shared empty `uiSchema` when none is passed (e.g. a custom field rendering them directly), so their memoized options and callbacks stay stable across renders. That shared `uiSchema` is frozen, so a custom `ObjectFieldTemplate` or `ArrayFieldTemplate` that writes to the `uiSchema` it receives when none was passed now throws a `TypeError` ([#5366](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5366))
- `Form` no longer re-derives its data when only the `validator` or `customMergeAllOf` prop was rebuilt. Both are compared by identity, and a parent commonly writes them inline, so a re-render replaced data the user had cleared or switched away from with the very default it came from — a switch to a `null` `oneOf` option, for instance, snapped back to the option the root `default` describes. The data is re-derived for the props that decide what it should be: the `schema`, a `ui:emptyValue` in the `uiSchema`, the one part of it the defaults are computed from after mount (`ui:initialValue` applies on mount and `reset()` only), and `defaultFormStateBehavior`, itself now compared deeply, with an `undefined` setting counting as unset, so that rebuilding it with the same settings is not a change either. Any other `uiSchema` change, a title or a widget written inline, leaves the data as it is. A `validator` or `customMergeAllOf` that does change how the schema resolves is not told apart from a recreated one, so it no longer fills in the defaults of the newly resolved schema or sanitizes data only the old resolution described, and a later edit does not sanitize it either; remounting the `Form` with a new `key` derives its data afresh from `initialFormData`, dropping the user's edits ([#5294](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5294))
- Fixed `liveValidate: 'onBlur'` validating whenever the form derived state from new props, rather than only when a field was left, so replacing the form data from outside showed the errors before any blur; the same conflation also merged every `extraErrors` entry in twice on that path
- Fixed live validation dropping every `extraErrors` entry when the form derived state from new props, so a server-supplied error disappeared as soon as the form data was replaced from outside
- Fixed `extraErrors` and `customErrors` being merged into the errors a second time whenever the form derived state from new props under `liveValidate: 'onChange'` without re-validating, so a re-render changing only `className` duplicated every server-supplied error, the duplication half of [#4408](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4408) (the clearing half was fixed by `getDerivedStateFromProps` in v6)
- Fixed a changed `validator`, `customMergeAllOf` or `defaultFormStateBehavior` leaving the previously retrieved schema in state, so live validation kept running against a schema the old settings had resolved
- `ObjectField` passes the object's own errors to `ObjectFieldTemplate` as `rawErrors`, as `ArrayField` does to `ArrayFieldTemplate` ([#5332](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5332))
- `SchemaField` no longer passes a field's own errors as `rawErrors` to the field it renders beside a `oneOf`/`anyOf` option selector, such as an object's `ObjectField` or a `ui:field` that doesn't replace the selector, since the selector is given them, and `FieldErrorTemplate` is already skipped then. An `ArrayField` rendered there still carries them in the `ErrorSchema` it raises when it adds, copies, removes or reorders an item, rather than dropping them. `MultiSchemaField` passes the field's `id` to `MultiSchemaFieldTemplate` ([#5332](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5332))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A schema with both `anyOf` and `oneOf` renders from its `anyOf`, and a select over object or array constants renders through `StringField`. See the upgrade guide for details ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309), [#5317](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5317))
- `OptionalDataControlsField`'s Add button stores `[]` rather than `{}` for an `anyOf`/`oneOf` schema whose options are all arrays, taking the type from `getOptionalDataControlsType()` ([#5316](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5316))
- A `$id` naming `FallbackField` no longer replaces a non-select `anyOf`/`oneOf` with an unsupported-field box while `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType` is off. Without that prop the field renders the unsupported-field template, which renders nothing of the options, so the option selector renders instead, the way it does for any other field with nothing of its own to show beside it ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- `ui:fieldReplacesAnyOrOneOf` takes the `anyOf`/`oneOf` away only for a `ui:field` that resolves to a field other than `FallbackField`, and never for one that resolves to the fallback UI, which renders the options itself. A name no field is registered under now renders them too, where an object union used to render nothing at all ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- A `ui:field` resolving to `FallbackField` — by name or by component — renders one type selector over one value field instead of exhausting the browser's memory ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- The fallback UI's type selector offers the type a schema names, or the types an `enum` or a `const` pins its value to, rather than all seven, so a schema reached through a `ui:field` or a `$id` naming `FallbackField` is not offered types it rejects. An unrecognized `type` alongside an `enum` or a `const` offers the types those values have for the same reason; a type list and an unconstrained `additionalProperties` entry offer what they did before ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- The fallback UI renders no type selector for a schema that allows a single type, since there is nothing to choose between, and renders the field for that type alone, without the `FallbackFieldTemplate` that lays a selector out above it — whose wrapper every theme gives a card or padding of its own, leaving a blank row above an ordinary field. This reaches a `type` list naming one recognized name alongside unrecognized ones, such as `['foo', 'null']`, an `enum` or a `const` whose values all have one type alongside an unrecognized `type`, as well as the schemas a `ui:field` or a `$id` names the field for ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- A non-select `anyOf`/`oneOf` supplies the types for a schema that says nothing about its own whichever way the fallback UI was reached for it, so a `ui:field` or a `$id` naming `FallbackField` no longer wraps such a union in a selector offering all seven types. An option naming its own type overrides whatever type that selector pinned, so switching type changed nothing on screen while casting the value away ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- A schema that only implies its type is rendered by the field for the implied one, so a schema with `properties` and no `type` reached through a `ui:field` or a `$id` naming `FallbackField` renders those properties, rather than a selector of all seven types in their place, or nothing at all where a non-select `anyOf`/`oneOf` was read as supplying the types. `getSchemaOwnTypes()` answers from `getSchemaType()` for such a schema, so the fallback UI and every other reader agree about what it holds ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- A `ui:field` the `anyOf`/`oneOf` options are rendered in place of no longer reaches those options. An option that declares no uiSchema of its own is rendered against the parent's, so each one rendered the field this schema had already declined: for a `ui:field` naming `FallbackField`, a selector of all seven types over each option's fields ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- A schema whose only allowed type is `null` keeps its non-select `anyOf`/`oneOf`: the fallback UI renders no type selector for one type and drops the options once `null` is the type in effect, since a `null` is the whole of the value and no option can describe it, so it is not the one that renders them. Such a schema — a `['foo', 'null']` beside an `anyOf`, say — rendered neither the options nor a field for them with the opt-in on, while the same schema renders the option selector with it off ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- Fixed `realValue` options whose `String()` matches, such as `1` and `'1'`, sharing a DOM value; the examples `<datalist>` no longer renders duplicate keys or suggests `null`, object or array values, and isn't rendered or referenced by the input when nothing is left to suggest ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `BooleanField` labels every boolean `enum` value `ui:enumNames` doesn't name Yes/No, and applies `ui:enumOrder` to its Yes/No options, a plain boolean's included, dropping a value the order doesn't list as it does for any `enum`. A constant option's empty `title` is kept as an empty label rather than read as Yes or No. `ui:enumNames` and `ui:enumOrder` apply only to `enum` values, and it warns when they're set beside constant options or when an `enum` beside a non-constant `anyOf`/`oneOf` hides option titles. See the upgrade guide ([#5319](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5319))
- `Form` computes the `formData` handed to `customValidate` with its own `SchemaUtilsType` and passes it to the validator, so those defaults honor the form's `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior`, which they never did before ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `LayoutMultiSchemaField` not passing `classNames` or `style` to its `FieldTemplate`, so a discriminated `oneOf`/`anyOf` rendered through a `LayoutGridField` now carries the same `rjsf-field`, `rjsf-field-<type>` and `ui:classNames` classes as any other field, plus `rjsf-field-error` when it has visible errors, and the `ui:style` the field declares. Two things change for a form that already renders one: a CSS rule matching `.rjsf-field` or `.rjsf-field-error` now reaches these cells, where it never did before; and the class names and style the `FieldTemplate` consumes are kept out of both the options and the `uiSchema` handed to the selector widget, following `SchemaField`, so a custom selector widget that styled itself from `options.classNames`/`options.style` or from `getUiOptions(uiSchema)` no longer receives them — that styling is now applied once, by the `FieldTemplate` wrapper. The field also passes the `hidden`, `formData`, `description` and `rawDescription` props it never set, so `ui:widget: 'hidden'` hides such a cell and a `ui:description`/`schema.description` on it renders. See the upgrade guide ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- `SchemaField` builds its `FieldTemplate`'s class list with the new `getFieldClassNames()` and strips the styling it consumed with the new `omitConsumedStyling()`, so a custom field rendering its own `FieldTemplate` can spell the `rjsf-field*` contract the same way ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A layout grid's discriminated `oneOf`/`anyOf` cell resolves `ui:disabled`, in both directions, and a `deprecated` schema as any other field does, with the new `getDeprecatedHandling()`, where it ignored both ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- `IconButton` renders a React element `icon` as-is, where it used to put it in a `glyphicon-[object Object]` class name ([#5382](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5382))
- Fixed a new `additionalProperties` entry with an `integer` schema, or a nullable `type` list such as `['integer', 'null']`, `['number', 'null']` or `['boolean', 'null']`, defaulting to the string `New Value`, which fails its own schema; it now defaults to `0` (or `false` for `boolean`) like a non-nullable `number` (or `boolean`) schema. An explicit `null` `const` or `default` is kept, where it became `New Value` before. Any other `type` list defaults by its first type, so `['integer', 'string']` gives `0` where it gave `New Value`. A nullable schema written as an `anyOf`/`oneOf` with a `null` option still gets a default that fails the schema, as before ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- `RichDescription` and `RichHelp` pass the same frozen empty `uiSchema` as `ObjectField` and `ArrayField` to the `MarkdownTemplate` when none is passed, so a custom `MarkdownTemplate` that writes to that `uiSchema` now throws a `TypeError` ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- A non-boolean falsy `ui:label`, such as `0` or `null` from an untyped JSON uiSchema, hides an object field's title and description and the fallback UI's type selector label, as it already hid a scalar field's label ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- The warning about a schema-required field that `ui:required` makes optional is also logged for a non-boolean falsy `ui:required`, such as `0` or `null`, which removes the required marker just as `false` does ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- `MultiSchemaField` validates a retrieved option under an `$id` derived from its content, so a precompiled form no longer throws when an option switch is set back and the form keeps the option it left ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338))
- `MultiSchemaField` checks that the data still fits the option it keeps when `transformChange` rewrites an option switch by handing the validator a `$ref` to where the option sits in the root schema, so a precompiled form no longer throws there, and the option is read as JSON Schema reads it: a `then` beside the option adds to it rather than overriding its keywords ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338), [upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change))
- A `ui:field` or `LayoutGridField` cell `render` given as a `memo()`, `forwardRef()` or `lazy()` component now renders. One that isn't a usable component logs a warning naming its field or cell instead of being silently ignored, and an ignored `ui:field` renders its field as though no `ui:field` were given, while an empty one such as `null` or `false` still opts its field out ([#5363](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5363))
- Fixed a `type` list of three or more entries starting with `null`, such as `['null', 'string', 'number']`, rendering `NullField`: it renders the field for its first non-null type, or, when that type is a `string`, `number`, `integer` or `boolean`, the field of another of those types the list names when only that type has the widget it renders with, such as `StringField` for a `textarea` on a `['null', 'number', 'string']` or `BooleanField` for a `checkbox` on a `['number', 'boolean']` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- With `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType`, a `type` list holding no value starts its type selector on the type of the field its `ui:widget` renders, so a required `textarea` on a `['null', 'boolean', 'string']` starts on `string` rather than on an unchecked checkbox holding no value. A value of a type the list doesn't name starts on that type too when its field can show the value, as a `null` does, and otherwise on a `string`, `number`, `integer` or `boolean` type whose field drops the widget, wherever the list names it, so a `range` on a `['number', 'string']` handed an object renders a text input. A global `widget` the selected type has none of is dropped from the value field, as a local one is, rather than throwing `No widget` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Fixed a fixed-items `ArrayField` throwing `Cannot read properties of null` for a `null` held by a `type` list naming `array`, such as `['null', 'array']` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Fixed adding a property to an `ObjectField` holding a string, which a `type` list naming `object` such as `['null', 'object', 'string']` can, spreading the string's characters into the new object ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- A new `additionalProperties` entry under an `enum` or a constant `anyOf`/`oneOf` starts on its type's default only when that is one of the options and not in `ui:enumDisabled`, and otherwise on the first option that isn't, so a `type: 'string', enum: ['a', 'b']` starts at `'a'` rather than `New Value`, and an `enum` of `[null, true, 'a']` on a `['null', 'boolean', 'string']` at `null` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Fixed checking an option of a multi-select `ArrayField` holding a `null`, which a `type` list such as `['null', 'array']` can, writing the `null` into the array beside the checked option ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- `ObjectField` seeds a new property from the subschema that will render it — a matching `patternProperties` pattern, `additionalProperties`, or an `unevaluatedProperties` where neither answers for the name — honoring that subschema's `const`, `default` and type, instead of the `null` only a field for `null` can render ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` seeds `null` for a name forbidden by an `unevaluatedProperties: false` or by a matching `patternProperties` subschema of `false`, as it already did for `additionalProperties: false` ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` adds a new property under a free `propertyNames.enum` name the schema describes, preferring a name a field can be rendered for over one matched only by a `false` pattern, and any name the schema does not forbid over one it does ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `ObjectField` seeds a new property from an `additionalProperties` carrying a `$ref` beside other keywords with those keywords layered over the referenced ones, where it used to resolve the `$ref` alone and discard them. An `additionalProperties: { type: 'string', $ref: '#/definitions/aNumber' }` now seeds and renders a string ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` seeds a new property whose subschema offers options that disagree about the type with a value of the first option's type, the option its selector opens on, where it seeded the `New Value` string none of the primitive options accepts ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` renders the extra keys the form data holds, and offers the add button for another, for an object whose only keyword describing those keys is an `unevaluatedProperties`; both used to need an `additionalProperties` or `patternProperties` named beside it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` reads an option spelled as a `$ref`, or composed of an `allOf`, through the subschemas merged into it for the type it seeds, so an `additionalProperties: { anyOf: [{ $ref: '#/definitions/aNumber' }, { type: 'boolean' }] }` no longer seeds the `New Value` string into the numeric input the selector opens on ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` re-seeds a property renamed onto a name another subschema describes, so a `newKey` holding `New Value` renamed onto a numeric pattern starts at `0` rather than leaving that string in a field that cannot show it. Only a property the add button seeded and nothing has written to since is re-seeded, so the data the form mounted with and anything the user entered are kept. A name the object's own `properties` declare is seeded from that property's schema and its own `uiSchema` entry, both of which the field that name renders reads ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` resolves the subschema it seeds a new property from against the `default` that subschema carries, so an `if` inside it is settled by the value the property is about to hold rather than satisfied vacuously by an empty object ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `FallbackField` counts an `unevaluatedProperties` as describing a union-typed object's members, so the `additionalProperties: true` it writes in for an object the schema says nothing more about no longer silences an `unevaluatedProperties: false` or offers an add button for keys the schema forbids ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `ObjectField` drops its record of a seeded property once the form data says what became of it, rather than when the write is made, so an add, a rename or a change that `transformChange` refuses leaves the property re-seedable: a rename set back used to cost the property its history, and the next rename left a `New Value` string in a numeric field ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `Form` is now a function component that applies each edit as it is made, where the class queued edits until React rendered. Type its ref as `FormRef<T>`, and read data through its methods or the event instead of the instance's `state`, `setState()` or `formElement`. Two things change for timing-dependent code: `onChange` runs before the inputs show the new value, and a later edit in the same event builds on the one before it whatever your `onChange` did with it. The upgrade guide shows the change for [the ref](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#reading-form-internals-through-a-ref) and for [each timing pattern](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#form-owns-its-data-breaking-change) ([#5438](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5438))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Raised the `react` peer dependency floor from `>=19` to `>=19.2`, the release that added `<Activity>`, whose hidden content `Form` treats as unmounted
- A `dependencies` or `if`/`then`/`else` change that swaps a different subschema in for a property now applies the newly selected subschema's `default` where the property still holds the one the subschema being swapped away declared. Sanitizing compared the two defaults only for a property holding a scalar, so an object- or array-typed one kept the old branch's value. The new default is written in place, so a branch that declares it only while the key holds a value keeps applying. A value edited away from the old default is kept, and so is one that either subschema does not itself declare a `default` for. Two consequences worth stating: a value equal to the old default is replaced no matter who supplied it, including one passed in `initialFormData`, because the two cannot be told apart; and a change can rewrite a sibling that still holds the old branch's default. Values are matched per key of the level that declares the default, so a `default` on a nested object replaces an untouched leaf while keeping an edited sibling, and one declared a level above keeps the whole object once any leaf of it is edited. A key whose replacement a later swap retypes takes that schema's default, or is left empty where it declares none ([#5349](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5349))
- Replacing the data from outside, as `setFieldValue('', data)` does, on a form that is not live-validated clears the validation errors of a root value it replaced: a root string, number or array, or a root that changed type, kept the errors of the value before, since only the properties of a root object were compared. A root array of the same length keeps the errors of the items that did not change, as an array under a property does. The root object's own errors, such as `minProperties`, are now cleared when a property changes, as those of every other object or array holding a changed field already were, fixing the rest of [#5034](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5034)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** An error a field raises through `onChange` is kept apart from the validator's errors, where one raised over a validator error used to be written into the validator's result. A parent that stores what `onChange` reports and passes it back as `formData` therefore keeps the raise, fixing [#5347](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5347), and an empty raise clears the field's earlier raise along with the validator's error, the second case in [#5348](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5348). The raise now stays on display after the next validation and is listed after the validator's errors; see the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#an-error-a-field-raises-is-kept-apart-from-the-validators-breaking-change)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The `errorSchema` passed to a field's `onChange` holds only the errors that field raises. The form no longer takes out of it, by comparing messages, the validator's errors and `extraErrors` a field handed back, so a field's own error that reads the same as an `extraErrors` message is kept when the parent clears `extraErrors`, fixing [#5348](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5348). `ArrayField` and the optional data controls no longer hand their `errorSchema` back: the form moves the errors of the items an `ArrayField` adds, copies, removes or reorders, and removing optional data or an additional property clears the errors at and below it, where an error a field below it had raised used to stay and keep the form from submitting. Likewise, renaming an additional property moves the errors under its key to the new name, and switching the option of a `oneOf`/`anyOf` drops the errors the fields raised at the field's own path and under every key the option that was left declared or the data held, and the validator's under the keys the new option does not declare, keeping everything under the properties the schema declares beside its options. A raise is shown on the change that makes it under `noValidate` too, where it used to wait for the next render the form derived from its props. See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#the-errorschema-passed-to-onchange-holds-only-the-fields-own-errors-breaking-change)
- `FormState.customErrors`, a private member of the form's state, is the `ErrorSchema` of the errors fields raised rather than an `ErrorSchemaBuilder` holding it
- `Form` lists an `extraErrors` message once when the validator reports the same message at the same path, where it was listed twice: in the `ErrorList`, on its field, and in the `errors` passed to `onError` and `onChange` ([#5442](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5442))
- `DateTimeWidget` shows a value of `0` as the epoch date instead of an empty field. `true` no longer shows a date in 1970, and a value `Date` can't convert, such as a bigint or a symbol, no longer crashes the render; an invalid `Date`, text that isn't a date and a date whose local year is before 1 give an empty input (see `utcToLocal()` under `@rjsf/utils`), fixing [#5431](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5431)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `AltDateWidget`'s date parts having no accessible name: each select is named by the field's label and the translated part, such as `When, year`, even when the label is hidden, and described by the field's description, help and errors. `SelectWidget` gives the `aria-label` and `aria-describedby` it is passed to its `<select>` through `widgetAriaProps()` ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- `AnyOfField` re-matches its selected option to changed data while rendering, so an option that no longer describes the data is never committed for a pass, and `LayoutMultiSchemaField`'s memo of its enum options depends on the values it reads. A `ui:title` of `null`, which the types do not allow, is read as unset, so the field's title and an option's label fall back to the schema's `title`. Neither component, nor `FallbackField`, holds a lint suppression or a destructuring default the React Compiler skips a component for ([#5482](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5482))
- `SchemaField` memoizes the retrieved `anyOf`/`oneOf` options it hands `AnyOfField` and `OneOfField`, and the `uiOptions` it resolves, so a re-render that changes neither the schema nor the data no longer hands the option field a new `options` array, which had it retrieve every option again and rebuild its option-change callback each time ([#5482](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5482))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `AnyOfField` and `OneOfField` render the `options` they are handed as they are: `SchemaField` retrieves each option for the data once and hands the retrieved schemas on, where the option field retrieved them a second time, so a keystroke inside an option resolves each option once. A custom field that renders `AnyOfField` or `OneOfField` itself with the raw option schemas now retrieves each one first, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#anyoffield-and-oneoffield-take-their-options-already-retrieved-breaking-change) shows ([#5482](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5482))
- Fixed `Form` clearing array and scalar values a sibling edit did not affect: an edit re-judges only the fields whose constraints it changes, so a sibling edit no longer drops values the schema offered when they were entered (matching v6), fixing [#5451](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5451), while a conditional that flipped still filters, keeping the [#5250](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5250) fix ([#5460](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5460))
- Added the `itemLabel` array option: set it to the property of each item that names it, or to `'.'` for an array of strings or numbers, and the item's move, copy and remove buttons are titled after it, such as `Remove report.pdf`, so screen readers can tell one item's buttons from another's. Items that share a name, as after a copy, are numbered in the order they were added, such as `Remove report.pdf (2)`, so while the items change through the field a number stays with its item when items are moved, copied or edited, and a copy takes the next number. Remounting the field or replacing its data from outside can renumber them. A number never makes a name that another item already has. Items are named only when their options can ever give them a button. Copy can't show without `copyable`, with `addable: false`, when `maxItems` leaves no room, or in a tuple array without `additionalItems`, and a tuple's fixed items, which can only show Copy, are named only when it can. A title that translates to an empty string is left off, so the button keeps its default title, and an `itemLabel` that isn't a non-empty string is ignored. `ArrayField` reads every item's name once per render of the array, and each item translates the titles of only the buttons it can show, each memoized on the name and whether its button shows, and passes them to `ArrayFieldItemButtonsTemplate` as `itemButtonProps`, which the template spreads onto the buttons. Without the option nothing changes: the buttons keep their default titles and custom `ButtonTemplates` receive no `title` ([#5474](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5474), [#4424](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4424))
- `DateTimeWidget` stores an edit of a value whose local year has five or six digits, such as the epoch `1e15`, where it stored `null` and so wiped the value: a `datetime-local` input writes such a year as plain digits, which `localToUTC()` now expands into the signed six-digit form `Date` reads. Years 1-9999 are the supported range, so a year past 9999, up to the last instant `Date` holds, 275760-09-13T00:00Z, stays on screen and fails the `date-time` format validation instead of the field being wiped and reported as not a string (see `localToUTC()` under `@rjsf/utils`), following up [#5473](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5473)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option of a field that is not an object now renders with every keyword of the field that describes its value, rather than with only its `required` list and `type`. An array whose options only limit its length renders its `items` rather than an "Unsupported field schema" error and keeps them when another option is selected, as a tuple does, along with the errors its item fields raised where both options render the items alike, each with a field per item, and the switch leaves the array's length as it was, one with `uniqueItems` and enumerated items renders a multi-select, and a `format: 'date'` string renders a date input. The field's `title`, `description`, `deprecated` and `default` stay with the field, except that a field whose own template renders no label, such as an array or a checkbox, passes its `title` to the option's field, over the option's own, whether its schema or its `ui:title` declares it, and its `description` to an option's field that declares none of its own, unless `ui:label` turns the label off, and its own `FieldTemplate` is then handed no `description`, so a template that renders the description of a field it leaves unlabelled, as `@rjsf/antd`'s does, doesn't render it a second time. A bound both the field and the option declare, such as `maxItems` or `minimum`, keeps the stricter of the two, since a value has to meet both, so an option's `maxItems: 5` no longer keeps Add enabled past the field's `maxItems: 3`. An option's `true` subschema, such as `items: true`, leaves the field's in place, a tuple included, as does a field's `false` subschema, and a tuple declaring no `additionalItems` stays at its length beside the other's `items` schema. An `enum` is the option's list as it stands, which its `ui:enumNames` are read by position against, two `required` lists ask for every name either asks for, a `not` or a conditional the option declares replaces the field's, a subschema either declares through a `$ref` is met with the other's by the field's `allOf` merge once the `$ref` is resolved, and any other keyword both declare is the option's. The form's defaults are computed from the option as it renders, too, a tuple's and an option written as a `$ref` included, which is followed only to the schema it names, leaving an `allOf` within it to the `allOf` defaults behavior, and which takes the field's `default` as an option written in place does once it inherits something from the field. A field that is not an object and declares both `anyOf` and `oneOf` takes the defaults of the selected option of the keyword it renders alone. See the v7.x upgrade guide ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359))
- `getCustomRenderComponent()` takes `string | JSXElementConstructor<never>`, a lookup name or any component whatever its props, instead of `string | ComponentType<any>`, and `findChildrenAndProps()` declares its return type. `getCustomRenderComponent()` and the `UIComponent` of `computeUIComponentPropsFromGridSchema()` return a `ComponentType<GenericObjectType>` instead of a `ComponentType<any>`, so assigning one to a `ComponentType` whose props have a required member no longer compiles; annotate the variable as `ComponentType<GenericObjectType>`, or leave it to inference. These are exports of `LayoutGridField`'s module, not of the package's entry point ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- `Form` memoizes the submit button element it renders, and `Label` and `SchemaExamples` are memoized, so a keystroke no longer renders the `SubmitButton` template, in any theme, core's field label or a field's `<datalist>` of suggestions again. A custom `SubmitButton` that reads something other than its props, state or context while rendering no longer gets that incidental re-render. The suggestions are reused only while a field's schema object is, which a schema merged from `allOf`, `if`/`then` or `dependencies` is not yet ([#5508](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5508), part of [#5334](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5334))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `SchemaExamples` is a `memo` component, so render it as an element: calling it as a function, as in `{SchemaExamples({ id, schema })}`, still type-checks and throws `SchemaExamples is not a function`. See the upgrade guide ([#5508](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5508))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The optional data controls are never rendered for the root field, and a nested field is no longer mistaken for it. The option a root `anyOf`/`oneOf` selects is no longer hidden behind an Add button, and a nested object or array that a recursive `$ref: '#'` describes now gets the controls
- Renaming a newly added property the user has not edited onto a name the schema declares under `properties` gives it that property's `ui:initialValue`, including when the two names are described by equal schemas
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `extraErrors`, a field's raised errors or a `customValidate` that hold an array for the items of an array field, such as `{ list: [{ name: { __errors: ['taken'] } }] }`: those errors showed on their fields but were left out of the error list, `onError` and `focusOnFirstError`, did not block the submit, replaced the validator's errors for the array, and a raised one was never dropped once its field changed. They are now handled as the object keyed by index that the validators build. The `errorSchema` passed to `onChange`, `onSubmit` and the templates keys an array field's items by index even where an array was supplied, so code that reads such a node as an array (`errorSchema.list.map(...)`, `.length`) has to read it by index instead ([#3827](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3827))
- Fixed a field's raised errors that hold a property named `__proto__`, such as a `JSON.parse()`d server response, showing that property's errors on its parent field and leaving them out of the error list: the form's copy of the raise, and of the raised errors a rename of a sibling property moves, now keeps `__proto__` as a property rather than as the copy's prototype
- Added the `presentation` prop to `Form` and `ThemeProps`, a `ThemePresentation` declaring which parts of a field (its label, description, errors and help) the registered fields and widgets draw themselves. `withTheme()` layers the props' declarations over the theme's, and `buildRegistry()` layers both over `@rjsf/core`'s own into `registry.presentation` with `mergeThemePresentation()`; `getTestRegistry()` takes one as a new last argument ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SchemaField` builds each field's plan with `getPresentationDeclaration()` and `resolveFieldPresentation()`, from the field it renders and the key that named it, the widget that field renders, `getFieldLabel()` and the field's text and errors, memoised so a render that changes nothing it reads hands on the same plan. It passes the plan as `presentation` to the `FieldTemplate` and the field, which passes it to its widget, and hands it to the field below rendering the same id, the selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option or the fallback UI's value, which builds its own plan under it. `ui:label` reaches that field as `hideError` does. The template's label comes from `getFieldLabel()` too, so a deprecated root field with no title is no longer labelled with the Deprecated marker alone. `StringField`, `BooleanField` and `ArrayField` pick their widget through the same helper `SchemaField` plans them with ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** a field's label, description, help and errors are drawn where its plan says, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#a-fields-label-description-help-and-errors-are-drawn-where-its-plan-says-breaking-change) describes. `SchemaField` derives `displayLabel` (true only where the template draws the label), the `description`, `help` and `errors` it hands the template (only where the template draws them) and the widget's `hideLabel` from the plan. An `anyOf`/`oneOf` field is labelled, described and given its errors by its own template whichever option is selected, so the option's control no longer draws a second label ([#5358](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5358)) and its errors no longer vanish with a `null` option; an option's own title and description render beneath the field's only where they differ, the description under `optionDescriptionId()`, and an option given no `uiSchema` of its own no longer takes the field's `ui:title` and `ui:description`. `MultiSchemaField` no longer rewrites the option's schema and uiSchema with the field's title and description, and `optionRendersFieldLabel()` is gone. A custom field named by `ui:options.field` or a `$id` draws its own label, as one named by `ui:field` did; `NullField` draws its own title and description; the fallback UI labels its value from outside only where the value is a single control; a field rendered beside its options draws its own parts under its `XxxOf` ids. `FieldTemplate`'s label carries `fieldLabelId()`, its `ui:widget: 'checkbox'` special case applies only to a template rendered without a plan, which `getDisplayLabel()` still tells to draw that label, and it draws the description where its plan, read through `templateDrawsDescription()`, gives it the template, or, rendered without a plan, where `displayLabel` lets it draw the label, as before; the widgets take `aria-describedby` (only ids that render), `aria-invalid` and a group's `aria-labelledby` from `getControlAttributes()`; `CheckboxWidget`, `ObjectField`, `ArrayFieldTemplate` and `ArrayFieldDescriptionTemplate` draw the label, heading and description the plan gives them, an `additionalProperties` object's heading keeping its `ui:title` or schema `title`, since its key input already shows its key, and a checkbox's description, like every field's, falling back to its schema `description` for an empty `ui:description`, which used to hide it; `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` labels the key through `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()`, so a checkbox entry's key is labelled as its value is, and names the key input by the key's label through `aria-label` where `ui:label: false` leaves no label drawn. The option selector draws the field's label, and its description, where the plan has the `anyOf`/`oneOf` field draw them itself; `ArrayField` hands its template the heading its plan gives it; a layout grid's discriminated `oneOf`/`anyOf` cell's template is handed no description under `ui:label: false`. Core declares `AnyOfField`, `OneOfField` and `FallbackField` as labelled by the template and `NullField` as drawing its own title and description ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The `anyOf`/`oneOf` option selector is handed its own plan from `getSelectorPresentation()` as `presentation`: where the field's template draws its label, the selector is named by it through `aria-labelledby`, it is described by the field's description, help and errors that render rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()` of its own id, none of which exist, and its `hideLabel` follows the plan, so a `ui:label: false` the field takes from a field above hides it too ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SchemaField` hands `FieldHelpTemplate` the field's plan, and `FieldHelpTemplate` renders under its help id, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's own `ui:help` takes `optionHelpId()` rather than repeating the field's `helpId()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- An object with no properties beside its `anyOf`/`oneOf` options hands the empty field it renders beside them a plan whose parts are all drawn above it, so where the field that renders the options draws its own label, as in a theme whose option selector names the field, the object no longer repeats it as a heading ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The fallback UI's value field keeps its `ui:label` under its field's plan, which already has the value's label drawn above it where a template's label names the value and has the value's widget draw it where the theme declares `FallbackField` as drawing its own label; turning it off, as it still does for a value field rendered without a plan, hid the description of an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option the value field renders ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A layout grid's discriminated `oneOf`/`anyOf` cell is planned as any other field is, and core declares `LayoutMultiSchemaField` as a field rendering one widget, so its selector is labelled through `aria-labelledby` where its widget declares that, and a cell whose union declares `type: 'object'` now draws its label, which `ui:label: false` hides, where it drew none and left the selector unnamed ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- A selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option titled as its field takes the field's label, Deprecated marker included, so a deprecated array's option repeating the field's title no longer draws a second, unmarked heading ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- A field component rendered beside its `anyOf`/`oneOf` options rather than in their place, such as a custom `ui:field` or an object with properties, keeps the parts it draws itself: `SchemaField` hands the field rendering the options a plan in which they are drawn by nothing, so where the theme has the option selector name the field, the selector no longer repeats the custom field's label or the object's heading, description and errors ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The option selector of an object with properties beside its `anyOf`/`oneOf` options is named by the object's heading through `aria-labelledby`, and described by what describes the object, where it used to have no name ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- A required checkbox, or any widget its theme declares `uncheckedIsFalse`, carries the native `required` attribute only where its schema demands `true`, as its `*` already did, so a required `boolean` left unchecked submits as `false` rather than the browser stopping the submit with its own prompt before RJSF validates it; the field's plan follows the same rule for the attribute, `control.attributes.required`, which `BooleanField` hands its widget as `required`, so a custom checkbox widget gets it too, and for the marker, `label.requiredMarker` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The option selector beside a custom `ui:field`, and the option below it, are no longer described by the ids of the parts the custom field draws itself, which it may not render; only core's `ObjectField` and `ArrayField` are trusted to render theirs ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` hands `SelectedOptionDescription` its `hideLabel` and plan, so the selected option's description renders under its own id, which the select is described by, and doesn't collide with the field's description ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ArrayField` hands its template, as `title`, exactly the heading the array draws: its plan's, with an `additionalProperties` entry's `ui:title` or schema `title` where it has one, as an object entry's, since its key input already shows its key, or, without a plan, its `ui:title` or own title, and none where `ui:label: false` turns it off. `ArrayFieldTemplate` reads only `title`, and lays out the optional data control with the items where it is empty, so an optional `anyOf`/`oneOf` array option whose heading a field above draws keeps its Remove button. `ArrayFieldTitleTemplate` draws the heading wherever it is handed a `title`, rather than reading `ui:label` again, which could disagree with where that control was laid out and drop it, so a custom `ArrayFieldTemplate` that hands it a heading of its own, rather than `title`, draws that heading under `ui:label: false` unless it leaves it out there itself ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out the optional data control with the properties where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading, so an optional object's Add or Remove button no longer disappears ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The fields hand a widget its deprecated `hideLabel` only through `getWidgetLabelProps()`, and a template its `displayLabel` and `rawErrors` only through `getDeprecatedTemplateProps()`, and ask `getDisplayLabel()` only through `displaysLabel()`; `FieldTemplate`, `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate`, `CheckboxWidget` and `SelectWidget` read the plan through `templateDrawsLabel()`, `hasTemplateErrors()` and `widgetDrawsLabel()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- An `anyOf`/`oneOf` option with no title of its own takes its field's label rather than the property name. While an optional data control keeps the option selector off screen, which leaves the field's template label no control to name, it is the option's heading, with the field's description drawn beneath it rather than above it, even where the field declares it draws its own description, so an optional `tags: { title: 'Tags', anyOf: [...] }` with no data is headed "Tags", not "tags". In a theme whose selector draws the field's label itself, it labels the option's control, so a `ui:title` names both the selector and the input, not the input by the property name. An option with a title of its own keeps it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RadioWidget` marks its `radiogroup` `aria-invalid` rather than each radio, where ARIA supports it, and `CheckboxesWidget` keeps it on each checkbox; both take their group's `role` from `getGroupAttributes()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** An `anyOf`/`oneOf` field each of whose options is an object, an array or a `null`, with an option naming no type taking the field's, has its plan name any control at the field's id through `aria-labelledby` rather than through the template label's `for`, since a container or a `null` renders no control at the field's id for that `for` to name. An array option counts so whatever widget it renders, so a multi-select or file array option, whose control does carry the field's id, is named by that label through `aria-labelledby`, as the option selector is whatever its field's options; a theme's `FieldTemplate` reading `templateLabelFor()` draws that label with no `for`, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_pet"]` for such a field should aim at `#root_pet__label` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- A custom field given as `ui:field`, `ui:options.field` or a `$id` that renders `SchemaField` under its own id, as a wrapper adding markup around the field does, leaves the label and description it was planned to draw itself to the field it renders, which draws them, so the description is no longer drawn nowhere while the control's `aria-describedby` names it; its template's help and errors are still drawn once ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldTemplate` marks its label with `*` from the plan's `label.requiredMarker`, falling back to `required` without a plan, so a label it draws for a widget its theme declares `uncheckedIsFalse` shows `*` only where the schema demands `true` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `TitleField` puts the `titleId()` it is handed on a `<span>` around the title's text rather than on its `<legend>`, so an object's option selector, named by that id, is named by the title alone, without the `*` or the optional data control's button. A selector or style that targets `legend#…__title` now targets `legend #…__title`. `FieldTemplate`'s label puts its `fieldLabelId()` on a `<span>` around its text in the same way, so a group or an option selector named through it, such as a required radio group or `anyOf` selector, is named by the label's text without the `*`, while a control its `for` names keeps the whole label. A selector or style that targets `label#…__label` now targets `label #…__label`, and the `#…__label` the other entries point at matches the label's text ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `MultiSchemaFieldTemplate` lays out no row for a selector it isn't given, as while an optional `anyOf`/`oneOf` field has no data, rather than an empty `.form-group` ([#5401](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5401), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate` draws its label with no `for` where its field's plan names the control through `aria-labelledby`, as for `RadioWidget`'s and `CheckboxesWidget`'s group, whose wrapper carries the field's id but can't be labelled by a `for`; the label still names the group through its own id. It reads its `for` through `templateLabelFor()`. A selector aimed at `label[for=…]` for such a field should aim at its `fieldLabelId()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `focusOnFirstError` focuses a focusable element carrying the field's id ahead of a form control named after the field, so it reaches a slider's thumb, which isn't a form control, rather than the hidden input such a slider keeps its value in, where its thumb carries the field's id. A group's wrapper carrying the id isn't focusable, so its first control is focused as before, and a wrapper that isn't focusable and names no form control, such as `@rjsf/antd`'s slider root around its handle, leads to the focusable element inside it. An object's or an array's own error, such as `minItems`, focuses nothing inside a `<fieldset>` carrying its id, as `@rjsf/core`'s and `@rjsf/antd`'s container templates render, since it is about none of the controls in it. Under a container template that puts the id on another element, or on none, the first control inside the container is still focused, and a widget that wraps its controls in a `<fieldset>` carrying its id has one focused only where it is a form control named after the field. A form control named after the field is focused only where it takes focus, so a group whose checkboxes aren't form controls, such as `@rjsf/shadcn`'s, which keep their values in hidden inputs named after the field, has its first checkbox focused rather than a hidden input. An element whose id starts with the field's, as a radio's `root_color-0` does, is looked for only where no element carries the field's id, so a disabled or hidden control leaves focus where it is rather than sending it to a sibling such as `root_nameSuffix` for `root_name`. Ids are compared as strings, which needs no `CSS.escape()`, so a DOM without the `CSS` namespace, such as an older jsdom, no longer throws on submit ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- A widget declared `uncheckedIsFalse` is planned as holding `false` unchecked only where its field renders a `boolean`, which `getDefaultFormState()` seeds a required one with. Rendered as another type, as a `type` list's string field renders a switch, an unchecked box answers nothing, so a required one carries the native `required` attribute and its label's `*` as any other control does ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RangeWidget` renders through the `BaseInputTemplate` a field's `uiSchema` names in `ui:BaseInputTemplate`, as the other widgets built on it do, rather than always the registry's. A read-only range is disabled, as `ColorWidget` is and every theme's own range is, since a browser ignores `readonly` on a range input and lets a user drag it, and a range draws no `allowClearTextInputs` clear button, which emptied a value the slider can't show and left it at its midpoint ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `RatingWidget` renders its stars as a `radiogroup`, named by the field's label through `aria-labelledby`, as `RadioWidget` is, rather than leaving the label's `for` on its hidden `<input>`, which named nothing a user reaches, so the stars had no group name in any theme. The field's id moves from that `<input>` to the `radiogroup`, as `RadioWidget`'s is on its group, so `focusOnFirstError` reaches the first star. Each star is described by its field's parts that render, and the `radiogroup` is `aria-invalid` while the field shows errors and `aria-required` while it is required, since the stars carry no `required` of their own. A widget of your own registered as `RatingWidget` takes core's declaration with the key, so declare it `labelledBy: 'for'` under its key where it renders a single control under its `id`, to keep the label's `for`, or `label: 'self'` where it draws its own label ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `AltDateWidget`, and so `AltDateTimeWidget`, wraps its list of date parts in a `group` `<div>` carrying the field's id, around the `<ul>`, which keeps its list role so the parts stay its items, named by the template's label through `aria-labelledby`, and core declares both `labelledBy: 'aria-labelledby'`, so the label draws no `for`, which named an id no date part carried, and `focusOnFirstError` reaches the first part. Its Now and Clear buttons are disabled with the field, so a disabled or read-only one leaves focus where it is rather than on a Now or Clear button that does nothing. It hands `DateElement` its plan, so each part is described by the field's parts that render, rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()`, and is `aria-invalid` while the field's errors show. A selector aimed at the label's `for` should aim at `#root_when__label`. A widget of your own registered as `AltDateWidget` or `AltDateTimeWidget` takes that declaration with the key, so declare it `labelledBy: 'for'` under its key where it renders a single control under its `id`, to keep the label's `for`, or `label: 'self'` where it draws its own label ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490), [#5542](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5542))
- `RadioWidget` focuses its checked radio under `ui:autofocus`, where Tab into the group lands, or the first a user can choose where none is checked or `ui:enumDisabled` disables it, and `CheckboxesWidget` the first checkbox a user can choose. Both focused the first option, which takes no focus when `ui:enumDisabled` disables it, and which in a radio group may be unchecked, so that Space checks it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RatingWidget` takes `ui:autofocus`, which it ignored, focusing its checked star, as `RadioWidget` focuses its checked radio, else its first star; a disabled or read-only rating takes none ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SchemaField` takes its `rjsf-field-error` class from the field's own errors and `hideError` directly, rather than from the deprecated `hasVisibleErrors()`, to the same effect, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's own `ui:hideError` still decides its class, as it decides the `rawErrors` its template is handed. The fields hand the deprecated `rawErrors` to the field component and the container template they render, unchanged, only through `getFieldErrorProps()` and `getContainerErrorProps()`, as they hand a widget its own through `getWidgetErrorProps()`; `ArrayField` still hands each item's `ArraySchemaField` the array's own errors rather than the item's, which are in its `errorSchema` ([#5325](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5325))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `Form` owns its data, as an uncontrolled `<input>` does. `initialFormData` is the seed, like `defaultValue`: read on mount and on `reset()`, filled in with the schema's defaults, and ignored at every other render. `formData` is kept as another name for the seed and wins when both are passed, so `formData={data} onChange={(e) => setData(e.formData)}` keeps working. A `formData` that changes after mount no longer replaces the data, and the form logs a warning in development the first time it ignores one. To show another record, remount the form under a `key`; to change the data in place, call `setFieldValue('', data)` on its ref. See [the upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#form-owns-its-data-breaking-change) ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** An edit is applied and shown before `onChange` is called, whatever the handler stores, so an `onChange` that does not store an edit no longer refuses it. Refuse or rewrite edits in `transformChange` instead ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- Added the `transformChange(next, previous)` prop, a pure function that decides what an edit leaves the form with, for a field's change and for `setFieldValue()`, before the form stores, validates and reports it: return `next` to take the edit, `previous` to refuse it, or another value. A refused option switch leaves the `oneOf`/`anyOf` selector on the option it showed, even where the data fits the option picked too, a rewrite that changes the field's own data puts it on the option the data it is rewritten to fits, keeping the option picked while that data still fits it, and one that leaves the field as the switch made it keeps the option picked, and a refused array reorder keeps each row paired with its own value. Another value is sanitized as the edit would be, so one that switches an `if`/`then`/`else` or other conditional drops the values of the branch it leaves ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `onChange` is called for edits only. It is no longer called on mount, or after a change of `schema`, of a `ui:emptyValue` or of `defaultFormStateBehavior`, for the defaults the form fills in, and `NullField` no longer calls `onChange(null)` from an Effect as it mounts: the defaults now fill a missing `type: 'null'` value in as `null`, so the submitted data is unchanged wherever that field rendered; one it didn't render, left out of a `LayoutGridField` layout or under a `ui:field` that doesn't render it, is now submitted as `null` as well, as a `default` or a `{ const: null }` already was. An object holding such a field that is set to `undefined` stays `undefined` rather than being re-created with its defaults. Read the data with its defaults through `getFormData()` ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A form hidden by `<Activity>` is treated as unmounted, as React asks of hidden content: until it is shown again it drops a field's change, blur and focus, `setFieldValue()`, `reset()` and `submit()`, and `validateForm()` returns its result without calling `onError`. A form that has unmounted drops the same, as v6 did for a change. In development, the first `setFieldValue()`, `reset()` or `submit()` a hidden or unmounted form drops is warned about ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- `submit()` submits the form's current value and calls `onSubmit` or `onError` before it returns, so `setFieldValue(path, value); submit()` submits the new value. Such a submit is checked by schema validation alone, since the browser's constraint validation reads inputs that have yet to show the edit ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- `reset()` re-reads the seed from the current props (`formData`, else `initialFormData`), clears every error except `extraErrors` and reports the result through `onChange`, as in v6 ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A custom field that wraps `ArrayField` or `ObjectField` has to pass `formData` through unchanged: both read the form's current value at their `fieldPath` to add, remove, rename and reorder, so a sorted or filtered copy makes them act on positions the form does not hold ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FormState`, the class component's state type, is no longer exported; `IChangeEvent` is the public shape of what reaches the callbacks ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- Without live validation, an edit clears the validator's errors of the fields whose values it replaced in every form, where v6 did so only for a form passed `formData`, and the errors an item reorder, a property rename or an option switch moved stay with the values they moved with. In development, the `formData` passed to `onChange` is deep-frozen, and so are `transformChange`'s `next` and `previous`, so a `transformChange` that mutates them throws, and the object passed as `initialFormData` or `formData`, from the form's first edit, validation, submit or reset on, since the form's data shares its unchanged parts with it ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- `Form` hands each commit's props and state to its model from an insertion Effect rendered first inside the form element, which React runs before any layout Effect of the commit and before the layout cleanup of any field the commit removes, so a command issued from a layout Effect, a widget's or an earlier sibling's, or from a removed field's cleanup, starts from the props of that commit; and the form attaches to its model from the first layout Effect in its subtree, so an edit a widget makes from a layout Effect as an `<Activity>` shows the form again is taken ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- `formData` is deprecated in favour of `initialFormData`, which it is another name for: the form reads it only on mount and on `reset()`, which the name `formData` hides by reading as a controlled value. It still works and still wins when both are passed. See [the upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#form-owns-its-data-breaking-change) ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- A `transformChange` that returns a copy of the data before the edit refuses it, as returning that data itself does, so the key moves the edit announced, an array reorder's row keys or a renamed property's place, are undone. An edit that leaves the data as it was, such as an option switch whose data fits both options, is taken by a copy, which is a copy of the edit's data too, and refused only by that data itself ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- A rename or add `transformChange` refuses leaves the React key the last accepted rename gave another property, so that property keeps its instance and focus ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- `submit()` submits a form whose `tagName` renders something other than a `<form>` by dispatching a submit event to it, where it did nothing ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- A field's change runs `transformChange`, `customValidate` and `transformErrors` the way it calls `onChange`, rethrowing a throw from a timer, so one that throws on a change a widget reports from an Effect no longer unmounts the form. Such a change stores nothing, and an array or object move it carried is undone ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- An array add, copy, remove or reorder that `transformChange` rewrites keeps a row's instance only where the form kept the item the change put there, and gives every other item a row of its own, so a sort or filter in `transformChange` never hands one item's row, and the focus or state it holds, to another ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- A field the user clears stays cleared when `transformChange` rewrites the edit, under `mergeDefaultsIntoFormData: 'useDefaultIfFormDataUndefined'` too, as it does for an edit taken as it is, rather than getting its schema `default` back from the rewrite ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- An `anyOf`/`oneOf` switch the form takes keeps the chosen option when the defaults the form derives for it, such as those of a `then` the switched value turns on, add to the data the switch sent. So does a switch `transformChange` rewrites only outside the field, such as by stamping another property, since the field keeps what the switch made it. A switch `transformChange` refuses or rewrites at the field, or an edit made from `onChange` sets back, is still re-matched to the data the form keeps ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- In development, a form passed both `formData` and `initialFormData` warns once that `formData` is the seed and `initialFormData` is ignored, and a `formData` written inline, a new object with the same content on every render, no longer draws the warning about a `formData` ignored after mount ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- In development, `ArrayField` and `ObjectField` warn once when a custom field renders them with other `formData` than the form holds at their `fieldPath`, such as a sorted copy, which has their add, remove, rename and reorder act on items they don't show ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** One click on Expand Cycle reveals one more level of a recursive `$ref`, whatever the recursion goes through: one through `properties` revealed two levels per click. A recursion `retrieveSchema()` did not stop now renders, with an Expand Cycle button where it closes, where the form ran out of memory or overflowed the stack: an `allOf` or a `then` around the recursive `$ref`, or a `then` declaring the recursive property ([#5501](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5501)), an `anyOf` option that refers back to the schema offering it, which renders the selector and nothing beside it, and definitions that all refer to one another. The first row of an array under a root `$ref` stops a recursive property where every other row does. Each array row the data holds renders the rows the data has below it under any root, where a row whose property enters the definition holding the array again was collapsed under a root that is not a `$ref`, and a row added to an array of a recursive definition gets the defaults the first row got rather than ones several levels deeper. A `$ref` spelled `#/definitions/%41` stops where `#/definitions/A` does, one level sooner than before, and so does a recursion through a chain of aliases, such as `c: { $ref: A1 }` with `A1 = { $ref: A2 }` and `A2 = { $ref: node }`, which collapses `root_c` where `root_c_c` was collapsed (see the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#expand-cycle-reveals-one-level-per-click-breaking-change))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The `anyOf`/`oneOf` option a field shows is the form's state, by field path and option list, rather than `AnyOfField`'s: it survives the field unmounting and mounting again, follows the moves the form makes, of array items and of additional properties renamed or removed, and starts over when a condition swaps the option list the field offers, where the index carried over to the new list. Typing inside an option no longer switches it to another the data now matches better: a selection holds for the data the form last rendered under it and the data the form's own changes inside the option leave at the field, so a switch or an edit `transformChange` refuses keeps it too, and other data, such as `setFieldValue()`'s, a reset, even one that brings back the data from before the switch, or an edit to a property the object declares beside its options that the options can disagree about, one an option names under `properties`, such as with a `const` that tells the options apart, or under `required`, or any such property where an option carries a keyword that reads the object whole, such as `minProperties`, `not` or `additionalProperties`, is shown under the option `getClosestMatchingOption()` picks for it, the selection winning a tie. An edit to a property every option is silent about, where each carries only `type`, annotations and `properties` and `required` that leave it out, holds the option shown, as an edit inside the option does: with `properties: { name }` beside `oneOf: [A{a}, B{a with maxLength 2, b, required b}]`, selecting B, typing `z` into `b`, `abc` into `a` and then `n` into `name` keeps B and `{ a: 'abc', b: 'z', name: 'n' }`, where 6.11.0 went to A at `abc` and, under `liveOmit`, dropped `b` there. That pick does not replace the selection, so data a later `setFieldValue()` sets that every option fits shows the selection again where it showed the option an earlier one matched, and a selection made for an array item `setFieldValue()` removes applies to the item it later puts at that index. A field whose data holds a value for the `discriminator.propertyName` its schema declares is shown under the option that value names, as before, so an edit to the discriminator switches. Adding an option to the list keeps the selection. `AnyOfField` and `OneOfField` read and write the selection with the new `useOptionSelection()` export, as a custom `AnyOfField` or `OneOfField` now has to. `CyclicSchemaField` keeps which collapsed `$ref` cycles are expanded in the form too, by path, so an expansion survives a remount and follows array moves, and renders an expanded field from `expandRefCycle()` rather than from a copy carrying `RJSF_REF_CYCLE_KEY: false`. See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#option-selection-and-cycle-expansion-are-form-state)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** An object field whose `anyOf`/`oneOf` options render beside it stubs the extra keys of its data once, for itself and the selected option together: a key the option declares is left to the option, and the object takes the option's `additionalProperties`, `unevaluatedProperties` and `patternProperties` beside its own, so its add button and the seed of a new key read them too. The option's field no longer stubs keys a second time, which rendered a key the option allowed without describing as a second, guessed field beside the object's ([#5462](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5462)), and the object no longer predicts which option is on screen to decide which keys to stub, which rendered a key the selected option declares twice where the prediction differed from the selection ([#5461](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5461)). A new key renamed onto a name the selected option declares is seeded from the option's declaration, where it kept the `New Value` string the add button wrote for a key the object describes, which the option's number field then showed. The option is the one `SchemaField` shows, so where `ui:fieldReplacesAnyOrOneOf` keeps the options from rendering, the key stays an extra key seeded `New Value`, as before. `ObjectField` renders the schema `SchemaField` hands it rather than retrieving it a second time, so a custom field that renders `ObjectField` with a schema it did not receive from `SchemaField` retrieves it first
- Switching from a `null` option, or from any option that left the field holding nothing, to one whose defaults are an object gives the field the defaults of the field with that option alone, so a `default` the field itself declares comes back beside the option's ([#5368](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5368)). Those defaults hold no key set to `undefined`, so a `default` an ancestor declares for the field, such as the root's `{ w: { p: { kind: 'obj', extra: 'from-root' } } }`, fills the keys they leave: the round trip gives `{ kind: 'obj', note: 'from-option', extra: 'from-root' }` at `w.p` where it gave `{ note: 'from-option' }`. An object property of the option gets the empty object it gets in a switch from another object option, as before: `meta: { type: 'object' }` is `{}` after either switch. `Form` no longer reads a change whose object holds only `undefined` values as a switch away from a `null` option
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `Form` sanitizes its data as the difference between the field tree the data before a change builds and the one the data after it builds, field by field, as each is rendered: the option the form selects and the `$ref` cycles the user expanded. A field that left the tree loses its value.
