A field whose constraints changed (its `type`, `const`, `enum` or constant options, `maxItems`, or the `default` its own schema or its parent's `default` object declares) has its value judged again, the way `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` judges one; a field whose constraints did not change keeps its value. So a `dependencies` or `if`/`then`/`else` swapped inside the selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option now replaces the old branch's default too: with option `X` selected and `X`'s `mode` choosing a `cfg` of `{ name: 'a' }` or `{ name: 'b' }` by `dependencies`, switching `mode` to `b` gives `{ thing: { mode: 'b', cfg: { name: 'b' } } }`, where 6.11.0 kept `{ name: 'a' }` ([#5465](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5465)). Every edit below the root is sanitized, an array or object write included: adding a second row to `list` where `if: { properties: { list: { minItems: 2 } } }` narrows `color` to `['r']` replaces `color: 'g'` with `'r'` as the row is added, where 6.11.0 did so at the next edit. A schema change of a form that is neither `disabled` nor `readonly` is sanitized as it arrives, as an edit of such a form is: narrowing an `enum` from `['a', 'b']` to `['a']` turns `color: 'b'` into `'a'` at once, where 6.11.0 kept `'b'` until an edit sanitized, and kept it for good when the schema had no conditional. A value `setFieldValue('', data)` sets is taken as written, and no later edit judges it unless the edit changes that field's constraints: a `color: 'c'` set that the `else` branch does not offer stays `'c'` across an edit of another field. An array `setFieldValue()` writes whole at its own path pairs each item that holds what an item of the array held with that item, so a reorder set back keeps the constraints its rows had. An extra key holding the old branch's `additionalProperties` default object takes the new branch's, as a declared property does, where 6.11.0 threw as it rendered such data. This replaces the settled snapshot the sanitize compared against, so `FormState` loses `formDataSchema`, `settledFormData`, `settledFormDataForPairing`, `settledRetrievedSchema` and `hasNestedConditionalSchema`, and gains `uiState`, the form's option selections and cycle expansions. See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#sanitize-is-a-diff-of-the-field-trees-breaking-change)
- `ui:required` is enforced inside the `anyOf`/`oneOf` option a field shows, rather than the one its data matches best, the live validation of an edit included, and below a collapsed `$ref` cycle once the user expands it; nothing below a collapsed cycle is required. Omitting extra data (`omitExtraData`, `liveOmit`) keeps the data of the option a field shows, and so does `liveOmit: 'onChange'` for an edit that leaves the data matching another option better, which switched the field and dropped the option's data. Both read the form's selections through `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()`'s and `omitExtraData()`'s new `fieldState` argument
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A field is named by one slot, read in order from its own `ui:field` and the schema's `$id`, so a `$id` naming a registered field renders it the way a `ui:field` does: beside the `anyOf`/`oneOf` option selector, and in place of the options under `ui:fieldReplacesAnyOrOneOf`, where a `$id` with a non-select `anyOf`/`oneOf` rendered nothing of the field ([#5391](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5391)). The selected option no longer renders the named field a second time: it renders the default field for its own schema, where the named field rendered twice for one data address ([#5358](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5358), its `ui:field` half). See [the upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#a-field-is-named-once-per-data-path-breaking-change)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A field named by `ui:field` or `$id` is applied once per data path: a `SchemaField` that the named field renders for its own data, and the selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option, render as though no field were named, so a field that wraps `registry.fields.SchemaField` with its own props renders the field once rather than until the heap runs out ([#5377](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5377)), with one label, description, help, extra-key row and option selector: the `SchemaField` the named field renders draws the label, description and help its plan hands down to it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490)), and leaves the extra key's input and remove button and the option selector to the `SchemaField` that named the field. `SchemaField` and `FallbackField` are not custom fields, whichever of the two names them: `ui:field: 'SchemaField'`, which ran out of memory, renders exactly as though no field were named, and `FallbackField` renders as it does for a schema whose type sends it there, labelled as though no field were named. So a custom field that renders `registry.fields.SchemaField` for its own data gets the default field there, whatever the `uiSchema` it passes names, and below a named field the selected option's own `ui:field` (from `uiSchema.anyOf`/`oneOf`) or `$id` is not applied either: with `ui:field: 'A'` on the union and `ui:field: 'B'` on the option, 6.11.0 rendered `A` and `B`, and v7 renders `A` and the option's default field. See [the upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#a-field-is-named-once-per-data-path-breaking-change)
- A field registered under a schema's `$id` that is not a component, such as a React element (`<MyField />` rather than `MyField`), is ignored with a warning naming the `$id`, and the field for the schema's type renders, where React threw `Element type is invalid` and the whole form failed to render ([#5439](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5439))
- An array item's `ArrayFieldItemTemplate` is handed as its `displayLabel` whether a label sits above the item's control, where it was `getDisplayLabel()` of the item's schema and its own `uiSchema`: a label the plan the item's `SchemaField` builds, from the props it is handed, has the template draw, which is the `displayLabel` its own `FieldTemplate` is handed, or one the item's field or widget draws itself where its declaration places it `labelPlacement: 'above'`. So the item's buttons are placed by the label drawn above its control, however the item's field is named. A custom field gives `false` whichever of `ui:field`, `ui:options.field` or `$id` names it, as one its `ui:field` named always did, where the other two gave `true`, and `true` where the `presentation` declares its label `'template'`, as for `@rjsf/core`'s own fields named for the item, where `ui:field` gave `false`, or declares the label it draws `labelPlacement: 'above'`. A `ui:field` naming no registered field, which is ignored with a warning, gives what no `ui:field` gives. An item with no label text, a `null` item, which `NullField` titles, a hidden item, and a boolean under `ui:widget: 'checkbox'` give `false`, and a boolean whose titled `oneOf` constants render a select, and an object `anyOf`/`oneOf`, whose selector its template labels, give `true`, as does an item the fallback UI (`useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType`) renders with a choice of types, whose type selector comes first and is labelled for the type whatever the item's title, unless `ui:label` is `false` or the item is hidden, which hides the selector with it; a `ui:definitions` entry for the item's `$ref` is read. An item whose widget draws its own label gives `true` where the theme declares that label above the control, as `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`, `@rjsf/chakra-ui` and `@rjsf/mantine` do, while the item's `FieldTemplate` is still handed `displayLabel: false`. A custom field wrapping `SchemaField` for its own data is planned as any custom field, so its item gives `false` unless the field declares its label `'template'`, though the field it wraps draws the label it hands down. The field's own components and templates are handed its `uiSchema` without an ignored `ui:field`, as they are without one naming `SchemaField`
- An `anyOf`/`oneOf` option whose own `uiSchema.anyOf`/`uiSchema.oneOf` entry names a field through `ui:field` or `ui:options.field`, registered or not, below a field named for the same data path warns once that the entry's `ui:field` is ignored, since the option renders as though no field were named there. An entry naming `SchemaField`, which renders the option the same either way, and a `null` option, which renders no field, don't warn. A `ui:field` a `ui:definitions` entry gives an option's `$ref` is copied into that option's entry, so it warns too, naming the option's entry rather than the definition ([#5358](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5358))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Under `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType`, a custom field that renders `FallbackField` without handing it its `presentation` has the value field draw the field's label and description however the form reaches the custom field. In 6.x, one reached through `ui:field` or `ui:options.field` ran out of stack, one reached through the schema's `$id` had its value labelled twice, by its title and as `Value`, and one that removed `ui:field` from the `uiSchema` it passes on had its value labelled `Value` and its description left undrawn. A custom field that draws its own label and description now has both drawn a second time, the description under the same `id` as the field's own copy, so such a field should leave its label and description to the value field ([#5389](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5389)). See [the upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#a-custom-field-rendering-fallbackfield-without-a-plan-has-the-value-field-draw-its-label-breaking-change)
- Fixed the errors a field raises under a property named `__proto__` being dropped, and a changed `ui:emptyValue` under a `uiSchema` key named `__proto__` not being applied to the data: the form state writes such keys as own keys, through `setOwn()` and `setByPath()`. Renaming an additional property keeps a sibling named `__proto__`, which it dropped from the data or turned into its prototype, and keeps the value of a property renamed to `__proto__`, which it lost; and a `ui:layoutGrid` leaf named like an `Object.prototype` member that the schema doesn't declare finds no schema rather than the inherited function ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- A `ui:layoutGrid` path that indexes a tuple past its `items` reads the tuple's `additionalItems` schema, as `ArrayField` renders those items, or no schema where `additionalItems` isn't one, or is the empty schema directly or through a `$ref`, so the cell falls back to its `render`. An index one past the last item found no schema even where `additionalItems` gave one, and one further read the last item's ([#5564](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5564))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Only the options RJSF reads from `ui:globalOptions`, the twelve `GlobalUISchemaOptionsKeys`, and the extension keys a form declares in the new `globalUiOptionsKeys` prop, which a theme declares too through the new `ThemeProps` field `withTheme()` adds the `Form`'s to, are read from it, since the registry's `globalUiOptions` are built by `pickGlobalUiOptions()`. Every field hands the declared keys to its widget beneath the field's own options, as it does RJSF's, a template reads them with `getUiOptions(uiSchema, registry.globalUiOptions)`, and a global `field`, `widget`, `help`, `title`, `description`, `hideError`, `readonly`, `disabled`, `placeholder`, `classNames`, template override or any other option a field reads applies to no field, so `SchemaField`, `FallbackField`, `LayoutMultiSchemaField` and the fields no longer handle one. `StringField`, and `NumberField` through it, hand their widget the global options beneath the field's own, as `ArrayField` and `BooleanField` do, so a global `allowClearTextInputs` or `optionValueFormat` reaches every string and number widget, not only one in a `LayoutGridField`. `LayoutGridField` no longer copies the globals into each cell's `ui:options` and `ui:globalOptions`, so a cell reads them from the registry as every other field does: its own options beat them, where the copy let a global `label: false` hide the label of a cell that set `ui:label: true`, and its widget's `options` hold no `globalOptions` ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607), [#5419](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5419), [#5527](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5527)). See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#uiglobaloptions-hold-only-the-options-rjsf-reads-there-breaking-change)
- `AnyOfField` and `OneOfField` render a field's own `ui:MultiSchemaFieldTemplate`, which `custom-templates.md` lists as an override, where only a `ui:globalOptions` one applied, and hand their option selector's widget the field's `uiSchema`, as `LayoutMultiSchemaField` does ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `LayoutGridField` renders a discriminated `oneOf`/`anyOf` cell through `SchemaField`, as it renders every other cell, and `LayoutMultiSchemaField` renders only the option selector widget rather than a `FieldTemplate` whose props it built by hand, so the cell resolves its `ui:required`, `ui:readonly`, `ui:autofocus` and `ui:definitions` entry as any other field does, its template is handed what any other field's is, and a cell with no title is labelled by its name. The selector's widget is typed from the first option that isn't a boolean schema, or as an object when there is none, so a selector with no option selected and a union with no options both render, where they threw, and `SchemaField` disables any field `ui:fieldReplacesAnyOrOneOf` names on a union with no options. `LayoutMultiSchemaField` reads its options from its schema rather than an `options` prop, which the grid no longer passes to any cell, and no longer reads `baseType`; `computeEnumOptions()` moved to `@rjsf/core/lib/components/fields/layoutMultiSchemaFieldSelector.js`, `getSelectedOption()` is removed in favour of that file's `getSelectedOptionIndex()`, `getSchemaDetailsForField()`'s `optionsInfo` carries only `hasDiscriminator`, and a field of your own registered as `LayoutMultiSchemaField` should render only its selector. The console warns once about a `ui:field` the cell or its `ui:definitions` entry names, which the grid replaces and the cell has never rendered ([#5419](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5419)). See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#a-discriminated-cell-of-a-layout-grid-renders-through-schemafield-breaking-change)
- A layout grid that lays out an array's items no longer hands the item's title, such as `Things-1`, to each cell it renders, which labelled every untitled cell of the item by it and headed a `LayoutHeaderField` cell with it over its object's title, so such a cell is labelled by its name, as it is in any other grid. Nor does a grid in an `additionalProperties` entry whose key was renamed hand that on, which labelled every cell by its name rather than its title, and no grid hands on an autofocus, from its own `ui:autofocus` or from an array whose items it lays out, which autofocused every cell and left the focus on whichever rendered last, so it focuses no cell, as an object's focuses none of its properties; a cell is focused by its own `ui:autofocus`. A grid in an `additionalProperties` entry no longer hands each cell's template the entry's `onKeyRename`, `onKeyRenameBlur`, `onRemoveProperty` and `propertyNamesEnum`, which edit the entry's key, not the cell's, and a cell the layout names by an `additionalProperties` key, of the grid's own or of an object within it, draws no key input or remove button, which had no handlers to call, since renaming or removing the key would take the cell out of the layout, and is labelled by its `ui:title` or title, as a declared property's cell is, rather than by the key. Cleared, it is stored as `''`, or as its `ui:emptyValue`, as an object stores a cleared entry, where it was stored as `undefined`, which a round trip through JSON drops along with the key and the cell; a `oneOf`/`anyOf` cell is stored as its option switch sends it, since the switch announces the key moves of its change with that value, which leaves it `undefined` and so drops the key in a round trip through JSON, unless its options are all constants, which render as a select, as an `enum` does, with no switch. A cleared discriminated cell was stored as `''`, which matches none of its options and failed validation, so it is now stored as `undefined` too ([#5419](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5419))

## @rjsf/daisyui

- Fixed the `getTestRegistry()` test helper passing an options object where positional arguments were expected, which silently dropped its templates and widgets; it now imports from `@rjsf/core/testing` ([#5272](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5272))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `TimeWidget` pads missing seconds and appends the browser's local UTC offset to its value on change, matching `@rjsf/core`'s `TimeWidget` (`format: "iso-time"` still pads seconds but skips the offset, since that format's timezone is optional). `DateTimeWidget` similarly formats a `format: "iso-date-time"` value as a naive local date-time string instead of a UTC ISO string, stripping a stored offset before parsing so it displays as the naive wall-clock time it represents; an unparsable stored value resolves to no selected date rather than crashing the calendar on an `Invalid Date` ([#3930](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3930))
- `generateTheme()` no longer returns a `fields` entry holding the unmodified core fields, matching every other theme; the `Form` already merges the core fields underneath a theme's overrides, so this changes no rendering ([#5266](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5266))
- `BaseInputTemplate` renders a `number`/`integer` field as `<input type="text" inputMode="decimal|numeric" pattern="..." title="...">` instead of `<input type="number">`, and no longer puts `step`, `min` or `max` on it, so the rendered markup and any snapshot of a numeric field change; see the `getInputProps()` entry under `@rjsf/utils` for the reasoning and the ways back to a native number input ([#4038](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4038))
- Added `ui:options.optgroups` support to `SelectWidget`'s custom dropdown, rendering a labeled header row before each group's options
- `SelectWidget` now honors `ui:enumDisabled`, rendering those options with `aria-disabled` and ignoring clicks and key presses on them
- `SelectWidget` renders a falsy option label, such as `0` or `false`, as its own text instead of leaving the row blank; only `null` and `undefined` still render as empty
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` renders an additional property's key as a `SelectWidget` of the names the parent schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows, instead of a free-text input, and takes the key from the new `keyName` prop rather than from the decorated `label`, so a `deprecated` additional property no longer offers its decoration as a key name, nor renames itself to it ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- `SelectWidget` gives the element that opens its dropdown the `id` it is passed, so a label pointing at it reaches the control ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- `SelectWidget` opens its dropdown from a real `<button>` rather than a `<div role='button'>`. `<label for>` only associates with a labelable element, so on a `div` neither `FieldTemplate`'s label nor the key label of an additional property reached the control, whatever `id` it carried ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The default `Form` export is a `ThemedForm` (see `@rjsf/core`): generic over the form data, so `<Form formData={data} />` infers `T` and `<Form<MyData> />` names it. The `Theme`, `Templates` and `Widgets` exports keep each component's own generic type instead of being instantiated at the default `T` (`any` in 6.x), so `withTheme<MyData>(Theme)` and `{ ...Templates, FieldTemplate: MyFieldTemplate }` typecheck for any `T`. `generateForm()` returns a `ThemedForm<T, S, F>`; `generateTheme()`, `generateTemplates()` and `generateWidgets()` keep their signatures, apart from the new `unknown`/`FormContextType` defaults, and still build a fresh object on every call. The `Widgets` export is the widget map like every other theme's; it used to be an alias for the `generateWidgets` function
- Fixed `SelectWidget`, `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` with object options and the `realValue` option value format ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309))
- Fixed `FieldTemplate` dropping the field label for every `boolean` not rendered as a checkbox, so a `select` ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666)), a `radio` or a custom widget had no visible title. The label is skipped only for the widgets that render their own — the checkbox and the toggle — rather than for the whole `boolean` type, so a nullable `boolean` (`type: ["boolean", "null"]`) no longer renders its title twice either ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `FieldTemplate` rendering a field's description a second time under the `checkbox` and `toggle` widgets, which render their own ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `FieldTemplate` rendering an empty `<label>` for a field with no title, which every root field and array item got. No title now means no label element, and the lone required `*` that accompanied it on an untitled array item goes with it ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `FieldTemplate` honors the `hidden` prop, rendering only the field's children as every other theme does. An `additionalProperties` entry hidden with `ui:widget: 'hidden'` therefore no longer renders its key input or remove button, and a field hidden by `deprecatedHandling: 'hide'`, which `SchemaField` reports through the same prop, is hidden rather than rendered in full ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Removed the second, `display: none` `<label>` `BaseInputTemplate` rendered for its input. It was out of the accessibility tree as well as off the screen, so it labelled nothing while giving every text input two `<label for>` ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- The widgets that render a _group_ of controls — `RadioWidget`, `CheckboxesWidget`, `RatingWidget` and `AltDateWidget`/`AltDateTimeWidget` — mark that group with `role='radiogroup'`/`role='group'`, carry the field's `id`, and name themselves from the `FieldTemplate` label through `aria-labelledby`, since a `label htmlFor` reaches a single control rather than the element wrapping them. None of them put the field's `id` on any element before, so the label named nothing at all ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `DateWidget` and `DateTimeWidget`'s popup triggers are named by the `FieldTemplate` label, announce the selected date after it, and show `ui:placeholder`, falling back to the field's label, where they showed `schema.title` and so contradicted a `ui:title` ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `AltDateWidget`'s year/month/day labels are associated with the selects they name through the new `dateElementId()`; they had no `htmlFor` and did not wrap their control, so each sub-control was unlabelled ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `ToggleWidget` taking its label text from `ui:options.label`, a flag saying whether a label should show rather than the text of one, so the label beside the switch was always empty while `FieldTemplate` rendered another above it. It renders the field's `label` and its own description as `CheckboxWidget` does, derives the required marker from `schemaRequiresTrueValue()`, and sets the `name` every other daisyui widget sets ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Fixed `DateWidget` mangling a `format: 'date'` value. It parsed the stored string with `new Date()`, which reads a date-only value as UTC midnight, then displayed it in local time, so a timezone behind UTC showed the previous day; and it committed `toISOString()`, a full UTC date-time that `ajv-formats` rejects for the format the field declares. It now reads a `YYYY-MM-DD` value with date-fns' `parseISO` and commits `YYYY-MM-DD`, as `@rjsf/antd` and `@rjsf/mantine` do. A stored value carrying a time still names the day it stands for in any timezone ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Both date widgets commit `ui:emptyValue`, `undefined` by default, where they committed `''` for no date. An empty string is no more a `date` or a `date-time` than a mangled one, so closing the picker without choosing a day left an optional field failing its own format ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `CheckboxWidget` and `ToggleWidget` mounting their input uncontrolled for a value of `undefined` or `null`, which a `type: ["boolean", "null"]` field holds, so the box kept whatever the user clicked even where the form rejected the change ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `CheckboxWidget` and `ToggleWidget`, which had drifted apart, render one shared boolean input, so the checkbox honors `ui:autofocus` as the toggle already did. Both wrap that input in a `<span>` rather than a `<div>`, which a `<label>` does not permit ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `FieldTemplate` decides which widget renders its own label from the registry entry rather than from this theme's component, so a consumer who wraps or replaces `CheckboxWidget` or `toggle` keeps exactly one label instead of getting a second one from the template ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `DateWidget` losing a stored value it misread. A date-time spelled with a lowercase `t`/`z`, or an epoch number, parsed as nothing, and dismissing the picker committed the empty value over it; one written with a space separator or with a zone offset of its own read as the wrong day behind UTC, and dismissing the picker stored that day. A value that is genuinely unreadable is now left alone rather than overwritten, in `DateTimeWidget` as well ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `DateWidget` and `DateTimeWidget` open their picker from a real `<button>` rather than a `<div role='button'>`, as `SelectWidget` now does. `<label for>` only associates with a labelable element, so the field's label named the trigger without reaching it: clicking the visible label neither focused the control nor opened the picker ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682), [#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Exported `getGroupProps()`, and re-exported `@rjsf/utils`' new `fieldLabelId()`, the helpers this theme's own widgets name themselves with, so a custom daisyUI widget or a replacement `FieldTemplate` need not rebuild the id suffixes by hand ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `CheckboxWidget` and `ToggleWidget` putting the `required` attribute on a box whose `false` the schema accepts, which HTML5 constraint validation then refused. A `boolean` a parent merely lists as `required` is filled in as `false` and is valid, yet the form could not be submitted at all: the browser stopped it with its own prompt, and RJSF called neither `onSubmit` nor `onError`, so the attribute now follows `schemaRequiresTrueValue()` as the `*` beside it already did. `@rjsf/mantine` takes the same view ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `DateTimeWidget` throwing a `RangeError` when the popup's time input is emptied or half-typed: the `NaN` hours invalidated the whole date, and the calendar's month caption formatted it before the user could finish. The last time the widget could read is kept instead ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `CheckboxesWidget` putting the `required` attribute on every option box. HTML5 constraint validation weighs each box on its own, so a required array could not be submitted until all of them were checked, and neither `onSubmit` nor `onError` fired. A radio group keeps the attribute, where the browser does read same-named inputs as one ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `DateWidget` and `DateTimeWidget` honor `ui:disabled` and `ui:readonly`, which neither read: the picker opened from a disabled field and committed a new value over it. A read-only trigger carries `aria-disabled` rather than the attribute, so the date it displays stays in the tab order for a keyboard user to read ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `CheckboxWidget`, `ToggleWidget` and both date widgets' triggers set `aria-describedby`, so the description, errors and help rendered around them are announced with the control ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- A second press on a date picker's trigger, or on the label pointing at it, closes the picker and commits the day it holds. The press was treated as one outside the popup, which closed it just before the trigger opened it again, so the label could never close it at all ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `FieldTemplate` leaves the label to `ui:widget: 'toggle'` on a schema that is not a `boolean`, which rendered two. Only an alias such as `checkbox` is resolved through the schema's type; a registry key reaches whatever type it is pointed at ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Both date widgets discard the date Escape closes their picker on, which is the one way out that does not commit. The calendar kept it, so the trigger displayed a day the form did not hold and the next close committed it without the user choosing again ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Both date pickers store what they hold only where the user chose a date, instead of on every close. Opening a picker and dismissing it rewrote the field: a `format: 'date'` field holding a date-time had the day the widget read out of it written back, which for a reader far enough from the zone that value was written in is not the day the value names, so a `'2020-01-03T12:00:00Z'` became `'2020-01-04'` in New Zealand ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `DateWidget` reads a stored date-time as the day its own text names, rather than the day its instant lands on in the reader's zone. A midday-UTC value — the convention a backend uses to keep a day clear of both zone edges — named the next day east of UTC+12 and the previous one west of UTC-12. An instant landing on local midnight still names that local day, which is the shape this widget itself used to write, so a reader exactly twelve hours ahead of UTC is the one place the two cannot be told apart: a day picked there is stored as the midday UTC instant of the day before, so a midday value reads as the local day its instant begins ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Both date pickers commit the shape the field's `format` declares rather than the one their own name suggests. `widgetMap` resolves `ui:widget: 'date'` and `'date-time'` on any `string` schema, so a `format: 'date-time'` field rendered with `ui:widget: 'date'` was committed a `YYYY-MM-DD` value that its own format rejects, and `ui:widget: 'date-time'` on a `format: 'date'` field an instant it rejects. Either picker now commits a date for a `date` field, a UTC ISO string for `date-time`, and a naive local date-time for `iso-date-time` ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Both date pickers' triggers fall back to the translated `AriaDateLabel` string, "Select a date", where the field has no value, no `ui:placeholder` and no title. The button rendered empty in that case, which left it both blank on the screen and nameless to a screen reader ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Both date pickers reopen on the month the stored value names after Escape discards the calendar the user navigated away to. Escape restored the date but not the month, so an empty field reopened on the year the user had just abandoned ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `CheckboxWidget` and `ToggleWidget` render the required marker for a required field whose schema is not a `boolean`, which `ui:widget: 'toggle'` can be pointed at. The template's label carried the marker until the widget took the label over, and the widget drew one only for a `boolean` that has to be `true`. The `required` attribute still follows `schemaRequiresTrueValue()`, since that one blocks the submit ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Both date pickers share the popup state, every way out of it, the calendar and the trigger itself, rather than each keeping a near-verbatim copy. Their document `keydown` and `mousedown` listeners are also bound only while the popup is open, where a closed picker previously held both and rebound them on every render ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `SelectWidget` announces the option the trigger it opens its dropdown from displays after the label naming that trigger, rather than having it replaced by that label. A name from outside an element replaces its contents, so the selected option went unannounced wherever a label pointed at the trigger: the `FieldTemplate` label of any titled select field, and each of the year/month/day labels an `AltDateWidget` renders. The trigger also gains the `aria-describedby` every other theme's select already had, which is what carries the field's description, errors and help ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- A widget that names itself from the `FieldTemplate` label — a group of controls, or a picker's trigger — finds that label wherever it has text. A field whose `title` is explicitly `''` still gets one there, from the property name the field's label falls back to, so those widgets no longer go unnamed, or drop the value they display, beside a label that is on the screen ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Both date pickers bind the listeners that close their popup to the document the form is rendered into rather than the one the module runs in, so Escape and a press outside close a picker inside an iframe, as the playground's own frame is. Escape also returns focus to the trigger when it closes the popup from inside it, instead of dropping focus to the document body ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Both date pickers display the date the form holds whenever their popup is closed, rather than the last one chosen in it. A form that does not take the committed value, or normalizes it back to the one it already held, left the trigger showing a day the form did not hold ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `DateTimeWidget` reads a stored value that is not a string — an epoch number, or a `Date` — as the instant it names, as `DateWidget` does, instead of rendering an empty trigger and writing its own day over that value at the first pick ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `DateWidget` no longer creates and tears down a `#date-picker-portal` element on `document.body` on every mount; its popup renders inline, and nothing was ever portalled into it ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `SelectWidget` hides the dropdown arrow from the accessible name its own contents give it where no label points at the button — a root field, a `oneOf` option selector — which otherwise ended in the glyph's spoken name, as `DatePickerTrigger`'s icon already did not ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- `AltDateWidget` names each of its selects by the date part it holds where the field has no property name, since the label it renders above each one points at it either way: a root field left that label replacing the year, month or day the select displays rather than being announced before it ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Fixed `realValue` options whose `String()` matches, such as `1` and `'1'`, sharing a DOM value ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate` applies `ui:classNames` and `ui:style` to the `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` wrapper it renders every field in, the way `@rjsf/core` does, rather than repeating the classes on that wrapper and on its own inner `field-template` div while putting the style on the inner one alone. A `ui:classNames` border or padding was drawn twice, once nested inside the other, and a `ui:style` meant to go with those classes landed on a different element than they did. `ui:options.daisy`'s own `className`, `style` and `theme`, which belong to that inner div, are unaffected. `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` takes the `style` that follows from this, and no longer interpolates an absent `classNames` into its class attribute, which produced a literal `undefined` class on every field rendered by a custom field that passes none. A custom `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` registered for this theme is now what applies both, so one that renders `<div className={classNames}>` and ignores `style` drops every `ui:style`, where ignoring it was harmless before. See the upgrade guide ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- `TextareaWidget` no longer replaces a `ui:options.rows` of `0` with the default of 5 rows. React leaves a `rows` of `0` off the textarea, so it gets the browser's default height of 2 rows, as with `@rjsf/core`'s `TextareaWidget` ([#5387](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5387))
- `FieldTemplate` leaves the label to the checkbox a `checkbox` widget renders on a `type` list such as `['number', 'boolean']`, rather than rendering a second one, and `CheckboxWidget` and `ToggleWidget` mark such a required checkbox no more than a required `boolean`'s, since it starts at the same `false` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Fixed date pickers reporting blur before the close commit renders ordinary parent updates. Done and outside clicks still save the draft; Escape still discards it. Blur is preserved if saving replaces the widget, reporting the value from before the save. Done and Escape return focus to the trigger before the popup unmounts, so focus stays inside the field while `onBlur` runs, and an `onBlur` or an `autoFocus` in the close commit that moves focus elsewhere keeps it. Opening and closing the popup report `onFocus` and `onBlur` in turn: opening it reports one focus where pressing an unfocused trigger reported two, and a close reports one blur, followed by a focus where the trigger holds it afterwards, as it does after Done, Escape and a press on the trigger. A press elsewhere takes the focus with it, and reports that one blur alone where it reported a second as the trigger lost the focus; one that leaves the focus on the trigger, as a press on a button that prevents its default action does, reports no blur until the trigger loses it. Focus that leaves an open popup from the keyboard still reports no blur until the popup is closed. Parent updates deferred to a later transition or async operation are not awaited, and since Done, outside clicks and a press on the trigger report blur from that commit, and every close the focus that follows it, a `flushSync` called from that `onBlur` or `onFocus` is deferred to the next render, and React logs an error for it. In a form rendered into another document, as the playground's iframe renders it, a press on the trigger or on its label closes the popup, where it closed the popup and opened it again. ([#5437](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5437))
- `FieldTemplate` no longer calls `getWidget()`, or needs a `try`/`catch` around it, to tell whether a field's widget renders its own label. It resolves a component as itself and a registry key through the registry's own keys, and recognizes the `checkbox` alias of a `boolean` by name, reading it from the registry under `DEFAULT_BOOLEAN_WIDGET`. A field that names another boolean alias, such as `radio`, whose key holds the checkbox or the toggle now also gets the template's label and description, so the description renders twice with the same id and the input's `aria-describedby` points at both; naming the key itself, `RadioWidget`, still gets only the widget's ([#5456](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5456))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `AltDateWidget`'s date parts are named by the field's label and the translated part, such as `When, year`, where they were named by the part alone, are described by the field's description, help and errors, and their visible labels go through `translateString` and are shown as translated, no longer capitalized. `SelectWidget` gives the `aria-label` and `aria-describedby` it is passed to its button through `widgetAriaProps()`. `AltDateWidget` hands each part's translated name to `DateElement`, which no longer translates it a second time ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- `FieldErrorTemplate` gives its list of errors the `errorId()` every widget's `aria-describedby` points at, so a field's errors are announced with it, and leaves out an empty message, as `@rjsf/core` does ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- The date pickers' Done button and the `DateTimeWidget`'s time input label go through `translateString`, as `TranslatableString.DoneLabel` and `TranslatableString.TimeLabel`, so a translator reaches them ([#5482](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5482))
- `CheckboxesWidget` and `RadioWidget` now honor `ui:enumDisabled`, rendering the options it lists `disabled`. The label of a disabled option, whether `ui:enumDisabled` lists it or the field is `disabled` or `readonly`, no longer shows a pointer cursor, and its text is dimmed ([#5408](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5408))
- `ArrayFieldItemButtonsTemplate` spreads the new `itemButtonProps` onto its buttons, so an array with the `itemLabel` option names each item in its buttons' titles and aria-labels ([#5474](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5474))
- Fixed the `SelectWidget` trigger falling back to a hardcoded `'Select...'` with no value selected, no `ui:placeholder` and no label, fixing [#5498](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5498). The fallback now reads `translateString(TranslatableString.SelectPlaceholder)`, the way the shadcn widget does, so a custom `translateString` localizes it (the default English text becomes `'Select ...'`)
- Declares its presentation: its toggle draws its own label and description, and its rating and date parts are labelled through `aria-labelledby`. `getGroupProps()` takes the field's `presentation` and follows it, `FieldTemplate` renders the description it is handed under a plan, whatever `displayLabel` says, and without one, through `templateDrawsDescription()`, only where `displayLabel` lets it draw the label, as before the plan, the boolean inputs draw the description `getWidgetDescription()` gives them and are described by the ids their plan renders, `FieldTemplate` guesses whether a widget draws its own label only without one, and `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` labels the key through `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldHelpTemplate` renders under the help id of its field's plan, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's own `ui:help` no longer repeats the field's id ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget`'s button takes the `aria-labelledby` its plan gives it, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option selector is named by its field's label, or an object's heading, where it used to be named by the option it displays ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Declares its toggle `uncheckedIsFalse`, so a required boolean it renders is planned as required only where its schema demands `true` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` hands `SelectedOptionDescription` its `hideLabel` and plan, so the selected option's description renders under the id the select is described by ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `getGroupProps()` takes the group's `role` and, under a plan, `aria-invalid` from `getGroupAttributes()`, so `RadioWidget` and `RatingWidget` mark their `radiogroup` invalid while the field has errors ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The widgets put their field's plan on the element a user focuses, through `getControlAttributes()`: `BaseInputTemplate`, `TextareaWidget`, `RangeWidget`, `TimeWidget` and the checkbox and toggle are `aria-invalid` while their errors show and described only by elements that render, and `RadioWidget`'s, `CheckboxesWidget`'s and `RatingWidget`'s options are described by their field's description, help and errors through `getGroupAttributes()`, each of `CheckboxesWidget`'s checkboxes `aria-invalid` too, since a `group` can't be. `RangeWidget`'s input and `RadioWidget`'s checked radio, where Tab into the group lands, or the first a user can choose where none is checked or `ui:enumDisabled` disables it, also take `ui:autofocus`, which they ignored ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `SelectWidget`'s trigger is a `combobox`, expanded while its dropdown is open, so it is `aria-invalid` while its errors show, which a button can't be, and holds the option it displays as its value rather than being described by it; with nothing selected and no placeholder, the field's label it displays is hidden from that value, which is left empty. Down and Up Arrow open its listbox and move into the selected option, else the first or last, Home and End open it onto its first or last option, the arrows, Home and End move between the options, Enter or Space on an option of a single select, or a click on one, chooses it and closes the listbox, returning focus to the trigger, as Escape does. The trigger is the widget's one tab stop, as a native select is: its options, within which a multiple select's checkboxes are drawn only, are reached with the arrows, which move on from an option `ui:enumDisabled` disables where a click has focused one, and Shift+Tab from an option closes the listbox as it returns focus to the trigger. The listbox's id is generated, where it was the field's id with a `__listbox` suffix that another field's id could share. With no label drawn for it, it is named by the field's label, else by its placeholder, as a native input falls back to its placeholder for its name, or, for a field with neither, such as an array item or an untitled option selector, by the translated select prompt, through `getControlAriaLabel()`, so an empty `aria-label` falls back the same way. A query for the trigger by the `button` role should use `combobox` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `DateWidget`'s and `DateTimeWidget`'s trigger is a `combobox` that controls the `dialog` its calendar opens in, as `SelectWidget`'s trigger is, so it is `aria-invalid` while its errors show and described by its field's parts, and holds the date it displays as its value rather than being described by it; with no date and no placeholder, the field's label it displays is hidden from that value, which is left empty. Down Arrow, or Alt+Down Arrow, opens its picker and moves focus onto its selected day, else today, as it does while the picker is open, and the dialog's id is generated, where it was the field's id with a `__popup` suffix. With no label drawn for it, it is named by the field's label, else by its placeholder, or by the translated date prompt. A query for the trigger by the `button` role should use `combobox` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` describes a `propertyNames` key select by nothing, rather than by its key's `__error`, `__description` and `__help` ids, which no element has ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate` draws its label under its plan's label id with the `for` that `templateLabelFor()` gives, which is none for a radio, checkboxes, rating or alt-date group named by it through `aria-labelledby`, and marks it `*` from the plan's `label.requiredMarker`, so a widget declared `uncheckedIsFalse` is marked only where the schema demands `true`. A selector aimed at `label[for=…]` for a group should aim at `label:has(#root_color__label)` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `TitleField` puts the `titleId()` it is handed on a `<span>` around the title's text rather than on the heading's wrapper, so an object's option selector named by that id isn't named "Pet Remove data for optional field" by the optional data control's button too. It draws the `title` it is handed rather than the `ui:title`, as core's does, so a heading the plan decorates under `deprecatedHandling: 'label'` keeps its `(deprecated)`. A selector aimed at `div#root__title` should aim at `span#root__title` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws only the heading `ArrayField` hands it as `title`, the one the array draws, as core's does, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option repeating its field's `ui:title` no longer draws it a second time, and lays out an optional array's data control with its items where it draws no heading, rather than dropping it with the heading. `ArrayFieldTitleTemplate` draws the heading wherever it is handed a `title`, rather than reading `ui:label` again, which could disagree with where that control was laid out and drop it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out the optional data control with the properties where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading, as core's does ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `MultiSchemaFieldTemplate` lays out no row for a selector it isn't given, as while an optional `anyOf`/`oneOf` field has no data, rather than an empty `<div>` ([#5401](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5401), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldErrorTemplate` puts `errorId()` on the one element holding the field's messages, and renders nothing where it is handed no errors, rather than an empty wrapper around a list carrying the id. Empty messages are left out of the list but still count, as the plan counts them, so the id is there whenever a control is described by it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Fixed a disabled or readonly `SelectWidget` committing an option chosen from the keyboard: its dropdown still opens on focus so the options stay readable, but choosing one changes nothing, and the trigger and each option are `aria-disabled`, the options dimmed as an option `ui:enumDisabled` lists is ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ArrayFieldTemplate` reads its empty-array message through `translateString`, so a custom `translateString` localizes `TranslatableString.EmptyArray` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RatingWidget` takes `htmlName` as its radios' `name`, as `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` do, so a form's `nameGenerator` reaches them ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `TitleField` and `ArrayFieldTitleTemplate` mark a required object's or array's heading `*`, as `@rjsf/core`'s do, outside the text carrying the title id, so an option selector named by that id isn't named "Pet *". `ArrayFieldTitleTemplate` puts the id on a `<span>` around the title's text rather than on its `<h3>`, as `TitleField` does, so a selector aimed at `h3#root_tags__title` should aim at `span#root_tags__title` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The first checkbox of `CheckboxesWidget` a user can choose, `TextareaWidget`, `TimeWidget`, `SelectWidget`'s combobox, except while disabled, as a disabled native select takes none, and so an `AltDateWidget`'s first part, and the `DateWidget` and `DateTimeWidget` triggers take `ui:autofocus`, which they ignored, as `@rjsf/core`'s widgets do, and a multiple `SelectWidget`'s listbox is `aria-multiselectable` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `AltDateWidget` hands `DateElement` its plan, so each date part is described by the field's parts that render, rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()`, whose ids no element may have, and is `aria-invalid` while its errors show ([#5542](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5542))
- `RangeWidget` and `TimeWidget` take `htmlName` as their inputs' `name`, as the other widgets do, so a form's `nameGenerator` reaches them ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Fixed `RangeWidget` showing a stored `0` at its schema's `default`, or at the middle of the range without one, and starting out uncontrolled where the field has neither a value nor a `default`, which React warned about as soon as the slider moved ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate`'s label puts its id, from `getLabelId()`, on a `<span>` around the label's text, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, so a group or an option selector named through it, such as a required radio group, is named by the text without the `*`; a selector aimed at `label#root_color__label` should aim at `label #root_color__label`. `SelectWidget`'s trigger and the `DateWidget` and `DateTimeWidget` triggers are `aria-required` while their field is required, which a button can't say through `required`. `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` names an `additionalProperties` entry's key input by the key's label through `aria-label` where `ui:label: false` leaves no label drawn. `getGroupAndOptionProps()` is exported beside `getGroupProps()`, for a custom group widget that renders its controls itself and so needs each control's `aria-describedby` and `aria-invalid` as well as the wrapper's props ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RatingWidget` gives `ui:autofocus` to its checked star, where Tab into the group lands, as `RadioWidget` gives it to its checked radio, rather than always to the first ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `DateWidget` positions its calendar popup from a layout Effect rather than a passive one, so the popup, which each opening mounts afresh, is in place before the browser first paints it ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- `DaisyUIFrameProvider` stores a chosen `dataTheme` from an Effect, not while rendering, and reads the stored theme as an external store, so it shows a theme another provider, `useTheme()`'s `setTheme()` or another tab stores as soon as it is stored, and reads no storage while a theme is chosen ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `useTheme()` reads the stored theme as an external store itself, inside a `ThemeProvider` or not, and follows one a frame provider, a `setTheme()` or another tab stores, where it kept the one stored when its `ThemeProvider` mounted. Inside a `DaisyUIFrameProvider` it names the theme that frame shows: a chosen `dataTheme` from the frame's first render on, and over a theme stored while it is chosen. Outside a `ThemeProvider` it named `night`, which nothing shows, and its `setTheme()` did nothing; it now names the stored theme, or `cupcake` where none is stored, and its `setTheme()` stores one. `ThemeProvider` holds no theme of its own and renders its children as they are, and `ThemeContext` is gone from `lib/theme/Theme.js` and `lib/theme/index.js`. See the v7 upgrade guide ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- `DateWidget`'s calendar popup follows its trigger when an element around the field scrolls, such as a scrolling panel or dialog the form is in, where it stayed put and followed only a scroll of the page ([#5563](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5563))
- The theme declares `daisy` as its `globalUiOptionsKeys`, typed by the new `DaisyGlobalUISchemaOptions` and the new `UiSchema` type, which takes it inline, so `FieldTemplate` reads a global `daisy` where the field sets none of its own through `ui:daisy` or `ui:options`, outside a `LayoutGridField` too. `DaisyGlobalUISchemaOptions` rejects a user extension named `multiple`, `color`, `size` or `colorGradient`, which `BaseInputTemplate` and `RatingWidget` read from a widget's options ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607))

## @rjsf/fluentui-rc

- `BaseInputTemplate` renders a `number`/`integer` field as `<input type="text" inputMode="decimal|numeric" pattern="..." title="...">` instead of `<input type="number">`, and no longer puts `step`, `min` or `max` on it, so the rendered markup and any snapshot of a numeric field change; see the `getInputProps()` entry under `@rjsf/utils` for the reasoning and the ways back to a native number input ([#4038](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4038))
- Added `<optgroup>`-equivalent support to `SelectWidget` via `ui:options.optgroups`, using `OptionGroup`
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` renders an additional property's key as a `SelectWidget` of the names the parent schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows, instead of a free-text input, and takes the key from the new `keyName` prop rather than from the decorated `label`, so a `deprecated` additional property no longer offers its decoration as a key name, nor renames itself to it ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- `SelectWidget` adds the `className` it is passed to its `Dropdown` rather than dropping it ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `SelectWidget` deriving its `validationState` from `rawErrors` alone, so the dropdown stayed in the error state under `ui:hideError` ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The default `Form` export is a `ThemedForm` (see `@rjsf/core`): generic over the form data, so `<Form formData={data} />` infers `T` and `<Form<MyData> />` names it. The `Theme`, `Templates` and `Widgets` exports keep each component's own generic type instead of being instantiated at the default `T` (`any` in 6.x), so `withTheme<MyData>(Theme)` and `{ ...Templates, FieldTemplate: MyFieldTemplate }` typecheck for any `T`. `generateForm()` returns a `ThemedForm<T, S, F>`; `generateTheme()`, `generateTemplates()` and `generateWidgets()` keep their signatures, apart from the new `unknown`/`FormContextType` defaults, and still build a fresh object on every call
- Fixed `SelectWidget`, `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` with object options and the `realValue` option value format ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309))
- Fixed `realValue` options whose `String()` matches, such as `1` and `'1'`, sharing a DOM value; `CheckboxesWidget` reports the focused option on focus and blur ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` no longer interpolates an absent `classNames` into its class attribute, which produced a literal `undefined` class on every field rendered by a custom field that passes none ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `AltDateWidget`'s date parts having no accessible name: each select is named by the field's label and the translated part, such as `When, year`, even when the label is hidden, and described by the field's description, help and errors. `SelectWidget` gives the `aria-label` and `aria-describedby` it is passed to its dropdown through `widgetAriaProps()`, and no longer renders an empty label for a field with none that is not required, which named the dropdown by nothing ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- Declares its presentation: its widgets draw their own label, its checkbox its description too, and an `anyOf`/`oneOf` field is named by its option selector. `FieldTemplate` renders the description it is handed under a plan, whatever `displayLabel` says, and without one, through `templateDrawsDescription()`, only where `displayLabel` lets it draw the label, as before the plan, `CheckboxWidget` draws the description `getWidgetDescription()` gives it and is described by the ids its plan renders, and `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` labels the key through `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldHelpTemplate` renders under the help id of its field's plan, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's own `ui:help` no longer repeats the field's id ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Declares `FallbackField` as drawing its own label, so the fallback UI's value is labelled by its own widget, which no template label did before ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` hands `SelectedOptionDescription` its `hideLabel` and plan, so the selected option's description renders under the id the select is described by ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget` takes its error state from `hasWidgetErrors()`, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's control is styled invalid while the field above draws its errors ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The widgets put their field's plan on the element a user focuses, through `getControlAttributes()`, so each is `aria-invalid` while its errors show and described only by the elements that render, rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()`: `BaseInputTemplate`, `TextareaWidget`, `CheckboxWidget` and `RangeWidget` on their input, and `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` on each option through `getGroupAttributes()`, with `RadioWidget`'s `radiogroup` rather than each radio `aria-invalid`. `RadioWidget` takes `ui:autofocus`, which it ignored, as core's does, focusing its checked radio, where Tab into the group lands, or the first a user can choose where none is checked or `ui:enumDisabled` disables it, and `CheckboxesWidget` the first checkbox a user can choose, where it took the first, which takes no focus where `ui:enumDisabled` disables it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` name their group by the label they draw, under the label id its plan gives it (`fieldLabelId()`, or `optionLabelId()` beneath a field above that draws its label under that id, as an `anyOf`/`oneOf` selector does), through `aria-labelledby`, and draw that label with no `for`, which named the radio group's wrapper, an element a `for` can't label, or, for `CheckboxesWidget`, an id no element had, so neither group had a name. `CheckboxesWidget`'s group now carries the field's id, as core's does, and a group whose label `ui:label: false` hides is named by that label's text through `aria-label`. A selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` for such a field should aim at `#root_color__label` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplate`'s `Field` no longer takes the field's `required`, which Fluent hands every Fluent control inside it that sets none of its own, so each widget marks itself as its plan says: it made every checkbox of a required `CheckboxesWidget` `required`, so a browser asked for each option rather than one and each option's label showed `*`, and a custom widget's control declared `uncheckedIsFalse` `required` whatever its schema. `RadioWidget` passes `required` to its `RadioGroup` instead, so each radio of a required field is `required`, as core's are, where only the group was `aria-required`, and `RangeWidget` passes it to its slider's input, as core's does. A custom widget whose Fluent control took `required` from the `Field` sets it itself ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `CheckboxWidget` draws no label of its own where its plan gives it none, as under `ui:label: false` or below a label drawn above it, as core's does ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `SelectWidget`'s label and `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate`'s key label point their `for` at the dropdown's button and the key input, where Fluent pointed it at an id of its own that no element had. The key label carries the key input's `fieldLabelId()`, and `SelectWidget`'s label the id its plan gives it, as `RadioWidget`'s and `CheckboxesWidget`'s do, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's select beneath a radio selector, which draws the field's label under `fieldLabelId()`, takes `optionLabelId()` rather than repeating that id, and an option selector drawn as a select takes the field's label id ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RangeWidget`'s slider input takes `htmlName` as its `name`, as the other widgets' inputs do, where its `name` was the field's id, and is focused under `ui:autofocus`, which it ignored ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldTemplate` draws the field's label where its plan gives the template the label, as for a custom widget declared `label: 'template'`, under the label id its plan gives it (`fieldLabelId()`, or `optionLabelId()` beneath a field above that draws its label under that id, as an `anyOf`/`oneOf` selector does) with the `for` that `templateLabelFor()` gives, which is none for a radio or checkboxes group named by it through `aria-labelledby`, and marks it required through `templateMarksRequired()`, so a widget declared `uncheckedIsFalse` is marked only where the schema demands `true`. It drew no label whatever the plan said, so such a field had none. The theme declares `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget`, `AltDateWidget` and `AltDateTimeWidget` `label: 'template'`, since none of them draws a label, so those fields are labelled by the template, a rating's `radiogroup` named by that label, where under `default: { label: 'self' }` they had no label at all. A widget of your own registered under one of those keys takes that declaration, so one that draws its own label, such as a date picker registered as `AltDateWidget`, should be declared `label: 'self'` under its key, as the upgrade guide's "Group labels" entry says ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `TitleField` puts the `titleId()` it is handed on a `<span>` around the title's text rather than on the box around the heading, its optional data control and its separator, and marks a required object's or array's heading `*` outside that `<span>`, as core's does, so an object's option selector named by that id is named "Pet" rather than "Pet Remove data for optional field". A selector or style targeting `#…__title` now targets the `<span>` inside the `<h5>` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws only the heading `ArrayField` hands it as `title`, the one the array draws, as core's does, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option repeating its field's `ui:title` no longer draws it a second time, and lays out an optional array's data control with its items where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out the optional data control with the properties where it draws no heading, as under `ui:label: false`, rather than dropping it with the heading, as core's does ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `MultiSchemaFieldTemplate` lays out no row for a selector it isn't given, as while an optional `anyOf`/`oneOf` field has no data, rather than an empty `<div>` ([#5401](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5401), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldErrorTemplate` renders a field's errors in one list under `errorId()`, where it rendered each message under that same id, so a field with two errors repeated it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `RadioWidget`, `CheckboxesWidget` and `SelectWidget` draw no label for a required field with no label text, which would hold only its `*` and name the control "star", where the old `<Label required>` and the select drew one, and nor do `RangeWidget`, `TextareaWidget` and `BaseInputTemplate`, which drew one when rendered without a plan; the controls stay `required`. A `SelectWidget` no label names is named by the field's label, its placeholder or the translated prompt, through `aria-label`, as a combobox takes no name from the value it displays ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The date parts of `@rjsf/core`'s `AltDateWidget`, which this theme renders through its `SelectWidget`, are drawn in the error state while the field's errors show, since each part's plan marks it `aria-invalid`, where they never were ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490), [#5542](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5542))
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` names an `additionalProperties` entry's key input by the key's label through `aria-label` where `ui:label: false` leaves no label drawn, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, and `SelectWidget` takes its label's id from `getLabelId()` and points its dropdown's `aria-labelledby` at that label, so the option selectors of a nested `anyOf`, which share an id, are each named by their own label, where the inner one's `for` named the outer one. `CheckboxWidget` falls back to the schema `description` for an empty `ui:description`, as every field's description does, where it used to hide it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Declares where its widgets draw their own label: above the control, `labelPlacement: 'above'`, for every widget, the `anyOf`/`oneOf` option selector and the fallback UI's type selector, and beside it, `'beside'`, for the checkbox, so `ArrayFieldItemTemplate`, which offsets an item's buttons by `displayLabel`, keeps them level with the control below a label its widget draws above it

## @rjsf/mantine

- **BREAKING CHANGE** Dropped support for `mantine` version 8; upgraded to `mantine` version 9, which requires React 19.2+. The `react` peer dependency floor is `>=19.2`, matching what `@mantine/core`/`@mantine/hooks` 9.6.1 themselves require (their `useEffectEvent` usage needs it)
- Fixed `ObjectFieldTemplate` and the fluid variant of `GridTemplate` rendering their root `Container` at Mantine's default constrained width (960px, centered), instead of filling the space the form was given. Both now pass Mantine's `fluid` prop
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Fixed `GridTemplate` silently dropping `gutter` from `ui:row`/`ui:col` options: Mantine 9 renamed `Grid`'s `gutter` prop to `gap`, so a `gutter` value spread onto `<Grid>` was leaking through as an unrecognized DOM attribute instead of controlling spacing. `GridTemplate` now maps `gutter` to `gap` (an explicit `gap` still wins if both are provided); existing `ui:row`/`ui:col` options that use `gutter` keep working, but should be renamed to `gap` going forward
- Updated the README's prerequisites, which still listed `@mantine/core|hooks|dates >= 8` and didn't mention the React 19.2+ requirement
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `TimeWidget` pads missing seconds and appends the browser's local UTC offset to its value on change, matching `@rjsf/core`'s `TimeWidget` (`format: "iso-time"` still pads seconds but skips the offset, since that format's timezone is optional). `DateTimeWidget`/`DateTimeInput` convert a `format: "date-time"`/`"datetime"` value to/from a UTC ISO string with a timezone offset instead of the naive `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss` string they produced before (which `@rjsf/validator-ajv8`'s `ajv-formats` 3 upgrade rejects for lacking one); `format: "iso-date-time"` keeps producing a naive string, with a `T` separator (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss`) matching the other themes, and a stored value that happens to carry an offset is stripped before being parsed so it displays as the naive wall-clock time it represents. An unparsable stored value resolves to no selected date rather than becoming a silent `Invalid Date` handed to `DateInput` ([#3930](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3930))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Fixed `BaseInputTemplate` routing `onChange` and `onChangeOverride` through one variable and handing the value to both, so an `onChangeOverride` received a string where its declared type, and `@rjsf/core`, both give the change event. An `onChangeOverride` used with this theme now receives the `ChangeEvent`, as it does in every other theme, including on a numeric field — which renders a `TextInput` (still `type="number"`) when an override is present, since `NumberInput` reports only the parsed value and never an event
- Fixed `FileWidget` dropping the selected file for a non-multiple input: Mantine's `FileInput` hands back a lone `File`, which is not iterable, so `Array.from()` produced `[]`. Its clear button sends `null` for a single input and `[]` for a multiple one, which used to throw a `TypeError` and silently keep the existing files respectively; both now normalize to the empty selection that `useFileWidgetProps` reports as a cleared value. An empty value is passed to `FileInput` as `null` rather than `''`, which it counted as a selection, so `ui:placeholder` now shows and the `clearable` button stays hidden until a file is chosen
- `BaseInputTemplate` and `FileWidget` default their generic parameters to `T = unknown` and `F extends FormContextType = FormContextType`, and constrain `S` to `RJSFSchema`, instead of defaulting to `any`
- Added `<optgroup>`-equivalent support to `SelectWidget` via `ui:options.optgroups`, using its `data` prop's grouped-item shape
- `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` renders an additional property's key as a `SelectWidget` of the names the parent schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows, instead of a free-text input, and takes the key from the new `keyName` prop rather than from the decorated `label`, so a `deprecated` additional property no longer offers its decoration as a key name, nor renames itself to it ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed `BaseInputTemplate` and every widget that renders an `error` (checkbox, checkboxes, color, date/time, file, password, radio, range, select, textarea and the alt-date widget) deriving it from `rawErrors` alone, so a field kept showing its error text and styling under `ui:hideError` ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The default `Form` export is a `ThemedForm` (see `@rjsf/core`): generic over the form data, so `<Form formData={data} />` infers `T` and `<Form<MyData> />` names it. The `Theme`, `Templates` and `Widgets` exports keep each component's own generic type instead of being instantiated at the default `T` (`any` in 6.x), so `withTheme<MyData>(Theme)` and `{ ...Templates, FieldTemplate: MyFieldTemplate }` typecheck for any `T`. `generateForm()` returns a `ThemedForm<T, S, F>`; `generateTheme()`, `generateTemplates()` and `generateWidgets()` keep their signatures, apart from the new `unknown`/`FormContextType` defaults, and still build a fresh object on every call
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed the `lib/templates/ButtonTemplates/index.js` module and its default `buttonTemplates()` export; the button templates are reachable through `Templates.ButtonTemplates` or `generateTemplates().ButtonTemplates`
- Fixed `DateTimeInput`, `TimeWidget`, `SelectWidget`, `TextareaWidget`, `PasswordWidget`, `ColorWidget`, `FileWidget`, `RadioWidget`, `CheckboxesWidget`, `AltDateWidget`, and `AltDateTimeWidget` not displaying a `DescriptionFieldTemplate` when a description is included in `schema` or `options`
- `DescriptionFieldTemplate` now inherits `line-height`, `font-weight`, and `font-size` from parent CSS. In most cases this is Mantine `Input.Description` component
- `DateTimeInput` and `TimeWidget` now filter the `options` prop using `cleanupOptions`. This will prevent `ui:options.style`, `title`, `label`, `help`, and other options from being applied automatically at the widget level
