The structural keys the old index signature hid are now declared: `additionalProperties`, `additionalItems`, `anyOf`, `oneOf` (both positional lists) and the legacy unprefixed `classNames`. A field holding a value with no form fields of its own — a `Date` or a `File`, or any value typed as a primitive — is a leaf, and one typed `unknown` keeps every name open ([#5279](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5279))
- Widened `useFileWidgetProps`' `handleChange` from `FileList` to `FileList | File[]`; themes legitimately hand it arrays, and `processFiles()` already used `Array.from()`
- `useFileWidgetProps`' `handleChange` treats an empty `FileList`/`File[]` as a cleared input, reporting `[]` when `multiple` and `undefined` otherwise. It previously appended nothing, so a multi-file widget silently kept its existing files
- `mergeObjects()` and `mergeSchemas()` annotate the values they pull off their operands as `unknown` rather than letting them ride in as `any` from `GenericObjectType`, so every use is guarded by `isObject()` or `Array.isArray()` rather than assumed
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `toFieldPathId()` and the `FieldPathId` type in favor of the branded `FieldPath` string type and `ROOT_FIELD_PATH`, built with `toFieldPath()` and read back with `fieldPathToList()`, `fieldPathToId()`, `fieldPathToName()` and `fieldPathEndsWithIndex()`. `titleId()`, `descriptionId()`, `helpId()`, `errorId()`, `examplesId()`, `buttonId()`, `optionalControlsId()` and `ariaDescribedByIds()` accept only a `string` id. Added `fieldPathFromList()`, which folds a `FieldPathList` into the `FieldPath` addressing the same field, and `expandButtonId()`, which every theme's `CyclicSchemaExpandTemplate` now uses in place of its own copy of the id. Removed the `useDeepCompareMemo()` hook, which existed to stabilize the `fieldPathId` object and has nothing left to stabilize now that field identity is two strings ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Extended `ui:emptyValue` to apply whenever a field is blank — on initial render and after a form reset, not just when a user clears a widget — by threading `uiSchema` (and, for a field reached through a `ui:definitions` fragment, the resolved uiSchema for that fragment) through the `getDefaultFormState`/`computeDefaults`/`getObjectDefaults`/`getArrayDefaults` pipeline (`getDefaultFormState()` and `SchemaUtilsType.getDefaultFormState()` gained a trailing `uiSchema` parameter), including for array items via the static (non-function) form of `uiSchema.items` — a plain object applying uniformly, or an array giving each position in a fixed/tuple `items` schema its own uiSchema — and for a dynamically-added property via `uiSchema.additionalProperties`. Added `ui:initialValue`, which pre-fills a field on render/reset (but not mid-edit, since a form reset is treated the same as the initial defaults pass) and takes priority over `schema.default`, and `ui:required`, which overrides a field's `required` status from the UI side only (`true` adds the required indicator and is enforced during validation via the new `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` export; `false` hides the indicator without suppressing schema-level validation, warning via `console.warn` when used on a schema-required field with neither `ui:initialValue` nor `ui:emptyValue` set) ([#4983](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4983))
- Added `StrictUiSchema<Checks, T, S, F>`, an opt-in form of `UiSchema`: its `Checks` union narrows `ui:widget`/`ui:field`/`ui:options` to only the values valid for each field's form-data type (where `UiSchema` accepts any value), recursing into nested objects/arrays. `@rjsf/utils` has no widgets of its own to always include, so `Checks` is never auto-populated; a theme package (e.g. `@rjsf/core`'s new `CoreUiOptionsChecks`) exports its own vocabulary next to its widgets, built from the new `UiOptionsCheck` shape, for consumers to pass (and extend further with their own entries unioned in). `UiSchema<T, S, F>` itself is otherwise unchanged ([#4916](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4916))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `shouldRender()`, `shallowEquals()`, the `ComponentUpdateStrategy` type and the `experimental_componentUpdateStrategy` entry of `GlobalFormOptions`; extend `PureComponent`, or use `shallowEqual` from `fast-equals`, instead ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- Added `replaceEqualDeep(prev, next)`, structural sharing that returns `next` with every subtree deep-equal to `prev` replaced by the `prev` instance; React elements count as equal by type, key and props, so inline JSX in a `uiSchema` does not read as a change. It reads the previous value's members as own properties only, so a JSON-sourced `__proto__` or `constructor` key can't hand `Object.prototype` back as shared form data. `SchemaUtils.retrieveSchema()` caches its last call per input schema and shares the unchanged subtrees of a recomputation, so treat retrieved schemas as immutable ([#5217](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5217))
- Fixed `getSchemaType()` returning `undefined` for a schema without a `type` whose `const` is falsy (`0`, `false`, `''` or `null`), which rendered such a constant as an unsupported field ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
  - **Potentially breaking change:** a typeless `{ const: null }` now resolves to `null` and renders through `NullField` exactly like an explicit `type: 'null'`. At the default `constAsDefaults` setting the emitted form data is unchanged, since such a property already contributed its constant as its own default. With `defaultFormStateBehavior.constAsDefaults` set to `'never'`, which suppresses that default, a typeless `{ const: null }` and a _required_ typeless `{ const: false }` now write `null` and `false` into the form data on mount where nothing was written before, each matching what the explicit `type` spelling already did. Required `{ const: 0 }` and `{ const: '' }` are unaffected
- Fixed `enumOptionSelectedValue()` in the `realValue` format converting the selected value with `String()` while `enumOptionValueEncoder()` encodes the options, so a selected `null` (`'null'` vs `''`) or object value never matched its option; the selected value is now encoded the same way. A `null` encodes to the empty string that a select's placeholder also carries, so a `null` option is still not selectable in the `realValue` format specifically — the default `indexed` format handles it ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
- Fixed `enumOptionSelectedValue()` reporting no selection whenever the selected value equalled the `emptyValue` a widget passes it, so an option carrying that widget's `null` or `''` sentinel never showed as selected; the value is now only treated as empty when no option carries it ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `getInputProps()` now defaults a `number`/`integer` field to `<input type="text" inputMode="decimal|numeric" pattern="...">` instead of `<input type="number">`, for every theme whose `BaseInputTemplate` renders a plain native `<input>`; an explicit `ui:options.inputType`, or the `updown` widget, still gives a native `number` input (with `step="any"` for a `number` with no `multipleOf`). Avoids the cross-browser keystroke-filtering, scroll-wheel, and locale decimal-separator quirks of a native number input, while the `pattern` still blocks a non-numeric submission the same way `required` already does. The `pattern` accepts the exponent notation `String()` renders very small and very large numbers with, either sign of it for a `number` (`1e-7`, `1e+21`) and only a positive one for an `integer`, which is never rendered any other way; each pattern accepts every spelling of its own type that `NumberField` shows while typing, so an `integer` takes a trailing separator and zeros (`5.`, `5.00`) and the fractional mantissa of an exponent form (`String(2 ** 70)`) while still refusing an actual fraction such as `1.5`, and both take a lone separator, which the field reports as 0. `step`, `min` and `max` are no longer returned for these fields, since they only constrain a native number input. The field's type is resolved with `getSchemaType()`, so a nullable `['number', 'null']` or `['integer', 'null']` schema — which `SchemaField` already renders through `NumberField` — gets the same treatment instead of falling through to a bare text input carrying `step`/`min`/`max`. A union of more than one non-`null` type keeps that plain text input, since its other members accept values a numeric `pattern` would reject. Added `getNumericInputTitle(inputProps, translateString)`, and the `TranslatableString.NumberInputTitle` ("Enter a number") and `TranslatableString.IntegerInputTitle` ("Enter a whole number") entries behind it: every theme whose `BaseInputTemplate` renders a plain native `<input>` now sets that `title`, so the browser appends it to the bare "Please match the requested format" it shows when the `pattern` rejects a value. The fourth parameter, formerly `autoDefaultStepAny`, is now `plainNativeInput` ([#4038](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4038))
- Fixed `mergeObjects()` reading an inherited member of the first object for a JSON-sourced `__proto__` or `constructor` key, and assigning a merged `__proto__` key through the setter, which replaced the result's prototype with the merged value
- Added `groupEnumOptions()`, `isEnumOptionsGroup()` and `flattenGroupedOptions()`, plus the `optgroups` `ui:options` key, giving every theme's `SelectWidget` a single shared implementation of `<optgroup>`-style option grouping
- Added the `AdditionalPropertyKeySelect` component, which renders an additional property's key as the theme's own `SelectWidget` over the names a schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows, and the `propertyNamesEnum` prop on `FieldTemplateProps`/`WrapIfAdditionalTemplateProps` that carries those names ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- `canExpand()` returns false once every name a schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows is held by a property, the way it already does at the `maxProperties` limit ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- Added `getFreePropertyNames()`, returning the names a schema's `propertyNames.enum` allows that neither a property of the schema nor a key of the form data has taken; an `enum` that allows no name at all — one that is empty, or that lists only non-strings — leaves nothing free, rather than reading as no constraint. `canExpand()`, `ObjectField`'s add handler and the key dropdowns all read their free names from it, so the add button and the add handler cannot disagree about which names are left. Also exported `getMatchingPatternProperties()`, which until now was internal ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Added the required `keyName` prop to `FieldTemplateProps`/`WrapIfAdditionalTemplateProps`, carrying the property's own name with none of the decoration `label` may have picked up — a `ui:title`, or the marker a `deprecated` schema adds. An `additionalProperties` key is read and renamed from it, so a `deprecated` additional property no longer shows its decorated label as an out-of-enum key, nor renames itself to it when its key input blurs ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- Added `getVisibleErrors()`, returning the errors a widget or template should surface in its own UI — empty whenever `ui:hideError` is in effect — so the themes derive their error state from one place rather than reading `rawErrors` directly ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- Added `hasVisibleErrors()`, the boolean form of `getVisibleErrors()`, for the widgets and templates that only need to know whether to render an error state ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- Added `errorSchema` to `FieldTemplateProps`, which `ArrayFieldTemplateProps` and `ObjectFieldTemplateProps` already declared, so a `FieldTemplate` rendering the errors itself can reach the ones `ui:hideError` keeps out of `rawErrors` ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- Added `getUnionTypes()`, which reports the types a schema allows when it allows more than one, `getKnownTypes()`, which reports the JSON Schema types a schema lists however many of them there are and which `getUnionTypes()` is built on, and the `JSON_SCHEMA_TYPES` constant listing every JSON Schema type name ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- `retrieveSchema()` marks the `additionalProperties` stub it builds for a property the schema puts no constraint on with the new `GUESSED_TYPE_FLAG` symbol, since that stub's `type` describes the data the property holds rather than anything the schema requires. Only a genuinely unconstrained schema (`true`, or one with no keyword asserting something about the value) is marked — one that constrains the value without naming a type, through an `enum`, `const`, `format`, length, range or a subschema keyword such as `allOf`, is not, so the fallback UI never offers types it rejects, while an annotation, identifier, `$defs` or unrecognized keyword never locks the type in place. The stub also keeps what that `additionalProperties` says about the value instead of discarding it, so a property constrained without a type renders as a field that honors the constraint rather than as free text. This part applies whether or not `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType` is set, so a property described by, say, `additionalProperties: { enum: ['a', 'b'] }` now renders as a select over that enum for every form, and data of another type than the enum allows is as invisible in it as it would be in a select the schema asked for by naming a type. It leaves out the subschema keywords, which could name a type that cannot merge with the guessed one, the identifiers, which every stubbed property would otherwise share, and a `default` of another type than the data. `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` reads the flag and treats such a type as no type at all when both schemas it compares are equally unconstrained, so a value whose type changes is kept instead of being discarded as data belonging to an old schema. A new schema that declares a type of its own is still the type the data has to satisfy, so switching from a free-form option to one that requires a number of the same key clears a string rather than leaving it in a number field ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** The generic defaults changed from `any`: `T` (the form data) now defaults to `unknown` and `F` (the form context) to `FormContextType`. Every field, widget and template inherits them, so a component or callback written without a type argument now receives `unknown` form data and must narrow it or name its type (`FieldProps<MyData>`, `IChangeEvent<MyData>`). Code that passes its own `T` keeps its types, and a value annotated with the bare default still fits a typed `Form`: component slots (the `TemplatesType` entries, `Widget`, `Field` and the `ui:` template overrides) are typed with the new `SlotComponent<P>`, which checks a component's props bivariantly, and `FieldProps.onChange`, `WidgetProps.onChange` and the `items` function of a `UiSchema` take their `T` the same way, so a `UiSchema`, `CustomValidator` or `ErrorTransformer`, a handler taking a bare `IChangeEvent`, and a component written against the bare props all fit a typed `Form<MyData>`. A component annotated as a React `ComponentType` or `FunctionComponent` still fits every slot, but a `Widget`, `Field` or template value is no longer assignable to a `ComponentType` annotation; annotate it with the RJSF type instead. The `F` default is a rename with no narrowing effect yet: `FormContextType` still aliases `GenericObjectType` (`Record<string, any>`), so `formContext.foo` is still `any` until `FormContextType` is retyped in a follow-up. `Registry.schemaUtils` is now `SchemaUtilsType<T, S, F>` rather than `SchemaUtilsType<T, S>`, which the old `any` default had been hiding
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `ValidatorType` has no `T` parameter: it is `ValidatorType<S, F>`, `validateFormData<T>()` takes the form data type per call, and `isValid()` and `rawValidation()` take `unknown` form data. A validator checks data of any shape against a schema, so one instance serves every `Form` whatever `T` it renders, and a validator package's default export fits a typed `Form<MyData>` again. `SchemaUtilsType.getValidator()` and `doesSchemaUtilsDiffer()`, `createSchemaUtils()` and every `schema/` utility take a `ValidatorType<S, F>`; `DefaultFormStateBehavior.arrayMinItems.computeSkipPopulate` drops the `T` it never used. `createSchemaUtils()` no longer picks `T` up from the validator, so pass it (`createSchemaUtils<MyData>(validator, schema)`) when the `SchemaUtilsType` should be typed. A `ValidatorType<MyData>` annotation or an `implements ValidatorType<T, S, F>` needs the `T` removed
- The `help` option of `ui:options` (and `ui:help`) accepts `string | ReactElement`, matching `FieldHelpProps.help` and the element `SchemaField` already renders; it was typed `string`, which a typed `UiSchema<MyData>` now enforces
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Added `logOnce()`, which logs a message through `console.warn()` (or `console.error()`) only the first time it is seen, and `resetLogOnce()` to forget what it has logged. The warnings and errors `@rjsf/utils` raises while resolving schemas and uiSchemas now go through it, and the `dependencies` `oneOf` warning from `retrieveSchema()` now names the dependency it is about. What has been logged is remembered by the module, not by a `Form`, so it outlives any one form: a test suite that asserts on one of these warnings sees it only in whichever test runs first, and needs `resetLogOnce()` before each test — e.g. `beforeEach(resetLogOnce)` in a Vitest or Jest setup file — while a server rendering many forms in one process reports a given message for whichever form reaches it first, so a second tenant's identical misconfiguration stays silent until the process restarts ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- `getItemUiSchemaForItem()` takes an optional fifth `arrayFieldPath` argument, the `FieldPath` of the array the item belongs to, and the error it logs when the function form of `uiSchema.items` throws names the item by that path instead of by index alone: a message that read `Error executing dynamic uiSchema.items function for item at index 0:` now reads `... for people[0]:`. `ArrayField` passes the path, so this is the message a form produces, and a test asserting on the old text has to be updated. A caller with no path to give — a custom `ArrayField`, say — can omit the argument, and the message names the index alone as before ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- Fixed `getClosestMatchingOption()` overflowing the stack when it had to score an option holding a recursive `$ref` — anything that scores `oneOf`/`anyOf` options, so a `MultiSchemaField`, `getDefaultFormState()` or `omitExtraData()` over a self-referencing definition. `resolveAllReferences()` leaves a recursive `$ref` in place, so `calculateIndexScore()` re-resolved the same definition at every level, against an empty object it made up once the form data ran out. It now follows a `$ref` only for as long as the property has form data to score, which also means a `$ref` whose value is missing or `null` no longer contributes the point it could previously earn for a `type: 'null'` property or a property-less `type: 'object'` definition — scores reachable through `getClosestMatchingOption()` are unaffected, because `resolveAllReferences()` has already inlined every `$ref` it can follow, but a caller reaching past the package index for `calculateIndexScore()` itself may see a lower score. `calculateIndexScore()` also reads each property's value as an own property, so a recursive `$ref` under a property legally named `toString`, `constructor` or `valueOf` is no longer scored against the value every object inherits ([#5337](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5337))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplateProps.rawHelp` is `string | ReactElement` and carries whatever `ui:help` resolved to, instead of being `string` and silently `undefined` for a `ui:help` passed as a React element. It is now a reliable truthy check for whether a field has any help, which is what a `FieldTemplate` gating on it wants; code that used it as a string (`rawHelp.length`, or passing it where a `string` is required) has to narrow it first ([#5326](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5326))
- Removed the `jsonpointer` dependency; a `$ref`'s JSON pointer fragment resolves through the schema's own keys alone, so a `#/__proto__` ref finds nothing instead of `Object.prototype`, unless the schema has a genuine own `__proto__` key
- Removed the `fast-uri` dependency; `$ref`/`$id` URIs are resolved and compared with the platform `URL` parser. Relative `$ref`s against a relative or fragment base (a root without `$id`, or a nested relative `$id`) now follow RFC 3986 resolution, so some refs that previously failed to resolve, such as `../other.json`, now do; comparison decodes every percent-encoded unreserved character (`%2E` matches `.` as well as `%7E` matching `~`); and `urn:` namespace identifiers are compared case-sensitively
- Moved `rawErrors` from `ArrayFieldTemplateProps` to `ContainerFieldTemplateProps`, so `ObjectFieldTemplateProps` declares it too ([#5332](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5332))
- Added an optional `id` to `MultiSchemaFieldTemplateProps`: the id of the field whose option is selected, which the selected option's field shares ([#5332](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5332))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Every reader of a schema's options reads `anyOf` before `oneOf`, and a select over object or array constants is treated as one value. Added `enumOptionValueLabel()`, `getXxxOfKey()`, `isConstantOptionList()`, `isConstantSelect()`, `isWholeValueSelect()` and `useSelectFocusHandlers()`. See the upgrade guide for details ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309))
- Added `getOptionalDataControlsType()`, which `shouldRenderOptionalField()` and `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` now share. An empty `anyOf: []`/`oneOf: []` counts as no option list, so a typed `object`/`array` beside one gets the controls, and `getDefaultFormState()` computes defaults for it from its own type. `getDefaultFormState()` keeps form data beside an empty option list or `enum: []` instead of replacing it with the default, and `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` no longer reports a `ui:required` field of an object `anyOf`/`oneOf` option while its value is `{}`, since the option hides its fields behind its own Add button then ([#5316](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5316))
- Added `dateElementId(rootId, type)`, the id `DateElement` gives each of the date element selectors an `AltDateWidget` renders, so a theme can point a label at the year, month or day control instead of rebuilding that shape itself ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Added `fieldLabelId(id)`, the id of the label a `FieldTemplate` renders above a field, which a group of controls points `aria-labelledby` at, since a `label htmlFor` cannot name a group ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `getDisplayLabel()` reads the widget a `boolean` field names from the reduced ui options rather than from the `ui:widget` key alone, so `ui:options: { widget: 'radio' }` and `ui:widget: 'radio'` agree about whether the template renders a label. A `boolean` whose widget was named through `ui:options.widget` had its label suppressed as though it had no widget at all, leaving a radio group or a select with no label above it; a theme's `FieldTemplate` now receives `displayLabel: true` for that spelling. Naming the checkbox a `boolean` already resolves to by its registry key, `CheckboxWidget`, now counts as naming no other widget and returns `displayLabel: false`, since that widget renders the field's label itself: every theme rendered a second label beside it before. The `checkbox` alias still returns `true`, which is the spelling a `FieldTemplate` keys its own checkbox layout on ([#5318](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5318))
- Added `getSchemaOwnTypes()`, returning the JSON Schema types a schema says its value has, or `undefined` for one that says nothing about it: the recognized names of a `type` list, the one a `type` names, the types a typeless `enum` or `const` pins the value to, or the type `getSchemaType()` infers. A schema whose type was guessed from the form data, or whose only `type` is a name JSON Schema does not define, says nothing. `@rjsf/core`'s fallback UI reads it both for the types its selector offers and for whether to offer one at all ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- Fixed `retrieveSchema()` choosing the wrong `if`/`then`/`else` branch when the form data is `0`, `false` or `''`: the condition was evaluated against `{}` instead of the value ([#5369](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5369))
- Fixed `getClosestMatchingOption()` ignoring a falsy value when scoring options: a `const` of `false`, `0` or `''` never raised or lowered an option's score, and a `0`, `false` or `''` under a nested `oneOf`/`anyOf` was not scored at all. A `null` or missing value is still not scored against a nested `oneOf`/`anyOf`, and a missing value is not compared to a `const: null`. A falsy `default` is still not scored, as before ([#5369](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5369))
- `useAltDateWidgetProps()`'s Clear button now blanks a partially filled date, which it left in place before, since clearing an already empty value did not change it. Completing a date, Clear and Now all drop the partial selection when they send a value, so a parent that rejects the value or stores it asynchronously shows its own value again rather than the selection ([#5381](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5381))
- Fixed `getDefaultFormState()` spreading a non-object value into index keys when a non-object schema has an object `default`: a string or array form data value (e.g. `'y'` for `{ type: 'string', default: { a: 1 } }`) or a computed `const` (`{ type: 'string', const: 'x', default: { a: 1 } }`) is now returned as is instead of as `{ "0": "y" }` / `{ "0": "x" }`, a number or boolean form data value, which was replaced by `{}`, is kept, and a string form data value the schema rejects (e.g. `'zz'` for `{ type: 'string', enum: ['a', 'b'], default: { a: 1 } }`) gives the `default` instead of merging in its characters ([#5386](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5386))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `enumOptionValueEncoder()` in favor of the new `enumOptionsDomValues()`, which encodes a widget's whole option list so `realValue` options whose `String()` matches, such as `1` and `'1'`, get distinct DOM values. See the upgrade guide ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- Added `getExampleSuggestions()` and `useOptionFocusHandlers()`, which every theme's `BaseInputTemplate` and `CheckboxesWidget` use ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315))
- `optionsList()` takes an optional `fallbackLabel` that labels an option nothing else names, before it falls back to the value, which `BooleanField` uses for its Yes/No labels. It reads a constant option's value from its constant even under a `discriminator` or `ui:optionsSchemaSelector`, which name a property of object options, so such a select no longer offers only `undefined` values. A constant option that declares that property is still read by it ([#5319](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5319))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Every validator-based schema function takes a `SchemaContext` — the `validator`, `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior` together — in place of its `validator`, and passes it along whole, so a setting can no longer be dropped mid-chain. Their separate `customMergeAllOf`/`defaultFormStateBehavior` parameters are gone. `getDefaultFormState()` takes a `SchemaContext` then a `GetDefaultFormStateProps` object; `createSchemaUtils()` and `SchemaUtilsType.doesSchemaUtilsDiffer()` take a `SchemaContext` and the `rootSchema`; `computeSkipPopulate()` receives it in place of the `validator`. `ValidatorType` is unchanged, as the bullet below describes. Fixes two sites that dropped a `customMergeAllOf`: `SchemaUtilsType.omitExtraData()` and the `$ref` options of a `oneOf` inside `dependencies` ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- `ValidatorType` keeps its v6 signatures: `validateFormData()`, `isValid()` and `rawValidation()` take no `SchemaContext`, so a custom validator needs no change. A context carries a `validator`, so passing one to a validator would have meant every implementation discarding the validator it was handed in favor of itself. `validateFormData()` gains one optional trailing `getCustomValidateFormData`, returning the data to hand `customValidate` with the form's defaults applied; left out, the validator computes them as it did in v6. It returns the data rather than being the data so that defaults which come out `undefined` are still an answer, and so a validator computing its own never makes the caller compute them too ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- `omitExtraData()` resolves each `allOf` entry's references before merging, as `resolveSchema()` does for the schema the form renders. An entry that is a `$ref` had its properties hoisted rather than followed, so the referenced schema's data was taken for extra data and deleted on submit; and a `customMergeAllOf` was handed a `$ref` the form's own merge never sees, so one that rejects it fell back to dropping the `allOf` and deleted fields the user could see and edit. The merge is also wrapped in a `try`/`catch` like `retrieveSchema()`'s, warning once and continuing without the `allOf` rather than throwing out of `Form`'s change and submit handlers ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- `createSchemaUtils()` warns, once, when it is handed a validator where the `SchemaContext` belongs, as a v6 call did. Spreading a validator leaves `validator` undefined, so the first schema function failed with a `TypeError` from inside `retrieveSchema()` that named nothing ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- Added `getFieldClassNames()`, which builds the `classNames` string a `FieldTemplate` receives (`rjsf-field`, `rjsf-field-<type>`, `rjsf-field-error` and `ui:classNames`), and `omitConsumedStyling()`, which returns a `uiSchema` with the class names and style a field's own `FieldTemplate` consumed removed in all four spellings, so a child cannot apply them a second time (see [#439](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/439)). Both are what `SchemaField` now calls, and a custom field rendering its own `FieldTemplate` should call them too ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- Added `getDeprecatedHandling()`, which resolves how a field has to render a `deprecated` schema — `hide`, `disable` or the `label` default a deprecated schema with no `ui:deprecatedHandling` falls back to — and returns undefined for a schema that is not deprecated. `SchemaField` calls it, and a custom field rendering its own `FieldTemplate` should call it rather than read the option directly ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327))
- Added `getTemplates(registry, uiOptions)`, which returns the registry's templates with each `uiOptions` override resolved through `getTemplate()`, and `resolveWidget(schema, widget, widgets)`, which returns `getWidget()`'s widget as `{ Widget }`. Core and the themes destructure the templates and widgets they render from these, which React's `static-components` lint rule accepts where it reports a component returned by a call during render ([#5384](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5384))
- Fixed `getDefaultFormState()` for an array with `minItems` whose `default` isn't an array: it's now ignored when filling the array, where a string default was concatenated into a string and any other non-array default (an object, a number, `true`) threw ([#5387](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5387))
- `logOnce()` compares a plain-object or array `error` by its JSON, so two different plain-object payloads logged with the same message are no longer taken for one, and the `logUnsupportedDefaultForEnum()` error spells an object default out as JSON rather than `[object Object]`. `enumOptionValueLabel()` spells a value the same way, so a value reads the same in an option label as in a warning: a `Date`, a `Map` or a nested `BigInt` is spelled as `String()` spells it, and a circular plain object or array no longer throws. ([#5382](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5382))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `UiSchemaDefinitions<S, F>` drops its `T` parameter and types every entry as `UiSchema<unknown, S, F>`, the type `UiSchema`'s `ui:definitions` key now uses too. A definition applies to whichever field references it, so typing it by the root form's data rejected the nested keys of the field it describes ([#5372](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5372))
- Fixed `getDateTimeLocalValue()` returning `undefined` for an epoch number or a `Date`: a finite number or a valid `Date` is now converted to its UTC ISO string for `date-time`/`datetime`, to the day it names for a `date` that is a UTC midnight, and to local wall-clock time for any other format. Its result also gains `requiresOffset`, true for `date-time`/`datetime` ([#5393](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5393))
- `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` treats an array's items the same way whether they are objects or scalars. An `enum: []` now offers no constraint instead of rejecting every value, matching how an empty `anyOf`/`oneOf` already read and how `isConstantSelect()` reads one, and leaves an `anyOf`/`oneOf` of constants beside it to decide — so an array element or a property value is kept where it used to be dropped or cleared, and a value outside a list of constants spelled that way is now checked against that list rather than against nothing. A `maxItems` of `0` now clears the array on both paths instead of only where the items are sanitized per element. Elements a narrowed items `enum` rejects were already dropped for both since [#5350](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5350) ([#5346](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5346))
- `dataURItoBlob()` keeps the decoding error it catches as the `cause` of the `File is invalid` error it throws ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` adds the `required` error for a non-boolean truthy `ui:required`, such as `1` or `'true'` from an untyped JSON uiSchema, which `SchemaField` already marks as required. That includes the string `'false'`, which is truthy ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- Added `uiBooleanOption()`, which reads a boolean `ui:` option such as `ui:required`, `ui:hideError` or `ui:label` as `Boolean()` does, since an untyped JSON uiSchema may hold `0`, `1`, `null` or a string there. It returns `undefined` only for `undefined` and reads `null` as `false`, so an option where `null` means unset, such as `ui:disabled`, `ui:readonly` or `ui:autofocus`, applies its inherited fallback before the call ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- `useAltDateWidgetProps()`'s `handleChange()` stores a picked part as a number, so `elements[].value` is the `number` that `DateElementProp` declares rather than the string a widget passed in ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- Clearing a date part with an empty string in `useAltDateWidgetProps()` leaves that part unset, rather than treating it as zero and emitting a date in the previous month or year ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `EnumOptionsType.value` is `unknown` instead of `any`, so the helpers typed by it (`enumOptionsSelectValue()`, `enumOptionsDeselectValue()`, `enumOptionsValueForIndex()`) return `unknown` values, and `enumOptionSelectedValue()` takes `value` as `unknown` and returns `string` (or `string[]` for `multiple`) or the type of its `emptyValue` (`undefined` when it is omitted) instead of `any`. A call that passes `<S>` explicitly together with an `emptyValue` no longer compiles, since the second type parameter then defaults to `undefined`: drop the type arguments, or pass `<S, typeof emptyValue>`. `UIOptionsType`'s `emptyValue`, `initialValue` and index signature are `unknown`, so `ui:options` values read without a declared key, and an `emptyValue` passed on from `options`, have to be narrowed before use. Code that used an option's value as a specific type (a `ReactNode`, a string, an `EnumValue` for `enumDisabled.includes()`) has to narrow or convert it first; see the [upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#enumoptionstypevalue-is-unknown-breaking-change) ([#5383](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5383))
- `getTemplate()` returns the registry's template when a `ui:options` template override is a string that names no registered template, instead of returning the string itself ([#5383](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5383))
- Added `schemaKey()`, the key a validator caches a compiled function under: the schema's `$id`, or its content hash when that is missing or empty. The three validators all call it, where each had a copy and they disagreed on the empty case ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338))
- A key merged with a matching `patternProperties` keeps the referenced schema's own `properties`, where it had rendered the pattern's fields instead of its own, and a recursive one renders the cycle indicator a recursive `$ref` renders rather than nesting fields until the heap runs out ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338))
- Added `isComponentType()`, which accepts `memo()`, `forwardRef()` and `lazy()` components and rejects React elements. `getUiOptions()` no longer drops a `ui:widget` given as one of those components, and `getWidget()` throws `Unsupported widget definition` for a widget that isn't a component. Widget names are looked up as own properties, so `'toString'` no longer resolves to an inherited function ([#5363](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5363))
- `getSchemaType()` resolves a `type` list to its first non-null type that JSON Schema defines, and returns `string | undefined`. `getDefaultFormState()` follows it, so a null-first list gets that type's defaults, unless it holds a value of another type it names: an object held by a `['null', 'array', 'object']` gets no array items, and a `null` passed in for a `['null', 'object']` is kept, as one nested in an object already was. `omitExtraData()` follows it too, so an object held by a `['null', 'object', 'string']` is pruned by its `properties`, or dropped when the schema declares none, where it was kept ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Added `getSchemaTypeForValue()`, which `omitExtraData()` and `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` use to leave a value of another type a `type` list names as it is, so a string held by a `['null', 'object', 'string']` is neither dropped nor checked for properties, and `omitExtraData()` keeps a `null` held by a `['null', 'object']` or `['null', 'array']` rather than dropping it ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Added `getWidgetType()`, the type whose field renders a widget, which `getWidget()` looks an alias up by, so a `type` list resolving to a `string`, `number`, `integer` or `boolean` takes a widget of another of those types it names. `resolveDefaultWidget()` takes the widget a `format` names only from the type the list resolves to, so an `email` on a `['number', 'string']` keeps its number input. A select over a `type` list takes only the widgets of the field it renders through: those of the one non-null type it names, or, when it names several, a string's in whatever order it names them, as `StringField` renders it, except that one naming an `object` or `array` takes only the `select`, `radio` and `hidden` that keep an option whole. So a `textarea` on a select over `['string', 'integer']` renders while a `checkbox` on an `enum` of `['a', true]` throws `No widget` rather than writing a `false` no option holds ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Added `getFieldTypeForWidget()`, the type of the field `SchemaField` renders a schema with its `ui:widget` by, which `SchemaField`, `getDisplayLabel()` and `getDefaultFormState()` follow, as do the value `ObjectField` gives a new additional property and daisyui's required checkbox marker: a required `['null', 'boolean', 'string']` shown as a `textarea` is not filled in with `false`. It reads a select over a `type` list as `SchemaField` renders it, with the new `getSelectFieldType()`: the one non-null type it names or else a `string`, so an `enum` of `[null, true, 'a']` on a `['null', 'boolean', 'string']` keeps its label and is not filled in with a `false` it does not offer. `getInputProps()` gives the native number input of an `updown` on a `['string', 'number']` a `step="any"` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Exported `isSchemaObject()`, which narrows a subschema that may be a boolean or missing to a schema object ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- `isCustomWidget()` reads a `widget` set to `undefined` as naming no widget ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- Added `callWithDeferredThrow()`, which calls a consumer's callback and rethrows its throw from a timer, so a widget that calls one from an Effect cannot unmount the form. The throw then escapes error boundaries: a browser reports it as an uncaught error, a test runner as an unhandled error, and Node as an `uncaughtException` ([#5437](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5437))
- `retrieveSchema()` stubs a key matching none of an object's `patternProperties` patterns from the data it holds, as it already did under `additionalProperties: true`, so renaming a property onto such a name leaves it editable instead of stranding its value behind an empty `{ type: 'null' }` field. A key the schema forbids is still stubbed as `{ type: 'null' }` ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` stubs a key whose matching `patternProperties` subschema names no type from the data the key holds, keeping what that subschema does say about the value, as it already did for a typeless `additionalProperties`. Such a key used to render as the unsupported field no type can draw ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- Added `getAdditionalPropertySchema()`, which returns the schema an object says applies to a key its own `properties` don't name: the `allOf` of the `patternProperties` subschemas whose patterns match it, or `additionalProperties`, or the `unevaluatedProperties` that answers where no `additionalProperties` evaluates it, with `false` for a key the object forbids and `true` for one it allows without describing. Either keyword spelled `undefined` reads as absent, the way a validator reads it. `retrieveSchema()` and `ObjectField` both read it, so a key's field and its seed cannot disagree about which schema describes it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- Added `getAdditionalPropertyType()`, which returns the type such a subschema says the property holds, through `getSchemaOwnTypes()`, so a typeless `enum` of numbers is stubbed and seeded as a number rather than as the `string` `getSchemaType()` answers for any typeless `enum` ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `retrieveSchema()` resolves the subschema describing an additional property before stubbing it, so a `$ref` beside other keywords has those keywords layered over the referenced ones and an `allOf` naming a type names the stub's type. A `$ref` used to be resolved alone, and other subschema keywords not resolved at all ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `retrieveSchema()` stubs an additional property whose options disagree about the type without naming a type of its own, where it used to name `object` — which rendered an empty object fieldset beside the option selector and seeded the `{}` no primitive option accepts. Options that agree on a type, `object` included, still name it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` stubs the extra keys the form data holds, and `canExpand()` offers the add button for another, for an object whose only keyword describing those keys is an `unevaluatedProperties`. Both used to need an `additionalProperties` or `patternProperties` beside it, and both now ask the new `allowsAdditionalProperties()`, as `ObjectField` does ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- Added `allowsAdditionalProperties()`, which returns whether an object takes keys its own `properties` don't name: any `patternProperties` says it does, whatever an `additionalProperties: false` beside it says, and otherwise the keyword describing those names answers, as long as it neither rejects them nor is missing ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` leaves a key an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option renders to that option, rather than stubbing it as an additional property beside the option's field, which left the one value two fields to be written from under one pair of ids. An option renders the names it declares inline, through its `$ref`, its `allOf` entries, the branch its condition takes, a `dependencies` entry the data brings in, and its own options; one that takes keys of its own renders every extra key the data holds. A reference is read only for the keywords the option leaves to it, since resolution replaces the definition's with the option's own rather than joining the two: a `properties` or an `if` the option shadows describes nothing it renders, so a name behind the shadowed one is the object's to stub. Under `additionalProperties`, or a matching pattern, only a name every option renders is left to the options, so a value under an option that declares none keeps a field to edit or remove it with; under `unevaluatedProperties`, which says nothing about a key the matching option evaluates, the names that option renders are left to it while a name only another option declares is stubbed. The matching option is the one `getFirstMatchingOption()` answers with, which is not always the one the selector is on: `MultiSchemaField` scores the options and keeps an explicit choice, neither of which this can read ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` leaves the identifiers (`$id`, `$anchor`, `$dynamicAnchor`, `$schema`, `$vocabulary`) and the containers (`$defs`, `definitions`) out of the stub it builds for an additional property whose type the subschema only implies, as it already left them out of one whose type is guessed from the data: copying an `$id` onto every sibling property would route them all to a field registered under it. A subschema that names its type or offers options keeps them, as does one reached through a `$ref` whose definition names its type. Every stub keeps the subschema's symbol markers, so a property described through a `$ref` still has that definition's `ui:definitions` entry applied to it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- Added `getXxxOfOptions()`, the companion to `getXxxOfKey()` for readers that want the `anyOf`/`oneOf` options themselves, so an empty `anyOf: []` is no longer read over a `oneOf` that has options ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `omitExtraData()` keeps a key an `unevaluatedProperties` describes where the object names no `additionalProperties` to evaluate it, with the precedence `getAdditionalPropertySchema()` reads the two keywords with. The form now renders such a key and offers the add button for another, so pruning them left a field whose value the next change deleted ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `getSchemaType()` reads an `unevaluatedProperties` as implying `object`, as it already reads `properties`, `additionalProperties` and `patternProperties`, so a subschema describing its keys only through that keyword renders them rather than falling through to a stub built from the value's type ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `getAdditionalPropertyType()` takes an optional `rootSchema` and reads a subschema that names no type of its own through the subschemas merged into it — its `allOf` entries and the definition its `$ref` names — so options spelled as references or composed of an `allOf` agree on a type the way inline options do. The first merged subschema to name a type answers, including for one whose options name no type between them, so `{ allOf: [{ type: 'number' }], anyOf: [{ minimum: 1 }, { maximum: 5 }] }` is read as a number ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `allowsAdditionalProperties()` reads an empty `patternProperties` as naming no pattern, the way an empty `properties` declares no name. `getFirstMatchingOption()` asks it whether an option describes keys its `properties` don't name, in place of its own copy of that question, so an option describing a map only through `unevaluatedProperties` is scored as the map it is rather than as a schema that matches nothing ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` reads an object's declared property names as own names, so a key called `constructor` or `toString` is stubbed rather than passed over as one the `properties` already name, and declares a `__proto__` key through `defineProperty` rather than an assignment the prototype's setter would take for a prototype to re-point. The walk of an object's options and the lookup of the references among them run only for an object whose data holds an extra key, and the type a subschema names is read once per subschema rather than once per key ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` reads a definition that two of the subschemas merged into one schema both name, where it had read it only on the first of them: a reference followed once is now left unfollowed only for the entries below it on that walk rather than for the ones beside it, as it already was for an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option. An entry that shadows the definition's `properties` no longer leaves the names behind it stubbed over the field the entry beside it renders them with, and one that shadows its `type` no longer leaves the type behind it unread ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` scores an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option whose `$ref` names no definition, or names one circularly, as it stands while matching the option an object's `unevaluatedProperties` leaves its keys to, where resolving it threw and took down an object that asked nothing but which of its keys to stub ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` reads the branch a `dependencies` entry's `oneOf` settles on the form data, rather than reading those branches as options a user chooses between, when deciding which keys an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option renders. `withExactlyOneSubschema()` merges that branch in with no selector involved, so a name only it declares was stubbed beside the field the branch renders it with, giving one value two fields under one pair of ids. The branches are read behind the `$ref` either the dependency or a branch of it is spelled as, which `withDependentSchema()` resolves before settling the branch: read unresolved, such a dependency offers no branches and such a branch names no condition, so none qualified and every name the matching one declares was stubbed ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `allowsAdditionalProperties()`, `getAdditionalPropertySchema()`, `additionalPropertiesKeyword()` and `getMatchingPatternProperties()` move to a module of their own. They read keywords off a schema and need nothing from the resolver, which had `getFirstMatchingOption()` and `retrieveSchema()` importing each other and `canExpand()` — which every theme's `ObjectFieldTemplate` calls — importing the resolver for one predicate. **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getMatchingPatternProperties()` is no longer exported from `schema/retrieveSchema.ts`, whose deep import was the only path it had on 6.x; it is exported from the package root, which is the path to take, and from `additionalPropertiesUtils.ts` ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` leaves a stub it built for an extra key out of the `patternProperties` merge, so retrieving a schema it has retrieved already — which `SchemaField` and `ObjectField` do to the same object on every render — describes that key as one retrieval does. The stub was merged with the very patterns it was built from, taking back the `$id` that routes every sibling property to the one field registered under it, a `default` of the pattern's type over a value of another, and a `not` the stub leaves to validation, so what rendered was not the stub the pattern builds. A key described by a pattern, by an `additionalProperties` and by an `unevaluatedProperties` now renders alike ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `getMatchingPatternProperties()` compiles a `patternProperties` pattern with the `u` flag, the way a validator compiles it — Ajv's `unicodeRegExp` is on by default, and a pattern invalid under that flag is one Ajv rejects at compile time. A pattern holding a unicode property escape such as `\p{Lu}` matched the literal text `p{Lu}` instead, so the key the form stubbed, offered the add button and seeded was not the key validation accepted. A pattern that flag rejects, such as one holding a `\-` or a `[\w-.]`, is compiled unflagged rather than left to throw out of every render of the object and every prune of its data, since it is a pattern only validation has anything to say about. `omitExtraData()` reads its patterns through the same function rather than compiling them twice more of its own, so the keys it keeps are the keys the form describes ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `omitExtraData()` keeps what every `patternProperties` pattern matching a key describes, where it filtered the value through the first matching pattern alone: `retrieveSchema()` stubs such a key with the merge of all of them, so a value only another of them describes was one a field on screen edited and the next change dropped. A pattern of `false` among them drops the key, as it forbids it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `retrieveSchema()` reads the rest of a `dependencies` entry spelled as a `$ref` without the `oneOf` behind that reference, as it does for an entry that spells its branches inline, when deciding which keys an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option renders. Read through the reference the branches were options of their own, so a name every one of them declares counted as a name the form renders even where no single branch qualified and `withExactlyOneSubschema()` merged none of the `oneOf` in, leaving the value it holds no field at all ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `omitExtraData()` reads what a key its object's `properties` don't name requires from the merge of the patterns describing it rather than off the `allOf` wrapping them, and keeps such a key where the object's own `required` names it. The wrapper requires nothing, so a pattern-matched object whose only child was required and empty was pruned the way an all-optional one is, and a required key described by a `patternProperties` pattern, an `additionalProperties` or an `unevaluatedProperties` was dropped from the data the form then reports it as missing from, for a key the user has a field for and has filled in as far as it asks them to ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- `omitExtraData()` drops a value whose `allOf` merges to `false`, which is what an `allOf` holding a `false` merges to and what no value satisfies. The merged boolean answered nothing to the keyword handlers that read it, so the value reached the fallback that returns it as it was written, keeping every key the other entries describe nothing about ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330))
- Fixed `makeAllReferencesAbsolute()` treating any property named `$ref` or `$id` as a reference, fixing [#5457](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5457). In 6.x a property named `$ref` holding an **object** value crashed resolution, while a **string** `$ref` carried by instance data, such as `default: { $ref: '#/a' }`, was silently rewritten to an absolute URI, changing the data a form pre-filled with no error, and a property named `$id` re-based its siblings. The walk now follows the schema's structure, recursing only through the keywords that hold a schema, an array of schemas or a map of names to schemas: only a string `$ref` is resolved, only a string `$id` re-bases its subtree, and instance data, schema-map entries named like data keywords, and unknown keywords all pass through untouched. The lookup side is fixed with it: an `$id` carried by `default`/`const` instance data is no longer matched as an embedded schema.
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Because the `makeAllReferencesAbsolute()` walk no longer looks inside unknown keywords, schemas kept under a container it does not know, such as an OpenAPI-style `components` block or an `x-*` vendor key, are no longer treated as part of the bundle: a `$ref` inside one is left as written rather than made absolute, and an `$id` declared there is no longer found as an embedded schema. The usual OpenAPI layout, with `components` at the root and `#/components/...` refs, keeps working, because a relative `$ref` left as written still resolves against the root. Two cases break: an `$id` declared inside the container is no longer found, and a relative `$ref` inside it that depends on a nested `$id` above it no longer resolves, so resolution that reached either before now throws `Could not find a definition for ...`. Move schemas that rely on those two cases under `$defs` (draft 2020-12) or `definitions` (draft 7) so the walk reaches them
- Exported `DEFAULT_BOOLEAN_WIDGET`, the registry key the `checkbox` alias of a `boolean` resolves to, so a theme can read that widget from its registry without copying the key ([#5456](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5456))
- `validationDataMerge()` keeps the existing `errors` when their `errorSchema` is empty, which it is when a `transformErrors` clears every `message`: merging an `ErrorSchema` into such a validation dropped them from the list, so a form that had `extraErrors` or a field's own errors to merge stopped listing them. It reads the `errors` whatever the `errorSchema` holds, so a `validationData` passed without the `errors` its type requires throws where one with an empty `errorSchema` used to be merged ([#5034](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5034))
- `getChangedFields()` reads a key that is missing as one holding `undefined`, whichever object lacks it and at any depth: a key only the second object had counted as changed even while it held `undefined`, and an object or array item that differed only in such a key was reported whole. An empty slot of an array reads as an item holding `undefined` too, where it used to be skipped. A parent that drops a key holding `undefined` from the `formData` of a form that is not live-validated therefore no longer clears that field's errors, nor those of the objects holding it. The comparison it reads the keys by is exported as `deepEqualsUndefinedAsMissing()` ([#5034](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5034))
- `validationDataMerge()` with `preventDuplicates` leaves out of the `errors` it appends the additional errors that `mergeObjects()` leaves out of the merged `errorSchema`, those whose message the existing `errorSchema` already holds under the same keys, where it listed them a second time. The list therefore shows an error once wherever its field shows it once, whatever way the existing error's `property` spells the path ([#5442](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5442))
- Added `propertyToPath()`, which splits an `RJSFValidationError`'s `property` into path segments the way `toErrorSchema()` does, giving an empty path for a missing or root `property` ([#5442](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5442))
- Added `TranslatableString.SelectPlaceholder`, which the shadcn multiple `SelectWidget` uses to name an untitled combobox and both shadcn select widgets use to fill the visible placeholder when the author gives no `ui:placeholder`; for the single `SelectWidget` that changes a previously blank trigger ([#5458](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5458))
- Fixed `utcToLocal()` returning an empty string for an epoch of `0`, which it took for no value, and `0NaN-NaN-NaNTNaN:NaN:NaN.NaN` for an invalid `Date` or text that isn't a date. It now gives an empty string for `null`, booleans and `Boolean` objects, which `Date` reads as the epoch or the millisecond after it; for a value `Date` throws on, such as a bigint, a symbol or an object with no primitive value, which used to crash the render; for anything that doesn't parse to a valid date; and for a date whose local year is before 1, which a `datetime-local` input can't show. Any other value still goes through `Date`, so an object whose `valueOf()` gives an epoch, such as a moment or dayjs object, converts as before. `getDateTimeLocalValue()` relies on it for that lower bound and keeps its own cap at the year 9999, which the themes' pickers need, so the year 0 now gives `undefined` there like the years before it; for `date-time` and `datetime` it still hands the themes the UTC ISO string, as before. The `jsonDate` parameter is typed `unknown`, fixing [#5431](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5431)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Added `TranslatableString.YearLabel`, `MonthLabel`, `DayLabel`, `HourLabel`, `MinuteLabel`, `SecondLabel` and `DateElementAriaLabel` (`'%1, %2'`), `dateElementLabel()`, `dateElementAriaLabel()` and `widgetAriaProps()`. `DateElement` gives its `SelectWidget` `dateElementId()` as its `name`, the translated part as its `placeholder`, the field's `label` and the part as its `aria-label` and the field's description ids as its `aria-describedby`; its `label` prop is optional, it takes an already translated `elementLabel` in place of translating the part itself, and its `name` prop is ignored and deprecated. `widgetAriaProps()` follows the ids its caller passed with `descriptionId(id)` while the select shows the description of its selected option, under the same conditions as `SelectedOptionDescription`, so that description is still announced, and a date part is described by its field's ids alone. `WidgetProps` documents `aria-label` and `aria-describedby`, which only a `SelectWidget` is required to honor ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355), [#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357))
- Added `TranslatableString.DoneLabel` ("Done") and `TranslatableString.TimeLabel` ("Time"), for the daisyUI date pickers ([#5482](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5482))
- Added `enumOptionsIsDisabled()`, the strict `ui:enumDisabled` check `groupEnumOptions()` and every `CheckboxesWidget` and `RadioWidget` now share ([#5408](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5408))
- Added the `itemLabel` option to `UIOptionsType`, the optional `itemButtonProps` prop (typed by the new `ArrayFieldItemButtonProps`) to `ArrayFieldItemButtonsTemplateProps`, and the `CopyItemButton`, `MoveDownItemButton`, `MoveUpItemButton`, `RemoveItemButton` and `ItemNameWithNumber` translatable strings ([#5474](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5474))
- `localToUTC()` reads a `datetime-local` value whose year has five or six digits, which the input writes as plain digits and `Date` reads only as the signed six-digit `+YYYYYY`, so `'10000-07-01T00:01'` gives the UTC ISO string with its year written as `+010000` where it gave the `null` that `toJSON()` gives an invalid date. Years 1-9999 are the supported range, as in `getDateTimeLocalValue()`, so a five or six digit year, up to the last instant `Date` holds, 275760-09-13T00:00Z, gives a string that fails `date-time` validation instead of a `null` that wipes the value. A four-digit year and any other value, text or not, go to `Date` as they are, and a value `Date` throws for, such as a bigint, a symbol or an object with no primitive value, gives `null` too. `utcToLocal()` takes a `Boolean` object from another realm, which `instanceof Boolean` misses, for no value too, following up [#5473](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5473)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `localToUTC()` takes `unknown` and returns `string | null | undefined`, where its inferred return type was `string | undefined`. It has always returned `null` for a value `Date` can't read, since `toJSON()` gives `null` for an invalid date though its type says `string`; a caller that assigns the result to `string | undefined` must now handle `null`, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#localtoutc-can-return-null-breaking-change) shows
- Added `getRenderedOptionSchema()`, the one place that decides what an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option inherits from the schema holding it, and `rendersOwnFieldBesideOption()`, which decides whether a field's option is read as rendered or merged with the whole field. `MultiSchemaField` renders the selected option with it, `getDefaultFormState()` computes the defaults of a field that is not an object from it, and the `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` walk now decides whether that option is an Optional Data Control from the same schema, rather than from the option merged with the whole of its parent. For a field that is not an object, whose option renders nothing beside it, the walk also descends into that schema, so a `ui:required` field within the parent's `items` that an option's `items: true` leaves in place is checked, and one the parent's `false` subschema forbids is not ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359))
- `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` reads whether both schemas are arrays through `getSchemaType()`, so an array whose type list also allows `null`, such as `['array', 'null']`, keeps its items across a switch as a plain `type: 'array'` does, rather than being cleared. A property or an array's items that both schemas give the same type list, such as `['string', 'null']`, keeps its value too, where two separately declared lists used to be read as different types. A tuple, an array declaring no `items` or the same boolean `items`, that both schemas describe alike is kept up to the new schema's `maxItems` rather than cleared, an array the new schema's `const` rejects is cleared so that the `const` can take its place, as is one its `enum` of whole arrays doesn't list, where the old schema declared a different `const` or list, so a sanitize pass a conditional elsewhere in the form starts doesn't drop an array the same `const` already rejected, and a property or an array's items whose type list names `object`, such as `['object', 'null']`, is sanitized property by property when it holds an object, as a `type: 'object'` one is, keeping a `null` it allows ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359))
- `withIdRefPrefix()` has an overload per input shape, so a schema in gives a schema out without a cast, and one for the union of them, which is the signature it had; `guessType()` takes `unknown`; `retrieveSchema()` re-applies the symbols it collects from `allOf` branches with `Object.assign` instead of indexing through `any` ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- Exported the `Bivariant<Args, R>` type, a callback whose parameters are checked bivariantly, which `SlotComponent` and the `onChange` props are built from, so another package can declare such a callback without restating it ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- `getExampleSuggestions()` returns the same array for the same schema object, so a `BaseInputTemplate` hands `SchemaExamples` the suggestions it handed it last time. Every caller shares that array, so it is frozen and typed `readonly string[]`, as is the `suggestions` prop of `SchemaExamples`; copy it to sort or add to it. The suggestions are read once per schema object, so `examples` or a `default` edited in place afterwards is not seen ([#5508](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5508))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `shouldRenderOptionalField()` takes the field's `fieldPath` as a required fifth argument, and its fourth, `uiSchema`, is no longer optional. It returns `false` for the root field, `ROOT_FIELD_PATH`
- `retrieveSchema()` is up to twice as fast on a schema with no `$ref`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Removed `isRootSchema()`. It decided whether a schema was the root by deep-comparing it with the root schema, which costs two whole-schema comparisons per call and mistakes a nested field that a recursive `$ref: '#'` describes for the root. Compare the field's `fieldPath` with `ROOT_FIELD_PATH` instead
- `retrieveSchema()` keeps the cycle flag it sets on a recursive `$ref` when that flag is the only change to the subtree holding it, so a recursion reached through a nested property renders a cycle indicator where it used to be expanded again on every render, hanging the form or overflowing the stack. A dependent property that points back at its enclosing definition is flagged too, where it was left as a bare `$ref`. A recursion a property reaches through an `allOf` or a `then`/`else` of its own, as in `child: { allOf: [{ $ref: '#/definitions/node' }] }`, is not flagged, and still nests its fields until the heap runs out ([#5501](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5501))
- `getDefaultFormState()` stops at a recursion it reaches through an `if`/`then`/`else`, an `allOf` or a `dependencies` entry of the recursive definition, or through an `allOf` or `then`/`else` that is itself the `$ref` back to it, whether in a property, in array `items` or in `additionalProperties`, within a level of where it stops at a `$ref`, keeping the parent's defaults, the schema's own `default`, `ui:initialValue` and `ui:emptyValue` there. Resolving the branch used to expand the `$ref` that points back at the enclosing definition, so the defaults had no `$ref` to stop at and followed the recursion one level further on every call until the stack overflowed, which kept a form with such a schema from rendering at all. The `dependencies` case fixes [#5493](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5493). It still overflows when the keyword sits on a property or on array `items` and holds the `$ref` further in, as the property or `items` it declares, and for a `dependencies` entry of a property ([#5500](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5500))
- Added `createRootSchemaGuard()`, the check a precompiled validator runs on the schema it is asked to validate with: the guard it returns throws when that schema is neither the root schema the validator was compiled from nor its resolution for the form data, and compares a given schema object only once. A precompiled validator of your own can use it in place of a deep comparison on every validation
- `retrieveSchema()` flags a property that points back at the definition its schema was resolved from when an `allOf` entry, a `then`/`else` branch or a `dependencies` entry declares the property and an earlier call has already resolved the schema, where it expanded the property again on every such call. A field is handed its schema resolved that way once a cycle indicator above it is expanded, so expanding one on such a recursion nested fields until the heap ran out. A branch that stood beside the `$ref` rather than in the definition is not part of it, so a `$ref` to the definition in there is a second use and is expanded. `getDefaultFormState()` with `allOf: 'populateDefaults'` reads an `allOf` beside the `$ref` of a schema that is not an object the same way, so the `items` such an `allOf` declares through a `$ref` to the same definition get the defaults they get when the definition is written out in place of the `$ref`
- Fixed `mergeDefaultsWithFormData()` copying the `defaults` into the result before checking their type, so a string or array `default` merged with object `formData` surfaced as index keys (e.g. a `'x'` default merged with `{ b: 2 }` produced `{ '0': 'x', b: 2 }`). The defaults are now only copied when they are themselves an object, fixing [#5405](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5405)
- A relative `$id` nested in another resource is resolved against that resource's `$id`, as draft 2020-12 specifies, both when `createSchemaUtils()` makes a bundle's `$ref`s absolute and when `findSchemaDefinition()` looks a resource up by its `$id`, where it was read as written and a `$ref` into or inside it threw `Could not find a definition`. A `$ref` resolved through a definition is then resolved against the resource that definition sits in, including under a root without an absolute `$id`. The keywords beside a `$ref` keep the base of the schema they were written in, rebased on an `$id` beside the `$ref`, as the `$ref` itself is. An `$id` under a keyword that holds no subschemas, such as `components`, is data and starts no resource. A `$ref` that `createSchemaUtils()` would rewrite to a relative URI a validator resolves again to somewhere else, as under a root without an absolute `$id` inside `nested/child.json`, is left as written, so validating against the rewritten root keeps working. In a draft-07 or 2019-09 root, or one without `$schema`, a `#/...` `$ref` inside a definition with its own `$id` resolves in that definition's resource, where it threw `Could not find a definition`, a 2019-09 `$id` beside a `$ref` rebases it as a 2020-12 one does, and a `$ref` naming an embedded resource by its URI finds it in every draft, where only a 2020-12 root did. An embedded resource whose `$id` ends in `/`, or in an empty fragment (`root.json#`, as draft-07 schemas often write one), is found by a `$ref` to it too, fixing [#5467](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5467)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `toErrorList()` lists the errors an `ErrorSchema` holds in an array for the items of an array field, which it skipped, and `validationDataMerge()` merges such an array as the object keyed by index that `toErrorSchema()` builds, rather than replacing the other `ErrorSchema`'s errors for that array with it or appending it to another array of item errors. `unwrapErrorHandler()` strips the `addError()` functions from an array of item handlers a `customValidate` returns, which it left in the `ErrorSchema`, and keys the items by index. A `null` or `undefined` an `ErrorSchema` holds for an item or field without errors is left out of the merge rather than replacing the other `ErrorSchema`'s errors there, and out of what `unwrapErrorHandler()` returns. `unwrapErrorHandler()` leaves what sits under `__errors` as it is, as the other walks do, where it unwrapped an object there. `validationDataMerge()` therefore returns such an array as the object keyed by index, also when there is nothing to merge, where it used to pass a caller's array through, and returns `validationData` itself only when its `errorSchema` holds no such array and no `null` or `undefined`. The `ErrorSchema` and `FormValidation` types reject an array as the node of array data, which their numeric index signature accepted, so a typed `ErrorSchema` literal that holds one, such as `{ list: [{ __errors: ['taken'] }] }`, no longer compiles and has to key the items by index. The new `errorSchemaChild()` returns a value an `ErrorSchema` walk descends into, a plain object or such an array, and `undefined` for an `__errors` array or a leaf ([#3827](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3827))
- Fixed form data and schema keys named like `Object.prototype` members, such as `toString`, `constructor` and a JSON `__proto__`, being read as inherited values or written through the prototype setter. `mergeDefaultsWithFormData()`, `mergeSchemas()` and `retrieveSchema()` keep a `__proto__` property as an own key; `retrieveSchema()` no longer treats an inherited name as a declared `dependencies` key; `getDefaultFormState()` keeps the defaults of a `__proto__` schema property and an `additionalProperties` default named `constructor` or `valueOf`, and no longer turns an inherited `toString` into an own key when it matches `allOf`, or reads an inherited member of a parent `const`; `omitExtraData()` reads and writes own keys only on submit; and `getDefaultFormState()` counts a property declared as `false` as a declared property rather than an additional one, so an object `default` still seeds its other keys beside one, fixing [#5515](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5515) ([#5524](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5524))
