- Added theme presentation metadata, which `@rjsf/core` and every theme read: the `PresentationDeclaration`, `WidgetPresentationDeclaration`, `ThemePresentation`, `MergedThemePresentation` and `FieldPresentation` types, the `WIDGET_FIELD_PRESENTATION` declaration, and `mergeThemePresentation()`, `getPresentationDeclaration()`, `resolveFieldPresentation()` and `getSelectorPresentation()`, which build a field's plan of who draws its label, description, help and errors, under which ids, and the ARIA attributes its control carries. A declaration's `uncheckedIsFalse` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388)) marks a checkbox that holds `false` unchecked ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added the helpers that read a field's plan, `getControlAttributes()`, `getControlDescribedBy()`, `getDescribedControlAttributes()`, `getWidgetDescription()`, `isLabelledByTemplate()` and `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()`, and the `optionDescriptionId()`, `optionHelpId()`, `optionLabelId()` and `selectedOptionDescriptionId()` id generators ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `getFieldLabel()`, one computation of a field's label for its template and its widget, which puts the schema's `title` ahead of the `title` prop ([#5394](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5394))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `Registry` has a required `presentation`; code that builds a `Registry` by hand adds `presentation: mergeThemePresentation()`, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#registry-has-a-required-presentation-breaking-change) shows ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- The props of fields, widgets and their templates have an optional `presentation`, the field's plan, which `widgetAriaProps()` and `SelectedOptionDescription` read when given ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `getDisplayLabel()`, a widget's `hideLabel` and a `FieldTemplate`'s `displayLabel` and `rawErrors` are deprecated in favor of the field's plan; they stay through v7 ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `getGroupAttributes()`, which splits the attributes `getControlAttributes()` gives a widget rendering a group of controls, such as radios or checkboxes, into the `role` and `aria-labelledby` for the group's wrapper and the `aria-describedby` for each control in it, and puts `aria-invalid` where ARIA supports it for that `role`: on a `radiogroup` rather than its radios, and on each control of a `group`, such as its checkboxes. A widget that draws its own label for the group passes that label's id, which names the group where its plan gives the widget the label and names the group by no other ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `templateDrawsLabel()`, `templateDrawsDescription()`, `hasTemplateErrors()` and `widgetDrawsLabel()`, which read a field's plan and fall back to the deprecated `displayLabel`, `rawErrors` and `hideLabel` only for a template or widget rendered without one, so those props are read in one place each, and a template rendered without a plan draws the description only where `displayLabel` lets it draw the label, as it did; `getWidgetDescription()` and `hasAdditionalKeyLabel()` read them through these. `AdditionalPropertyKeySelect` and `SelectedOptionDescription` declare a `hideLabel` of their own rather than taking the widget's ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `getArrayDescription()`, the description an `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws: the one its plan says the array draws itself, or, without a plan, its `ui:description` or schema `description` ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `titleDrawsOptionalDataControl()`, whether an `ArrayFieldTemplate` or `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out its optional data control in the heading it draws for its `title`, rather than with its items or properties, as a readonly or disabled container does and one drawing no heading, so core's and the themes' container templates read that rule from one place ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `templateLabelFor()`, the `for` of the label a `FieldTemplate` draws: the field's `id`, except where the field's plan names the control through `aria-labelledby`, as for a group of radios or checkboxes, whose wrapper isn't an element a `for` can label ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `templateMarksRequired()`, whether a `FieldTemplate` marks the label it draws as required: where the field's plan says so, which leaves the marker off a control declared `uncheckedIsFalse` whose schema doesn't demand `true` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388)), or, for a template rendered without a plan, where the field is `required`, so core's and the themes' `FieldTemplate`s read that rule from one place ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `hasWidgetErrors()`, the widget's counterpart of `hasTemplateErrors()`: whether a widget's control has errors to show, for its error state, from the `aria-invalid` its field's plan gives the control, whoever draws the messages, falling back to the deprecated `rawErrors` with `hideError` only for a widget rendered without a plan, so an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's control is styled invalid while the field above draws its errors ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `getGroupLabelling()`, which gives a widget rendering a group of controls that draws its own label for the group whether it draws one, the id of the field's label from `getLabelId()`, which it draws it under, and the attributes `getGroupAttributes()` gives the group and each control, with the group named by that label where its field's plan names it by no other, so `@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s, `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s and `@rjsf/mui`'s radio and checkboxes widgets, which draw that label themselves, read that rule from one place ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `AdditionalPropertyKeySelect` gives its `SelectWidget` a plan of its own, which describes it by none of the ids a widget rendered without a plan falls back to, `<key id>__error`, `__description` and `__help`, which no element has, and names it by the key's label through `aria-label` where neither the select nor its template draws that label, so a key select whose template draws no key label beside it is no longer unnamed. A template that draws the key's label beside the select passes `labelledByTemplate`, so the select isn't given an `aria-label` over it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `templateDrawsPlannedLabel()`, whether a `FieldTemplate` in a theme whose widgets draw their own label draws the field's label: only where the field's plan gives the label to the template and there is label text to draw, and never for a template rendered without a plan, so `@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s, `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s, `@rjsf/mantine`'s and `@rjsf/mui`'s `FieldTemplate`s read that rule from one place ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `resolveFieldPresentation()` names a control whose label `ui:label: false` hides by the label's text, through an `aria-label` in its plan's `control.attributes`, which `getGroupAttributes()` puts on a group's wrapper and `getSelectorPresentation()` gives an `anyOf`/`oneOf` selector, so the control keeps an accessible name in every theme. An `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's control takes its field's hidden label's text, as that label names it while it shows. `widgetAriaProps()` lets a caller's `aria-label` win over it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `isNamedByTemplateLabel()`, whether a label the `FieldTemplate` draws names a widget's control, from the plan or, without one, from the label the widget is handed, and `getControlFallbackName()`, the field's label, its placeholder or a translated prompt, which a control that takes no name from what it displays, such as a combobox trigger, takes where none does, so `@rjsf/daisyui`'s and `@rjsf/shadcn`'s comboboxes read those rules from one place. `getControlAriaLabel()` gives such a control's `aria-label` from the two, and `widgetDrawsLabelText()` whether a widget draws a label of its own with text in it, so each theme's select names its trigger by one rule. `getFieldTypeForUiSchema()`, the type of the field a field's own `ui:widget` renders it by, is exported ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `DateElement` takes its field's `presentation` and hands its `SelectWidget` a plan of its own, so each date part of an `AltDateWidget` is described by the field's parts that render, rather than by `ariaDescribedByIds()`, whose ids no element may have, and is `aria-invalid` while the field's errors show, while keeping the name `dateElementAriaLabel()` gives it. `widgetAriaProps()` adds the ids a caller passes to its plan's, keeping the plan's other attributes, rather than replacing them, and replaces only the `ariaDescribedByIds()` a select rendered without a plan falls back to, which an empty `aria-describedby` from its caller now replaces too, where it used to fall back to it ([#5542](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5542), [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `enumOptionsAutofocusIndex()`, the index of the option a `RadioWidget` or `CheckboxesWidget` focuses under `ui:autofocus`: a radio group's checked option, given its value, where Tab into the group lands, else the first one `ui:enumDisabled` leaves enabled, so `@rjsf/core`'s and every theme's radio and checkboxes widgets read that rule from one place ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- Added `getLabelId()`, the id of a field's label: the one its plan gives it, whoever draws it, numbered with `optionLabelId()` beneath a label a field above draws, or `fieldLabelId()` without a plan, so a widget that draws its own label draws it under the id its plan points `aria-labelledby` at. `getGroupLabelling()`'s `labelId` is that id even where the template draws the label, rather than `fieldLabelId()`, so a widget that hands it to its own label slot points at the label the template draws ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `FieldTemplateProps.displayLabel` and `rawErrors`, `WidgetProps.hideLabel` and `rawErrors`, `getDisplayLabel()` and `SchemaUtilsType.getDisplayLabel()` are tagged `@deprecated`, kept through v7 for a template, widget or field rendered without a plan; `@rjsf/core` and every theme read them only through `templateDrawsLabel()`, `hasTemplateErrors()`, `widgetDrawsLabel()` and `hasWidgetErrors()`, or where they keep one out of the props they spread onto an element. With a plan, the plan wins: `@rjsf/core`'s and every theme's templates and widgets ignore one of these props handed alongside a `presentation`, so a custom field that hides a widget's label with `hideLabel` while passing its plan hands it a plan whose label isn't the widget's instead ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490))
- `FieldProps.rawErrors` and `ContainerFieldTemplateProps.rawErrors`, which carry a field's own errors whatever `hideError` says (a custom `ArraySchemaField` is handed the array's), and `getVisibleErrors()` and `hasVisibleErrors()`, which apply `hideError` to them, with `VisibleErrorsProps`, their parameter type, are tagged `@deprecated`, kept through v7 for a field, template or widget rendered without a plan: read `getErrorsToDraw()` for the messages a component draws itself, and `hasWidgetErrors()` and `hasTemplateErrors()` for an error state, each of which falls back to them only without one. `ContainerFieldTemplateProps.presentation`'s description now covers the container's own errors. The props are still handed over in full, for a component that renders the hidden errors its own way ([#5325](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5325))
- Added `getErrorsToDraw()`, the error messages a widget, or an `ArrayFieldTemplate` or `ObjectFieldTemplate`, draws itself: its field's plan's `presentation.errors.messages`, with `ui:hideError` applied, where `presentation.errors.by` is `'self'`, and none where the `FieldTemplate` or a field above draws them, falling back to the deprecated `rawErrors` with `hideError` only for a component rendered without a plan. `@rjsf/mantine`'s widgets and `ObjectFieldTemplate` read their messages through it ([#5325](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5325))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getDefaultFormState()` fills a missing value of a `type: 'null'` schema in as `null`, outside a rendered `anyOf`/`oneOf` option, and adds it, with the objects that hold it, whatever `emptyObjectFields` says, since it is the one value such a field can hold, also for a property an object's `allOf` or `if` brings in, or makes `null`, where the `allOf` or `emptyObjectFields` setting leaves that keyword's defaults out, for a schema `null` only through its own `allOf` or `if`, for its `default: null`, for an array item or additional property the data holds, and for a value the data holds as `undefined`, though an object the data holds as `undefined` keeps it, as one with no `null` schema in it does; not inside an optional object the `enableOptionalDataFieldForType` UI option renders without its fields. Its return type and `SchemaUtilsType.getDefaultFormState()`'s include `null`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `retrieveSchema()` parses the root schema once into a graph and resolves the schema it is given one level at a time: a `$ref` under `properties`, `items` or an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option is handed out as it stands, for the field that renders it to retrieve, where it was resolved in place. The graph is kept per root schema object, so the root is treated as immutable: a change made to it in place is not seen, even by a new `createSchemaUtils()` or by `Form` rendering it again, where the next `retrieveSchema()` read it before; pass a new schema object to change it, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#the-root-schema-is-treated-as-immutable-breaking-change) shows. A schema handed in that the root does not hold is kept once per content for as long as the root, so it must not be changed once handed in either, and one built from form data keeps an entry for each distinct value until the root changes. Every subschema it hands out carries the new `RJSF_PROVENANCE_KEY` Symbol saying where it came from, so retrieving its own output gives the same schema back, with `RJSF_REF_CYCLE_KEY` where it was, and a copy with that flag set to `false` expands by one level. A caller that reads a nested subschema out of one result retrieves it in turn, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#retrieveschema-resolves-one-level-at-a-time-breaking-change) shows
- `retrieveSchema()` terminates on every recursive schema, including the ones it overflowed the stack on ([#5452](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5452) a and b), and compares a `$ref` by the subschema it reaches rather than as written. A schema that merges itself back in through an `allOf`, a `then` or a `dependencies` entry no longer leaves a literal `$ref` on the result. `resolveUiSchema()` resolves a field's `$ref` through the same graph, an `$anchor` included, so the options of a definition an `$anchor` names get their `ui:definitions` entries, where it logged `could not resolve $ref in resolveUiSchema` and left them out. Under a draft 2019-09 or 2020-12 root, its `$schema` written with or without a trailing `#`, `retrieveSchema()`, `getDefaultFormState()` and the form resolve a `$ref` to an `$anchor`, such as `{ $ref: '#nodeA' }`, where they threw `Could not find a definition for ...`. An extra key of the data whose `additionalProperties` or `patternProperties` `$ref` names a definition with an `allOf` gets that definition's `ui:definitions` entry, as a property does, where the `allOf` merge dropped `RJSF_REF_KEY`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `resolveAllReferences()` is removed from `lib/schema/retrieveSchema.js` and `lib/refOf.js` from the package, as are the `retrieveSchema()` helpers there (see the `retrieveSchemaInternal()` entry below). `getClosestMatchingOption()` and `omitExtraData()` resolve only an option's own `$ref`, leaving the ones below it to the validator
- `getDefaultFormState()` with `allOf: 'populateDefaults'` fills an array of a recursive definition whose `minItems` sits in an `allOf` to the depth it fills the same array whose `minItems` sits beside its `items`, one level less than before
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getDefaultFormState()` computes the defaults in one pass over the same schema graph `retrieveSchema()` reads, each field merged with its `$ref`, `allOf`, `if`/`then`/`else` and `dependencies` as the form renders it, and a recursive `$ref` stopped by the rule the form uses, expanded once wherever it stops. It terminates on every recursive schema, including the ones it overflowed the stack on ([#5500](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5500), [#5452](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5452) a and c, definitions whose `dependencies` refer to one another, a recursion behind an `allOf` under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'`, which is expanded once as every other one is, a schema at the root that refers back to itself through a `then`, which fills the property it recurses through as it does under a property, a recursive `$ref` under a key a `patternProperties` pattern matches, which keeps the pattern's defaults where it stops, and chains through several cycles of definitions), and stays fast on definitions that all refer to one another. Some defaults change, each listed in the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#getdefaultformstate-folds-over-the-schema-graph-breaking-change): extra keys of the data, additional ones and those a `patternProperties` pattern matches, are filled down to where the schema stops recursing, and keys a `default` object names no longer grow on every call; a `then` gives an untyped property its default; a chain of `dependencies` is filled in one call; the keywords beside a `$ref` apply at every level, a `default` included, which now also overrides the referenced schema's in a draft-07 tuple position and in `additionalItems`, along a chain of aliases and past a `$ref` back into a recursion; an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option written as a `$ref` is merged with its field's `default` as one written in place is; a `$ref` spelled `#/definitions/%41` stops where `#/definitions/A` does, and a recursion through a chain of aliases stops at its first alias; an `allOf` behind the `$ref` of an extra key of the data follows the `allOf` defaults behavior as one behind a property's `$ref` does, so under the default `allOf: 'skipDefaults'` its defaults are no longer filled; and under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'` a `dependencies` entry inside a merged `allOf` is filled when a default qualifies it, as one beside `properties` is
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `computeDefaults()`, `getObjectDefaults()`, `getArrayDefaults()` and `getDefaultBasedOnSchemaType()` in `lib/schema/getDefaultFormState.js` no longer take a `_recurseList`: each call starts the recursion afresh at the schema it is given, so `computeDefaults()` on a recursive `$ref` expands it once, as `getDefaultFormState()` does, and a `default` beside the `$ref` it is given overrides the referenced schema's
- `getDefaultFormState()` gives `{}` where it gave `undefined` for a definition that merges a schema which merges it back in, such as an OpenAPI-style `Cat` whose `allOf` holds the `Pet` whose `then` is `Cat`, taken as the root schema with no data. It scores a field's options as the schema declares them, so a parent's `customMergeAllOf` no longer reaches the option its children choose for their defaults. Every change to schema resolution in this release is listed with its results before and after in the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#breaking-changes-schema-resolution)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getClosestMatchingOption()` scores a property written as a `$ref` as the definition it names, with the keywords beside it, as far as the form data reaches, whether or not the `$ref` is recursive, where it overflowed the stack scoring a recursive one. Each `$ref` it follows resolves in the resource of the declaration holding it, so a property declared both beside a `$ref` and in the definition it names, under an `$id` of its own, reads the `$ref` that definition writes there. `calculateIndexScore()` in `lib/schema/getClosestMatchingOption.js`, called directly, scores such a property the same way, so an object it names gets the point an object written in place gets, and it no longer merges the `allOf` of the definition a `$ref` names (see the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#deep-imports-and-precompiled-validators-breaking-change))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A `$ref` with keywords beside it reads as JSON Schema reads it, under every draft, in the form, the defaults, option scoring and `omitExtraData()`: as the `allOf` of those keywords and of the schema it names, where the keywords beside a field's own `$ref` overrode that schema keyword by keyword, and under draft-07 those beside a `$ref` another `$ref` reaches were dropped. `cat: { $ref: Pet, properties: { meow } }` renders `meow` beside `Pet`'s fields and takes `Pet`'s `required`, where it rendered `meow` alone; its defaults fill both whatever the `allOf` defaults behavior, since the keywords beside a `$ref` are no `allOf` the schema declares; and `omitExtraData()`, which the `omitExtraData`/`liveOmit` props run, keeps the fields of both. With `O = { properties: { a: { default: 'a' } } }`, `M = { $ref: O, properties: { b: { default: 'b' } } }` and `v: { $ref: M }` under draft-07, the form renders `root_v_b` beside `root_v_a` where it rendered `root_v_a` alone, and the defaults are `{ v: { b: 'b', a: 'a' } }` where they were `{ v: { a: 'a' } }`. A `$ref` beside nothing but annotations (`title`, `description`, `default`, `examples`, `readOnly`, `writeOnly`, `deprecated`, `$comment`) and identifiers (`$id`, `$anchor`, `$defs`, `definitions`, `$schema`) reads as the schema it names, the annotations beside it over its own, as before. `refDefinition()` reads the definition a chain of such `$ref`s names merged, so option scoring and the `ui:definitions` walk see the properties and options of both sides. A `customMergeAllOf` merges the two as it merges an `allOf`, in the form and wherever a function is handed the context holding it, such as option scoring, `omitExtraData()` and the defaults. See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#keywords-beside-a-ref-breaking-change)
- `getDefaultFormState()` called without a `rootSchema` resolves the schema's `$ref`s against the schema itself whatever the form data, where it did so only while the data was empty and threw `Could not find a definition for ...` once the data held anything: `{ definitions: { a: { type: 'string', default: 'x' } }, properties: { p: { $ref: '#/definitions/a' }, q: { type: 'string' } } }` with the data `{ q: '1' }` gives `{ p: 'x', q: '1' }`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `findSchemaDefinition()` is deprecated and resolves its `$ref` through the schema graph, as the form does: a `$ref` to an `$anchor` resolves where it threw, the keywords beside a `$ref` the target holds read as the `allOf` of both where draft-07 read them as overrides, the result is marked with `RJSF_REF_KEY`, and the `baseURI` parameter v7 had added is gone. It is kept for callers written against 6.x; resolve a `$ref` as the form does with `schemaUtils.retrieveSchema({ $ref })`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `createSchemaUtils()` no longer rewrites the `$ref`s of a draft 2020-12 root against its `$id`: `getRootSchema()` returns the root schema it was created with, and each `$ref` is resolved once, against the `$id`s around it, where it is looked up ([#5529](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5529)). A `$ref` the rewrite left as written, because a validator would resolve the rewritten one again to somewhere else, resolves against the resource it sits in, wherever it is looked up: under `$id: 'schemas/root.json'`, with `$defs: { x: { type: 'number' }, child: { $id: 'nested/child.json', $defs: { x: { type: 'string' } }, additionalProperties: { $ref: '#/$defs/x' } } }`, `schemaUtils.retrieveSchema()` of the `additionalProperties` that `getRootSchema()` holds gave the root's `{ type: 'number' }` and now gives the child's `{ type: 'string' }`. A `ui:definitions` entry is keyed by the `$ref` as the schema writes it: under a root whose `$id` is `https://example.com/root.json`, a field `{ $ref: '#/$defs/s' }` took the entry keyed `https://example.com/root.json#/$defs/s` and now takes the one keyed `#/$defs/s`. `makeAllReferencesAbsolute()` is removed from `lib/findSchemaDefinition.js`. See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#createschemautils-no-longer-rewrites-the-roots-refs-breaking-change)
- A `$ref` whose only other keywords are `$id`, `$anchor`, `$defs`, `definitions`, `$schema` and `$comment`, or the annotations `title`, `description`, `default`, `examples`, `deprecated`, `readOnly` and `writeOnly`, reads as the schema it names with those annotations on top where another `$ref` reaches it, rather than as the `allOf` of the two, so `getDefaultFormState()` fills its defaults as `retrieveSchema()` resolves it: with `$defs: { a: { $defs: { n: { type: 'number', default: 7 } }, $ref: '#/$defs/a/$defs/n' } }` and `properties: { l: { $ref: '#/$defs/a' } }`, the defaults were `{}` and are `{ l: 7 }` ([#5529](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5529))
- An `$id` under a keyword that holds no subschemas, such as `components`, no longer changes what a `$ref` beside it resolves against where another `$ref` reaches the schema holding it: with `$defs: { real: { $id: 'n.json', type: 'boolean' }, s: { type: 'string' } }`, `components: { Pet: { type: 'object', properties: { n: { $id: 'n.json', $ref: '#/$defs/s' } } } }` and `properties: { pet: { $ref: '#/components/Pet' } }` under a root whose `$id` is `https://e.com/root.json`, the form threw `Could not find a definition for #/$defs/s.` and now renders `root_pet_n` as a string field ([#5529](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5529))
- Added `rootSchemaIdOf()`, which returns the id a validator registers the root schema under, its `$id` less any fragment, or `ROOT_SCHEMA_PREFIX` where that leaves nothing, as for no `$id`, one such as `#root` or one that is not a string, and `withRootIdRefPrefix(schema, rootSchema)`, which prefixes a local `$ref` with that id where `withIdRefPrefix()` prefixes it with `ROOT_SCHEMA_PREFIX`, so `withRootIdRefPrefix({ $ref: '#/if' }, { $id: 'http://example.com/root.json#' })` gives `{ $ref: 'http://example.com/root.json#/if' }`, and resolves a relative `$ref`, such as `kinds.json#/definitions/cat`, against the root's `$id` where it has one, so it names the resource the root embeds rather than one beside the schema it sits in. The validators prefix the local `$ref`s of an option or a condition with the id they register the root under, which a root's `$id` names, since `retrieveSchema()` leaves them as written. A `$ref` a subschema of the root holds below an `$id` of its own, the subschema's or its resource's, is named by the pointer to the target it resolves to there, since the option or `if` a form hands the validator no longer sits under that `$id`: under a 2020-12 root, an option of a `pet.json` resource holding `kind: { $ref: '#/$defs/cat' }` is scored against `pet.json`'s `$defs` rather than the root's, as on v7, and a relative `$ref` there such as `kinds.json#/definitions/cat` resolves against the resource's `$id` rather than the root's. `withRootIdRefPrefix()` takes the id the validator registers the root under as an optional third argument, for a validator that registers it under another. A subschema `retrieveSchema()` handed out, such as a property merged from the keywords beside a `$ref` and the definition it names, has its `$ref` read from the declaration that wrote it, so a view of such an option is checked against the resource's definitions too. A relative `$ref` below an `$id` the schema handed over holds resolves against that `$id`, read against the root's, where it names no subschema of the root: under `$id: 'https://ex.com/schemas/a.json'`, `{ $ref: 'ext.json' }` names `https://ex.com/schemas/ext.json` rather than `https://ex.com/ext.json`. `@rjsf/validator-ata` and `@rjsf/validator-cfworker` register a root whose `$id` is not a string as one without an `$id`; `@rjsf/validator-ajv8` rejects such a root, as it rejects any schema whose `$id` is not a string, so its `isValid()` against that root returns `false` and `validateFormData()` reports the schema as failing to compile ([#5387](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5387))
- Added `resolveConstantOptions()`, which resolves the `$ref`s of a schema's `anyOf`/`oneOf` options when they all name constants. `retrieveSchema()` hands an option written as a `$ref` out as it stands, for the field that renders it to retrieve, except a select's, whose constants it resolves this way; `getDisplayLabel()`, `getDefaultFormState()`, `omitExtraData()` and `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` read the options of a schema they are handed through it, and a `oneOf` or `anyOf` of constants written as `$ref`s renders as a select or multi-select with their titles, one over object or array constants keeping its value whole, with its label, `default` and first constant as its defaults, through `omitExtraData` and when the schema changes. When the schema changes, an array a select over whole arrays written as `$ref`s does not offer is cleared, as one written in place is, where it was kept; an option of the old schema whose `$ref` names nothing under the new root lists no values, so the new options decide
- `getDefaultFormState()` under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'` gives a property the default of the `anyOf`/`oneOf` option an `allOf` entry chooses, over the default an earlier `allOf` entry declares, for an object nested under a property as it already did for the same object at the root, and as the form renders it: with `o: { type: 'object', allOf: [{ properties: { c: { default: 'm0' } } }, { oneOf: [{ properties: { c: { default: 'opt' } } }, { properties: { z: { type: 'number' } } }] }] }`, `{ o: { c: 'opt' } }` where it gave `{ o: { c: 'm0' } }`. In such composed shapes the option's keys can also come first in the defaults object; the values are unchanged
- `getOptionalDataControlsType()` and `isOptionalDataControlsType()` take an optional `rootSchema`, which reads an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option written as a `$ref` as the definition it names; `shouldRenderOptionalField()` passes the registry's, so a `oneOf` of `$ref`s to objects gets the Optional Data Controls its inline spelling gets, now that `retrieveSchema()` hands such options out as written
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getDefaultFormState()` computes each property's defaults from the property as the schema declares it, so under `allOf: 'skipDefaults'` a deep `customMergeAllOf` no longer fills a property's `allOf` defaults, and a root `dependencies` entry counts a default as its property's value whatever else the form data holds, as a nested one already did. See the v7 upgrade guide ([#5535](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5535))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getDefaultFormState()` under `allOf: 'skipDefaults'` skips the `allOf` of a property merged with the `patternProperties` its name matches, and of the definition a `$ref` with keywords beside it names, as it skips any other property's, where the shallow merge of the property with its patterns, or of the keywords with the definition, filled that `allOf`'s defaults: with `properties: { cx: { allOf: [{ properties: { n: { default: 'n' } } }] } }` and `patternProperties: { '^c': { title: 'C' } }`, the defaults are `{}` where they were `{ cx: { n: 'n' } }`
- Added `getOptionSelectorSchema()`, the schema of an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option's selector property as it is read: retrieved where it is written as a `$ref`, as it stands otherwise. `findSelectedOptionInXxxOf()` and `LayoutMultiSchemaField` both read the selector through it, so the option one selects is the one the other lists
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `ValidatorType.isValid()` is handed `{ $ref: '#/<pointer>' }` naming a subschema where it sits in the `rootSchema` passed beside it: each `if` a field's resolution checks, the property an option names as its discriminator, an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option whose `required` scoring has nothing to drop, the option `MultiSchemaField` keeps, and the property a dependency's `oneOf` option is matched on, inside `{ type: 'object', properties: { <key>: { $ref } } }`. A property an option declares more than once where it applies whatever the data, as `allOf: [{ $ref: Base }, { properties: { petType: { const: 'cat' } } }]` does when `Base` declares `petType` too, is handed as an `allOf` of a `$ref` to each declaration. With `{ properties: { a: { type: 'string' } }, if: { properties: { a: { const: 'x' } }, required: ['a'] }, then: { ... } }`, `retrieveSchema()` calls `isValid({ $ref: '#/if' }, data, rootSchema)` where it called `isValid({ properties: { a: { const: 'x' } }, required: ['a'] }, data, rootSchema)`. A `ValidatorType` of your own has to resolve such a `$ref` against the `rootSchema`, as the three RJSF validators do; one that ignores `rootSchema` treats every condition and option as unresolvable (see the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getFirstMatchingOption()` and `getClosestMatchingOption()` match an option as JSON Schema reads it, through a `$ref` to it in the root, and check in JavaScript that the data holds a key the option declares: a key its `properties` or `patternProperties` name, or those of its `$ref` target, its `allOf` entries, its `then`/`else` and its `dependencies` schemas. Where something in that scope declares `properties`, scoring drops every `required` in the scope, and every `dependencies` entry listing names, where it dropped only the option's own top-level `required`, and only when the option itself declared `properties`. An option whose scope declares no `properties`, such as `{ title: 'By email', required: ['email'] }`, is still matched with its `required`, as before: with `properties: { email, phone }`, the options `[{ required: ['email'] }, { required: ['phone'] }]` and the data `{ phone: '1' }`, `getFirstMatchingOption()` gives `1`. An option written as a `$ref`, or composed with `allOf`, is matched the way one written out in place is: with `A = { properties: { a: { type: 'number' } } }`, `B = { properties: { b: { type: 'number' } } }`, the options `[{ $ref: A }, { $ref: B }]` and the data `{ b: 1 }`, `getFirstMatchingOption()` gives `1` where it gave `0`; with `Base = { properties: { id }, required: ['id'] }`, the options `[{ properties: { kind: { const: 'y' } } }, { allOf: [{ $ref: Base }], properties: { kind: { const: 'x' } } }]` and the data `{ kind: 'x' }`, it gives `1` where it gave `0`. Where several options match, or none does, `getClosestMatchingOption()` scores each with the properties of its `allOf` entries and its `$ref` target too, each definition counted once however many entries name it, so options sharing a base are told apart by the properties each adds: with `Pet = { properties: { name }, required: ['name'] }`, the options `[{ $ref: Pet, properties: { meows }, required: ['meows'] }, { $ref: Pet, properties: { barks }, required: ['barks'] }]` and the data `{ name: 'x', barks: true }`, it gives `1`. An `allOf` adds nothing where one of its entries reaches a `$ref` that resolves to nothing or loops, through its `$ref`, its `allOf` entries or its properties, which leaves the form nothing to merge, so the option scores what it declares beside the `allOf`, as v7 scores every option: with `ok = { allOf: [{ $ref: '#/definitions/missing' }], properties: { b: { type: 'number' } } }`, the options `[{ properties: { a: { type: 'string' } } }, { allOf: [{ $ref: ok }, { properties: { b: { type: 'number' } } }] }]` and the data `{ b: 1 }`, both options score nothing, so it gives the first, `0`, `omitExtraData()` drops `b`, and `getDefaultFormState()` keeps it without throwing. With a `discriminatorField`, the discriminator property alone decides only where one of its declarations can reject the data's value: data that holds no value for it yet, or declarations that all accept the value, such as a base's `{ type: 'string' }` with no `const` or `enum`, leave the option to be matched as a whole. An option is no longer matched against a copy with the data's extra keys stubbed into its `properties`, which made a precompiled form throw ([#5454](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5454), [#5455](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5455))
- Added `getValidationSchemas()`, which returns every schema a form hands its validator for a root schema, keyed by `schemaKey()`: the root, a `$ref` to each `if` and option and to the property each option declares under any `discriminator.propertyName` in the root, so a discriminator declared beside a `$ref` to the options or in another `allOf` entry is covered, the copy of an option scoring derives, the condition each `oneOf` option of a `dependencies` schema is matched on, and, where an option is a boolean, `{}`, the schema `SchemaField` retrieves it as, with `{ not: {} }`, the schema a `false` option reads as. The list is read off the schema graph at each place the root holds a subschema the form checks, so it depends on the content of the root alone, whichever objects the root shares, and grows with its size: code compiled from a schema module that reuses one object serves an equal copy of it, such as its JSON, and the other way round, where `schemaParser()` enumerated the forms a schema resolves to with every combination of its conditions, dependencies and `patternProperties`. It terminates on a `oneOf` of a `$ref` to the root beside `dependencies`, which overflowed the stack ([#5452](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5452) c), and covers the schema a form validates before an `if` without an `else` holds ([#5453](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5453))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getValidationSchemas()` replaces `schemaParser()`, and its list no longer depends on how `allOf` is merged. Its keys and contents change, so precompiled validator code generated by an earlier version must be regenerated, or it throws `No precompiled validator function was found`. A schema a merge makes has no place in the root and is not listed, as it was not in 6.11.0: an option a `customMergeAllOf` rewrites, or one made by merging the `oneOf`s of two `patternProperties` that match the same key, throws that error with a precompiled validator
- Added `getValidationTarget()`, which returns the `{ $ref }` naming where a schema sits in the root, the schema `retrieveSchema()` handed out for it included, or, for a schema with no place there, the schema itself, a `$id` it carries replaced by `<that $id>?rjsf=<its own hash>`.
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `lib/schema/retrieveSchema.js` no longer exports `retrieveSchemaInternal()`, `resolveSchema()`, `resolveReference()`, `resolveCondition()`, `resolveAnyOrOneOfSchemas()`, `resolveDependencies()`, `processDependencies()`, `withDependentProperties()`, `withDependentSchema()`, `withExactlyOneSubschema()` or `getAllPermutationsOfXxxOf()`, which only expanded every branch for `schemaParser()`; `lib/combinationsOf.js` and `lib/parser/ParserValidator.js` are removed, and so is `JUNK_OPTION` from `lib/schema/getClosestMatchingOption.js`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** A `dependencies` schema's `oneOf` option is applied when the property the dependency names fits what the option declares for it in its own `properties`, or in those of its `$ref` target, its `allOf` entries, its `then`/`else` and its `dependencies` schemas, the scope option scoring reads, where only its own `properties` or its `$ref` target's counted. With `properties: { k: { enum: ['a', 'b'] } }` and `dependencies: { k: { oneOf: [{ allOf: [{ properties: { k: { enum: ['a'] } } }], properties: { ka: { default: 'A' } } }, { allOf: [{ properties: { k: { enum: ['b'] } } }], properties: { kb: { default: 'B' } } }] } }`, the data `{ k: 'b' }` gets the defaults `{ k: 'b', kb: 'B' }` and the form renders `kb`, where the defaults were `{ k: 'b' }` and the form warned `ignoring oneOf in dependencies`. The same reading can make a second option apply, and then neither does: with `oneOf: [{ allOf: [{ properties: { k: { enum: ['a', 'b'] } } }], properties: { ka: { default: 'A' } } }, { properties: { k: { enum: ['b'] }, kb: { default: 'B' } } }]`, the data `{ k: 'b' }` gets the defaults `{ k: 'b' }`, the form renders `k` alone and warns `ignoring oneOf in dependencies`, where the first option was skipped, the defaults were `{ k: 'b', kb: 'B' }` and the form rendered `kb` (see the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getOptionMatchingSimpleDiscriminator()` is removed. Option matching no longer called it, and it read only an option's own `properties`, so with the options `[{ $ref: '#/definitions/A' }, { $ref: '#/definitions/B' }]`, whose definitions give `kind` the `const`s `'a'` and `'b'`, the data `{ kind: 'b' }` and the discriminator `kind`, it returned `undefined`. Use `getFirstMatchingOption()` or `getClosestMatchingOption()` with the `discriminatorField`, which read the discriminator property through the option's `$ref`, its `allOf` entries and the rest of its scope and return `1` there
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `JUNK_OPTION_ID` is removed: option scoring no longer validates a junk option, and nothing read it
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `relaxOptionsForScoring()` is removed. Option scoring no longer called it: `omitExtraData()` relaxes the whole scope of an option itself, from where the option sits in the root. To score options with their `additionalProperties: false` relaxed yourself, hand `getClosestMatchingOption()` or `getFirstMatchingOption()` copies of them with `additionalProperties: true`
- `schemaParser()` is deprecated: it is `getValidationSchemas(rootSchema)`, which lists the schemas a form hands its validator, under the name 6.x exported it as, so it returns the new list and code calling it still has to regenerate its precompiled validators. Call `getValidationSchemas()` in its place. **BREAKING CHANGE:** `lib/parser/schemaParser.js` exports it by name only, where 6.x exported it as that module's default, so `import schemaParser from '@rjsf/utils/lib/parser/schemaParser.js'` becomes `import { getValidationSchemas } from '@rjsf/utils'`
- An `if` below a subschema carrying an `$id` of its own is checked against that resource, so a `$ref` inside the `if` reaches the resource's definitions. It was handed to the validator detached from where it sits, its `$ref`s read against the root's base: with `n: { $id: 'nested.json', definitions: { kind: { const: 'nested' } }, if: { properties: { kind: { $ref: '#/definitions/kind' } } }, then, else }` and the data `{ n: { kind: 'nested' } }`, the `else` applied
- Added `resolveUri()` and `normalizeUri()`, which resolve a URI-reference against a base URI and normalize a URI for comparison as the schema graph reads a `$ref` and an `$id`, so a validator package can tell which resource of the root a `$ref` names as the form does, and `keywordShape()`, which says which keywords of a schema hold subschemas, and in what shape, as the schema graph walks them
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getClosestMatchingOption()` returns an option the data matches whenever one does, and one of the options whenever there are any: a tie goes to the `selectedOption` only where it is among the candidates, and to the first candidate otherwise, where the `selectedOption`, `-1` or an index past the options was returned. A property holding an `anyOf`/`oneOf` of its own scores what the option picked among those scores, plus one where the data matches it, where that option's index was added to the score. A field whose first option is `{ type: 'null' }` and whose data several other options match opens on one of those rather than on `null`. The pick is remembered per validator, option list and data object, so a recursion through option lists matches each level of the data once, and the number of validations grows linearly with the depth of the data rather than by the number of options per level ([#4990](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4990))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `retrieveSchema()` takes the index of the selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option as a new last argument, `selectedOption`, and with it stubs the extra keys of the data once for the schema and that option together: a key the option declares is left to it, and the option's `additionalProperties`, `unevaluatedProperties` and `patternProperties` join the schema's own, `allOf`ed where both describe a key and `false` where either forbids it. Without it, the extra keys are stubbed for the schema alone, a key an option declares included, as on 6.11.0: the prediction of the names an option renders described above, and the reading of an option's `$ref`, `allOf`, condition and `dependencies` it took, are removed ([#5461](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5461), [#5462](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5462)). The `anyOf`/`oneOf` options it hands out for an object field are not stubbed, while a field of any other type, which renders no field of its own beside the option, leaves the option to stub its own extra keys; `getRenderedOptionSchema()` renders the option of an object without its `additionalProperties`, `patternProperties` and `unevaluatedProperties`, which the object's field stubs with it. `SchemaUtilsType.retrieveSchema()` takes the same argument
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getAdditionalPropertyType()` no longer takes a `rootSchema` and reads the schema it is given as it stands, its options and `allOf` entries included, without following a `$ref`. Resolve the subschema with `retrieveSchema()` and `resolveAnyOfOrOneOfRefs` first, as `retrieveSchema()` and `ObjectField` now do, so options spelled as `$ref`s agree on a type as before
- Added `expandRefCycle()`, which returns the expansion of a field `retrieveSchema()` flagged with `RJSF_REF_CYCLE_KEY`: the schema to retrieve it from one level further, its `$ref` path restarted. **BREAKING CHANGE:** `retrieveSchema()` no longer reads a copy carrying `RJSF_REF_CYCLE_KEY: false` as an expansion; such a copy is resolved like any other copy of the field it was made from
- `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` and `omitExtraData()`, and the `SchemaUtilsType` methods of the same names, take an optional `fieldState`, a `FormFieldState`: the option each field shows and whether the user expanded a collapsed `$ref` cycle, by field path. With it, the `ui:required` walk follows the option shown and descends into an expanded cycle, and omitting keeps the data the shown option describes. Without it, a field's option is the one its data matches best and nothing below a collapsed cycle is walked, as before. Omitting keeps its two policies: an optional object whose filtered content is all empty is pruned, and an `anyOf` over empty data applies every branch, both as on 6.11.0
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` and `SchemaUtilsType.sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` take `newSchema`, `oldSchema` and `data` alone, as on 6.11.0: the `oldData`, `resolvedOldSchema` and `oldDataForPairing` parameters v7 added for `Form`'s settled snapshot are removed, and the `enum` filter always runs. `Form` decides which fields to judge from its field tree instead. An object property whose data holds no object is sanitized to an empty object, or left out where its schema declares no `properties`, where it used to be sanitized as an empty object, filling the new defaults of its own properties and descending into their object properties in turn, which never ended behind a recursive `$ref`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `schemaHasNestedConditional()` is removed. `Form` sanitizes as a diff of the field trees before and after a change, which reaches a `dependencies` or `if` at any depth, so it no longer asks whether a schema nests one
- Added `constrainsValue()`, which says whether a schema keyword constrains the value the schema describes, directly or by way of other subschemas, as opposed to `type`, an annotation, an identifier, a container such as `$defs`, a `discriminator` or an `x-` extension. `Form` reads it to tell whether an edit to one property of an object can be a choice between the object's `anyOf`/`oneOf` options
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `ValidatorType.isValid()` must not change the data it is handed, at any depth, since `getClosestMatchingOption()` reuses a pick it made for some data during the same call without comparing the data again. A custom validator whose engine fills in defaults, coerces values or removes properties as it validates has to hand it a copy of the data in `isValid()`, as `@rjsf/validator-ajv8` now does under Ajv's `useDefaults`, `coerceTypes` and `removeAdditional` options and `@rjsf/validator-ata` already did (see the v7 upgrade guide)
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getUiOptions()` leaves a `required` in its `globalOptions` out, and no longer returns the root uiSchema's `ui:definitions` and `ui:globalOptions` as the `definitions` and `globalOptions` options, so no widget's `options` carry them: `ui:globalOptions.required` reached a boolean field's widget, an array field's select, checkboxes or file widget and the `anyOf`/`oneOf` selector as `options.required`, though neither the required marker nor validation reads a form-wide `required` ([#5527](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5527), items 1 and 2). See [the upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#getuioptions-returns-a-fields-options-alone-breaking-change)
- Added `labelPlacement` to a `PresentationDeclaration`, where a label the component draws itself sits: `'above'` its control, as a theme's text input draws its label over the input, or `'beside'` it, as a checkbox draws its own. `mergeThemePresentation()` layers it as it layers a part, so a theme's `default` reaches its widgets unless an entry declares otherwise, and `getPresentationDeclaration()` returns the one of the declaration that settled the label on `'self'`, the widget's when the field leaves its label to one, or else the field's, and leaves it out where nothing declares it or the template draws the label. `@rjsf/core` reads it only for an array item's `displayLabel`
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `useAltDateWidgetProps()` returns `buttonsDisabled`, true when the widget is disabled or read-only, when its `handleClear` and `handleSetNow` do nothing, so `@rjsf/core`'s and every theme's `AltDateWidget` disable their Now and Clear buttons from it rather than each repeating `disabled || readonly`. It is a required member of `UseAltDateWidgetResult`, so a hand-built one, such as a mocked return of the hook, has to include it ([#5378](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5378))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `getDateElementProps()`, and so `useAltDateWidgetProps()`'s `elements`, give an unset date part, which a `DateObject` holds as `-1`, an `undefined` `value`, and `DateElement` hands its `SelectWidget` `undefined` for a `-1` it is given. So `@rjsf/antd`'s, `@rjsf/daisyui`'s, `@rjsf/mui`'s, `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s and `@rjsf/shadcn`'s parts report it to `onFocus` and `onBlur` as `undefined` rather than `-1`, as `@rjsf/core`'s, `@rjsf/react-bootstrap`'s and `@rjsf/mantine`'s already did, and a custom `SelectWidget`, or a custom `AltDateWidget` that renders its own controls from `elements`, is handed `undefined` for it ([#5378](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5378))
- Exported `setOwn()`, which writes a key as an own property, `__proto__` included, without reaching the prototype setter, so `@rjsf/core`'s form state uses it rather than a copy of its own ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** Fixed more keys named like `Object.prototype` members being read as inherited values or written through the prototype setter. `setByPath()` writes a `__proto__` segment as an own key, where it dropped the whole write, so `ErrorSchemaBuilder` and `toErrorSchema()`, which write through it, file an error at a `__proto__` path under that key rather than dropping it, and an error a `transformErrors` points at `.__proto__` reaches that field, since the validators build their `errorSchema` with `toErrorSchema()` after it runs; `getPropertySchema()` returns `{}` for an undeclared property such as `toString` rather than the inherited function; `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` writes a `__proto__` key as an own key, so a value it sets or sanitizes under a `__proto__` property is kept rather than lost; and `optionsList()` reads a selector property by own key, so an option that doesn't declare one named like an `Object.prototype` member is read by its constant rather than through the inherited function; `getOptionSelectorSchema()` returns `undefined` for a selector named `__proto__` that the option doesn't declare, rather than `Object.prototype`; and `orderProperties()` keeps a property named `__proto__`, which it left out of a `ui:order` wildcard or let a `ui:order` without one leave unlisted, and drops an undeclared `toString` the order names rather than rendering it, fixing [#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561). A value now kept under a declared `__proto__` property fails validation where the schema sets `additionalProperties: false`, since AJV doesn't count `__proto__` among the declared properties, as a `default` there already did; and code that copies what `setByPath()` returns with `Object.assign()` or a recursive merge of its own reaches the prototype setter, as the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#changes-to-existing-utility-functions) says
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `GlobalUISchemaOptions` holds only the twelve options RJSF reads from `ui:globalOptions`, exported as `GlobalUISchemaOptionsKeys`, rather than any key, so a `ui:globalOptions` literal with another key no longer compiles. Its new `UserExtensions` parameter adds the keys a user's own components read, which `GlobalUISchemaOptionsExtensions` rejects where they reuse an option name RJSF reads for a field, the new `ReservedUiOptionName`, which includes the ones its widgets read without `ui:options` declaring them, such as `format` and `yearsRange`, or declare an index signature, and `ThemeGlobalUISchemaOptions` adds a theme's keys and a user's on top of them. A per-field `ui:` option stays as open as before ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607)). See the [v7 upgrade guide](https://rjsf-team.github.io/react-jsonschema-form/docs/migration-guides/v7.x%20upgrade%20guide#uiglobaloptions-hold-only-the-options-rjsf-reads-there-breaking-change)
- The new `pickGlobalUiOptions()` keeps from a root uiSchema's `ui:globalOptions` only the keys the new `GLOBAL_UI_OPTIONS_KEYS` lists and the extension keys a form declares, dropping with a warning one of the new `RESERVED_UI_OPTION_NAMES`, and warning once about each of those per-field names `ui:globalOptions` holds, such as a global `readonly`, which no longer applies; the new `getGlobalUiOptions()` returns the registry's `globalUiOptions` typed with a theme's or custom component's extensions ([#5607](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5607))
- Fixed `getDefaultFormState()` under `nestedDefaultsPrecedence: 'ancestorWins'` giving a property its own `default` where an ancestor's default holds a falsy value for it, such as `0`, `false`, `''`, or `null` for a property whose type allows it: an ancestor's falsy value wins as a truthy one did ([#5624](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5624))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `mergeDefaultsWithFormData()` applies `overrideFormDataWithDefaults` and `defaultSupercedesUndefined` at every depth, where a key whose default and form data were both objects with no object or array inside the default was shallow-spread, letting the form data win one level down whatever the flags said. So `getDefaultFormState()` replaces an `enum` value its options no longer offer with the field's default inside an object, as it did at the top. To keep that from replacing values the defaults are computed from, a field whose object data is its value as a whole, such as a select over object constants or a `type: 'string'` select whose `enum` holds objects, keeps that value where its options offer it and takes its default otherwise, the properties of an object with an `allOf` keep the defaults they were merged with, such as an `if`/`then` `const`, and the entries of a tuple are computed from their own data rather than from an empty object ([#5523](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5523))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `ensureFormDataMatchingSchema()`, and so `getDefaultFormState()`, drop object form data held by a schema whose types leave out `object`, such as a `{ type: 'string', const: 'x' }`, a `type: 'array'` or a `['number', 'string']`, at the top and at any depth inside object data, so the field takes its defaults as one the data lacks does, where the object was kept although it could never validate. Object data is a plain object with no primitive value of its own, so a `Date`, or an object whose `valueOf()` gives an epoch as a moment object's does, is kept for a date field; a schema naming no type of its own, or a `type` list naming `object`, keeps object data as before. Inside object data, a select over object constants none of which is the data takes its default too, rather than keeping the data or blending it with that default, as at the root, where a `type: 'string'` select whose `enum` holds objects did the same ([#5522](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5522))

## @rjsf/validator-ajv8

- Imports the `Ajv` class by name and reaches `ajv-formats` and `ajv/dist/standalone` through `.default`, which is where Node exposes those CommonJS modules' default export to ESM importers. No runtime change; the previous default-import spelling only typechecked under bundler resolution ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Upgraded `ajv-formats` from 2.x to 3.x. The `time` format now requires a timezone offset (`Z` or `±HH:MM`) per RFC 3339 instead of treating it as optional, so previously-valid `formData` without an offset will fail validation (`date-time` already required an offset in 2.x, so it's unaffected). The new `iso-time`/`iso-date-time` formats keep accepting offset-less values and are mapped to `TimeWidget`/`DateTimeWidget` like `time`/`date-time` are, so a field using them keeps its native time/date-time picker ([#3930](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3930))
- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `customizeValidator()` and `createPrecompiledValidator()` lose their `T` parameter along with `ValidatorType`: they are `customizeValidator<S, F>()` and `createPrecompiledValidator<S, F>()`, and the default export, which no longer carries a `T`, fits every `Form`, typed or not. The first type argument is now the schema type, so drop a leftover data type argument: `customizeValidator<MyData>()` / `ValidatorType<MyData>` fails at the declaration when `MyData` is not assignable to `JSONSchema7`, and otherwise compiles with the schema typed as `MyData`, which may show up only as an error on the `Form`'s `schema` prop, or not at all. See the validator section of the v7 upgrade guide
- `isValid()` logs a schema compilation error through `logOnce()`, so the same error isn't repeated on every re-render. The message names the schema by its `$id`, or by the hash `isValid()` already computes when it has none, and says whether the schema itself failed or the form data did, so two failures that read alike are reported separately ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- `validateFormData()` takes an optional trailing `getCustomValidateFormData`, returning the data to hand `customValidate` with the form's defaults applied; `Form` supplies it so those defaults honor its `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior`. The precompiled validator takes the `customMergeAllOf` at construction, from `createPrecompiledValidator()`, and resolves the root schema it is handed with it ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `createPrecompiledValidator()` takes its `localizer`, `suppressDuplicateFiltering` and `customMergeAllOf` in one options object after the `rootSchema`, rather than as three trailing positional parameters, so a call supplying only the merge no longer has to pass two `undefined`s. ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- Fixed `validateFormData()` and `rawValidation()` throwing for a schema whose `$id` is not a string, such as `null`, `5`, `true` or `{}`, which ajv rejects: they now report that as a validation error, as for any other schema that fails to compile ([#5387](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5387))
- `CompiledValidateFunction` drops its redundant `this: Ajv | any` parameter and keeps `(data: any): boolean`, preserving support for hand-written validators with a specific data type ([#5379](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5379))
- Fixed a schema whose `$id` is the empty string being keyed under that `$id`: two such schemas shared one compiled function, and every `$ref` into a root carrying one failed to compile. Both are keyed as a schema with no `$id` is ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338))
- The `errors` a validation returns keep an error whose `message` a `transformErrors` cleared whenever a `customValidate` is passed, as they do without one, since `validationDataMerge()` no longer drops the existing errors when their `errorSchema` is empty. A form whose `transformErrors` clears every `message` therefore reports those errors through `onError` instead of submitting when it also has a `customValidate`, as it already did without one ([#5034](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5034))
- `CompiledValidateFunction` takes `unknown` data instead of `any`. Its call signature is the `Bivariant` type of `@rjsf/utils`, so a hand-written validator that declares a narrower `data` parameter still satisfies `ValidatorFunctions`, and it is still an interface ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- A precompiled validator checks a schema against the one it was compiled from once per schema object rather than on every validation, which matters for a form passed an equal copy of the compiled schema and for live validation, which validates against the form's resolved schema. A schema object accepted once is accepted again without comparing, including when it was changed since or, for a resolved schema, when the form data it is validated with is not the data it was resolved for, so a caller of `validateFormData()` that resolves the root schema itself passes the resolution for the data being validated, as `Form` does
- `validateFormData()` lists the errors of a `customValidate` that returns an array for the items of an array field and keys them by index in the `errorSchema`, where it left them out of `errors` and let that array replace the validator's own errors for the array field. See the `@rjsf/utils` entry for `validationDataMerge()` and `unwrapErrorHandler()` ([#3827](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3827))
- `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` names the root schema in the root-local `$ref`s of every other schema it compiles, as the runtime validator does with `withRootIdRefPrefix()`, leaving out the empty fragment the root's `$id` may end in, since `retrieveSchema()` leaves a `$ref` below the schema it resolves in place and a schema compiled under an `$id` of its own would otherwise resolve it against itself
- `isValid()` registers a root schema its Ajv dialect rejects without checking it against the meta-schema, so a `$ref` into it resolves from the first call on: with the default draft-07 validator, an `if: { $ref: '#/$defs/I' }` under a 2020-12 root picks `then` for data that matches `I` on a fresh validator too, where the first call returned `false` and gave the `else` defaults, and only later calls with the same root validated. A root the dialect cannot compile, such as a draft-07 tuple root under `Ajv2020`, no longer turns every later check of the validator, under any other root, to `false`; a condition or option the form checks by a `$ref` into such a root is compiled as part of it, so it fails as validating that root does, as it did in 6.11.0. To validate a 2019-09 or 2020-12 root under its own dialect, pass `AjvClass: Ajv2019` or `Ajv2020` to `customizeValidator()`
- `isValid()` resolves a `$ref` local to the root, such as the `{ $ref: '#/if' }` a form checks a condition with, against the root's own `$id` when it has one, less the empty fragment an `$id` such as `http://example.com/root.json#` ends in, where it named the root by `ROOT_SCHEMA_PREFIX`, under which a root carrying an `$id` is not registered. With `{ $id: 'http://example.com/root.json', definitions: { I: { properties: { k: { const: 'x' } } } }, properties: { k: { default: 'x' } }, if: { $ref: '#/definitions/I' }, then: { properties: { t: { default: 't' } } }, else: { properties: { e: { default: 'e' } } } }`, the defaults are `{ k: 'x', t: 't' }` where they were `{ k: 'x', e: 'e' }`
- Fixed `isValid()` answering a schema with the function it compiled for an earlier root: the same `{ $ref: '#/definitions/X' }` validated `'s'` as valid against a root whose `X` is a `number` after a root whose `X` is a `string`. Replacing the root now drops the schemas compiled against the one it replaces
- `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` exports a `$ref` into the root as the function Ajv compiles for that subschema of the root, and compiles each other schema `getValidationSchemas()` lists under its key, so a recursive definition used as an option compiles, where it threw `can't resolve reference #/definitions/node`. A precompiled validator no longer answers the junk option `getClosestMatchingOption()` stopped using, and the error for a schema it has no function for names the subschema a `$ref` points at
- `isValid()` registers a root whose `$id` ends in an empty fragment, such as `http://example.com/root.json#`, under that `$id` without the `#`, which is how Ajv keys it, so a root edited under the same `$id` replaces the one before it. A validator that had checked a condition against such a root then answered every schema for the edited root `false`, logging `schema with key or id "http://example.com/root.json" already exists`: the edited root `{ $id: 'http://example.com/root.json#', title: 'edited', type: 'object', properties: { k: { type: 'string', default: 'x' } }, if: { properties: { k: { const: 'x' } } }, then: { properties: { t: { type: 'string', default: 't' } } }, else: { properties: { e: { type: 'string', default: 'e' } } } }` gave the defaults `{ k: 'x', e: 'e' }` and gives `{ k: 'x', t: 't' }`
- `isValid()` validates a copy of the data under Ajv's `useDefaults`, `coerceTypes` or `removeAdditional` options, or with a keyword registered with `modifying: true`, so it no longer writes defaults or coerced values into the data it is handed or deletes properties from it, as `ValidatorType.isValid()` now requires (see the `@rjsf/utils` entry); `validateFormData()` and `rawValidation()` still validate the data itself. A precompiled validator does the same for each function `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` compiled under one of those options, which the generated code marks with the new optional `CompiledValidateFunction.changesData`. Arrays and plain objects are copied key by key and any other object with `structuredClone()`, so a class instance is checked as a plain object of its own properties, and data holding an object that can't be copied, such as a class instance holding a function, makes `isValid()` return `false` with a warning, or throw for a precompiled validator. Under the other options nothing is copied, nor for a keyword that writes into the data through the code it generates, such as ajv-keywords' `transform`, which declares no `modifying`
- A precompiled validator that finds no function for a schema names it in the error beside the lookup key: by the place in the root a `$ref` pointer it was handed points at, or by the `$id` of the subschema the first `allOf` entry holds ([#5528](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5528))

## @rjsf/validator-ata

- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `customizeValidator()` and `createPrecompiledValidator()` lose their `T` parameter along with `ValidatorType`, as does the exported `ATAValidator` class (now `ATAValidator<S, F>`): they are `customizeValidator<S, F>()` and `createPrecompiledValidator<S, F>()`, and the default export, which no longer carries a `T`, fits every `Form`, typed or not. The first type argument is now the schema type, so drop a leftover data type argument: `customizeValidator<MyData>()` / `ValidatorType<MyData>` fails at the declaration when `MyData` is not assignable to `JSONSchema7`, and otherwise compiles with the schema typed as `MyData`, which may show up only as an error on the `Form`'s `schema` prop, or not at all. See the validator section of the v7 upgrade guide
- `isValid()` logs an error it catches through `logOnce()`, so the same error is not repeated on every re-render. The message names the schema by its `$id`, or by the hash `isValid()` already computes when it has none, so two schemas that fail with the same error text are reported separately. It does not say whether the schema or the form data is at fault, because ata does not check the whole schema when the validator is built: a schema defect such as an unparseable `pattern` throws while validating the data, where claiming the data was at fault would send you to debug the wrong thing ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- `validateFormData()` takes an optional trailing `getCustomValidateFormData`, returning the data to hand `customValidate` with the form's defaults applied; `Form` supplies it so those defaults honor its `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior`. The precompiled validator takes the `customMergeAllOf` at construction, from `createPrecompiledValidator()`, and resolves the root schema it is handed with it ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** `createPrecompiledValidator()` takes its `localizer`, `suppressDuplicateFiltering` and `customMergeAllOf` in one options object after the `rootSchema`, rather than as three trailing positional parameters, so a call supplying only the merge no longer has to pass two `undefined`s. ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- `isValid()` and `rawValidation()` key a schema whose `$id` is the empty string by its hash, so two such schemas no longer evict each other from the cache they share, and a root carrying one is registered where `withIdRefPrefix()` rewrites local `$ref`s to resolve ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338))
- The `errors` a validation returns keep an error whose `message` a `transformErrors` cleared whenever a `customValidate` is passed, as they do without one, since `validationDataMerge()` no longer drops the existing errors when their `errorSchema` is empty. A form whose `transformErrors` clears every `message` therefore reports those errors through `onError` instead of submitting when it also has a `customValidate`, as it already did without one ([#5034](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5034))
- `CompiledValidateFunction`'s call signature is the `Bivariant` type of `@rjsf/utils`, as `@rjsf/validator-ajv8`'s is, so a hand-written validator that declares a narrower `data` parameter satisfies `ValidatorFunctions` ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- A precompiled validator checks a schema against the one it was compiled from once per schema object rather than on every validation, which matters for a form passed an equal copy of the compiled schema and for live validation, which validates against the form's resolved schema. A schema object accepted once is accepted again without comparing, including when it was changed since or, for a resolved schema, when the form data it is validated with is not the data it was resolved for, so a caller of `validateFormData()` that resolves the root schema itself passes the resolution for the data being validated, as `Form` does
- `validateFormData()` lists the errors of a `customValidate` that returns an array for the items of an array field and keys them by index in the `errorSchema`, where it left them out of `errors` and let that array replace the validator's own errors for the array field. See the `@rjsf/utils` entry for `validationDataMerge()` and `unwrapErrorHandler()` ([#3827](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3827))
- `isValid()` resolves a `$ref` local to the root against the root's own `$id` when it has one, less the empty fragment it may end in, and builds every validator again when the root changes, so a schema such as `{ $ref: '#/definitions/X' }` is never answered by a validator built beside an earlier root
- `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` compiles each schema as a document carrying what it reaches of the root, since `ata-validator` compiles each schema as a document of its own and resolves a `$ref` only to an entry of the `definitions` or `$defs` in it: every subschema of the root it names through a `$ref`, such as a definition, a property, an option or the root itself, as an entry of its own in one container, the `$ref` renamed to it, so a root holding both `definitions` and `$defs`, or a definition whose name a `$ref` percent-encodes, compiles too. A `$ref` beside other keywords is compiled as an `allOf` entry beside them, as 2019-09, 2020-12 and Ajv read it, since `ata-validator` compiles no `$ref` beside keywords that assert something. A property that refers back to itself, `tree: { type: 'object', properties: { name: { type: 'string' }, kids: { type: 'array', items: { $ref: '#/properties/tree' } } } }`, validates, reporting `.tree.kids.0.kids.0.name` for `{ tree: { kids: [{ kids: [{ name: 5 }] }] } }`, where compiling it overflowed the stack. A `$ref` names a subschema of the root by any spelling of the URI of the resource holding it, relative, with its host in another case, through dot segments or percent-encoding an unreserved character, and a `$ref` from the root into a resource nested in it by that resource's URI resolves too. A `$ref` that names nothing in the root, or one into another document, leaves `ata-validator` nothing to compile, which throws `was not compiled to a standalone validator` when the form validates against it
- `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` compiles a schema holding only annotations, such as the root `{ title: 'Anything' }`, which `ata-validator` compiles no function for, by adding `not: { not: {} }`, which always holds. A precompiled validator for that root threw `@rjsf/validator-ata: schema "-4a64df0a" was not compiled to a standalone validator` on `isValid()` and now returns `true`
- `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` compiles an option that carries an `$id` of its own, such as `{ $id: 'myOption', type: 'object', additionalProperties: { type: 'string' } }`, which a precompiled validator threw `was not compiled to a standalone validator` for when a form scored it: `ata-validator` compiles nothing for a document holding a subschema with an `$id` of its own, so the root and the copy of a subschema a document carries go without those `$id`s, and a `$ref` below such an option is read against the option, as JSON Schema reads it, rather than against the root ([#5455](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5455))
- A precompiled validator that finds no function for a schema names it in the error beside the lookup key: by the place in the root a `$ref` pointer it was handed points at, or by the `$id` of the subschema the first `allOf` entry holds ([#5528](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5528))

## @rjsf/validator-cfworker

- **BREAKING CHANGE:** `customizeValidator()` loses its `T` parameter along with `ValidatorType`, as does the exported `CFWorkerValidator` class (now `CFWorkerValidator<S, F>`): it is `customizeValidator<S, F>()`, and the default export, which no longer carries a `T`, fits every `Form`, typed or not. The first type argument is now the schema type, so drop a leftover data type argument: `customizeValidator<MyData>()` / `ValidatorType<MyData>` fails at the declaration when `MyData` is not assignable to `JSONSchema7`, and otherwise compiles with the schema typed as `MyData`, which may show up only as an error on the `Form`'s `schema` prop, or not at all. See the validator section of the v7 upgrade guide
- `isValid()` logs an error it catches through `logOnce()`, so the same error is not repeated on every re-render. The message names the schema by its `$id`, or by the hash `isValid()` already computes when it has none, so two schemas that fail with the same error text are reported separately.
