It does not say whether the schema or the form data is at fault, because `@cfworker/json-schema`'s `Validator` constructor only dereferences the schema and leaves `$ref` resolution and keyword checks to `validate()`: a schema defect such as a dangling `$ref` or an unparseable `pattern` throws while validating the data, where claiming the data was at fault would send you to debug the wrong thing ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- `validateFormData()` takes an optional trailing `getCustomValidateFormData`, returning the data to hand `customValidate` with the form's defaults applied; `Form` supplies it so those defaults honor its `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior`, which they never did before ([#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385))
- `isValid()` and `rawValidation()` key a schema whose `$id` is the empty string by its hash, so two such schemas no longer evict each other from the cache of built validators ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338))
- The `errors` a validation returns keep an error whose `message` a `transformErrors` cleared whenever a `customValidate` is passed, as they do without one, since `validationDataMerge()` no longer drops the existing errors when their `errorSchema` is empty. A form whose `transformErrors` clears every `message` therefore reports those errors through `onError` instead of submitting when it also has a `customValidate`, as it already did without one ([#5034](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5034))
- `validateFormData()` lists the errors of a `customValidate` that returns an array for the items of an array field and keys them by index in the `errorSchema`, where it left them out of `errors` and let that array replace the validator's own errors for the array field. See the `@rjsf/utils` entry for `validationDataMerge()` and `unwrapErrorHandler()` ([#3827](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3827))
- `isValid()` resolves a `$ref` into a `dependencies` entry named like a keyword, such as `type`, `required` or `properties`, which `@cfworker/json-schema` leaves out of its lookup, wherever it sits: under a property whose name holds a `#`, or in a resource of its own `$id`, by the pointer from the root a form hands, so a form checking the `oneOf` of such a dependency no longer logs `Unresolved $ref`
- `isValid()` registers a root carrying an `$id` under that `$id`, where it registered it under `ROOT_SCHEMA_PREFIX` in its place, so a `$ref` in a 2020-12 root with an `$id`, which resolves against that `$id`, finds the root. A condition holding a `$ref` under such a root took `else` whatever the data: with `{ $schema: 'https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema', $id: 'https://example.com/root.json', $defs: { isB: { properties: { k: { const: 'b' } }, required: ['k'] } }, type: 'object', properties: { k: { type: 'string' } }, if: { $ref: '#/$defs/isB' }, then: { properties: { t: { type: 'string', default: 'THEN' } } }, else: { properties: { e: { type: 'string', default: 'ELSE' } } } }` and the data `{ k: 'b' }`, the defaults are `{ k: 'b', t: 'THEN' }` where they were `{ k: 'b', e: 'ELSE' }`. Options read from such a root match the same way: with `oneOf: [{ $ref: '#/$defs/isA' }, { $ref: '#/$defs/isB' }]`, `isA` and `isB` requiring `k` to be `'a'` and `'b'`, `getFirstMatchingOption({ k: 'b' }, schemaUtils.getRootSchema().oneOf)` gives `1` where it gave `0`. A schema handed under the root's `$id` less any fragment, such as the root itself, is validated as that document rather than beside the registered root, which `@cfworker/json-schema` rejects as a duplicate URI, so `isValid(rootSchema, data, rootSchema)` for a root whose `$id` ends in an empty fragment answers rather than returning `false`
- `isValid()` and `validateFormData()` resolve a `$ref` that escapes a `#` in a name as `%23`, as RFC 6901 spells it, such as `{ $ref: '#/definitions/a%23b' }` held by the root, which `@cfworker/json-schema` reported as an unresolved `$ref`, since it keys the subschemas it looks a `$ref` up in by pointers that keep a `#` as it is

## Dev / docs / playground

- **BREAKING CHANGE** Dropped support for Node 20, 23, 25, and 24 releases before 24.11.0; `engines.node` is now `^22.18.0 || ^24.11.0 || >=26.0.0` across all packages, matching the active Node.js LTS lines, and CI now runs against Node 22, 24, and 26
- Upgraded pnpm from 10.17.1 to 12.6.0, which was previously pinned because pnpm 11+ requires Node >=22.13
- Dropped the `tsx` dev dependency; every Node release the `engines` field now allows strips types natively, so a TypeScript script runs under `node` directly
- The playground imports each shadcn theme's tokens from `src/themes/shadcn`, where a shared `base.css` holds the `@theme inline` mapping the ten themes had in common and each theme file holds only its own tokens ([#5287](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5287))
- Upgraded TypeScript to 7.0.2. The root `typecheck` runs in 5 s instead of 22 s; `@rjsf/docs`'s editor-only tsconfig no longer extends `@tsconfig/docusaurus`, which sets `baseUrl`, an option TypeScript 7 removed (Docusaurus's own `@docusaurus/tsconfig` still sets it too) ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- `module` is now `nodenext` in `tsconfig.base.json` (was `esnext` with `moduleResolution: bundler`), so `tsc` checks the published `lib/` against Node's ESM rules. `@rjsf/chakra-ui` keeps bundler resolution with a comment saying why: `@chakra-ui/react` ships declarations Node's rules cannot resolve. Test files gained `with { type: 'json' }` on JSON imports and import `userEvent` by name, both of which the stricter rules require ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- Enabled `erasableSyntaxOnly`, which rejects `enum`, `namespace` and parameter properties; the four exported enums were the only violations ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- `packages/utils/test` no longer emits declarations for the validator packages' tests to consume. Each validator test project includes the shared schema test suite directly, so every test project is now `noEmit` ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- The root and playground `package.json` now say `"type": "module"`, so the playground typechecks under `nodenext` like every published package; `scripts/get-version-tag.js` is `.cjs` since it uses `require`. The playground's `ajv`, `ajv-i18n` and `@monaco-editor/react` imports use the same Node-correct spellings as the validator tests ([#5244](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5244))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Every package is now ESM-only: the CommonJS, bundled ESM and UMD builds in `dist/` and the `./dist` and `./dist/*.cjs` export paths are gone; `lib/` (per-file ESM plus declarations) is the only published output. `require()` of the packages keeps working on every supported Node version via native `require(esm)` ([#5255](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5255), fixes [#3812](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3812))
- Replaced the per-package `tsc -b` emit plus `esbuild` + `rollup` bundling with a single `tsdown` run driven by one shared `tsdown.base.mts`, which emits `lib/`. The build only transpiles; typechecking is the separate root `tsc --build`, and tsc no longer emits JavaScript (the package tsconfigs are `emitDeclarationOnly`) ([#5255](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5255))
- **BREAKING CHANGE** Upgraded `react` and `react-dom` to 19.2+ across the repo (required by the `@rjsf/mantine` upgrade to Mantine 9) and raised every theme package's `react` peer dependency floor from `>=18` to `>=19`, matching the "React 19 is officially supported on all the themes" policy already declared in the v7 upgrade guide. Fixed the handful of type errors this surfaced from React 19's stricter types (`useRef` without a non-null initial value now returns `RefObject<T | null>`, and a bare `ReactElement`'s props default to `unknown`)
- Bumped `react`/`react-dom`/`@types/react`/`@types/react-dom` further, to 19.3.0. Doing so and running `pnpm install` initially forked the graph into two react copies (19.2.8 and 19.3.0) with `react-dom@19.3.0` paired against `react@19.2.8` in part of the tree, breaking hooks across that boundary. That turned out to be lockfile inertia rather than a real conflict: only the root and playground `package.json` declare `react` directly, every other package has it as a loose peer range (`>=18`), and `pnpm install` only re-resolves entries whose specifier changed — the ~578 peer-resolved `react@19.2.8` entries were left as-is since 19.2.8 still satisfied `>=18`, and `react-dom` (bumped directly) got paired against those stale entries. `pnpm dedupe` re-resolves the whole graph and fixes it without needing a version-pinning override (which would've also silently overridden any third-party package with a legitimately narrower react peer range)
- Fixed the playground crashing with "Cannot destructure property 'fields' of 'undefined'" on load and on every theme switch. It stored the `withTheme()`-created `Form` component directly via `useState(withTheme(...))`/`setFormComponent(withTheme(...))`; now that `withTheme()` returns a plain function instead of a `forwardRef`-wrapped object, React's `typeof` check treats a bare function passed to `useState`/a state setter as a lazy initializer/updater and calls it with no arguments. Both call sites now wrap the value in `() => withTheme(...)`
- The playground's `FormComponent` is now derived with `useMemo(() => withTheme(themes[theme].theme), [themes, theme])` instead of being kept in its own `useState` and manually synced with the `theme` state on every selection. Removes the duplicated state, the `react/hook-use-state` lint suppression, and structurally rules out the `useState(withTheme(...))` crash class fixed above
- Bumped the root `@types/react` floor to `^19.2.18`: `SubmitEvent`, now part of the public `FormProps['onSubmit']` type, was only added to `@types/react` in that release — every earlier 19.2.x patch (verified 19.2.0 through 19.2.17) lacks it and fails to compile against `@rjsf/core`'s published types
- Removed a dead assertion in `Form.props.test.tsx` that waited for React 18's "Function components cannot be given refs" warning; verified via a standalone repro that React 19 emits no such warning for this case, so the branch could never run. The two sibling React-18-only console assertions in this file were already removed
- The playground registers the `@rjsf/core/markdown` renderer; samples retain their per-field markdown flags. ([#5263](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5263))
- Added `oneOf`/`anyOf` fields with constant options and no `type` (including mixed-type constants, boolean constants with a `null` option and a `oneOf` nested behind a `$ref`) to the playground's `One Of` and `Any Of` samples, and matching form snapshot tests for every theme ([#4666](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4666))
- Added `optgroups` documentation to `uiSchema.md` and an `optgroups` example to the playground's widgets sample
- Added a `Property Names` playground sample and documented `propertyNames.enum` key dropdowns in the objects and custom-templates docs ([#4682](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4682))
- Added a shared `hideErrorTests()` suite covering `ui:hideError`, and documented `getVisibleErrors()` and `hasVisibleErrors()` in the utility-function reference, the `ui:hideError` directive, the custom `BaseInputTemplate` example, the custom widget and custom field prop lists, and the v7 upgrade guide. It renders each widget a theme can render — its own registrations merged over core's defaults, so the widgets it inherits still exercise its `BaseInputTemplate` — twice under `ui:hideError`, once with an error and once without, asserting the two are identical and that neither is `aria-invalid`, so a widget deriving its error state from `rawErrors` alone fails whatever prop or class its theme signals invalid through, and a newly registered widget with no fixture fails rather than being skipped. A theme registering a widget the suite has no schema for passes one through its optional `fixtures` argument. It lives in its own file per theme because every render advances React's `useId` counter for the rest of the module, which would otherwise renumber the ids in the `formTests()` snapshots each time a widget is added ([#3684](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3684))
- Documented multiple types in the single-schema docs, the `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType` form prop, the utility-functions reference (`getUnionTypes()`, the `GUESSED_TYPE_FLAG` and `JSON_SCHEMA_TYPES` constants, and the `retrieveSchema()`/`sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` behavior behind them) and the v7 upgrade guide, and replaced the playground's `Fallback` sample with one covering all three fallback cases: a typeless field, a field allowing several types, and an object with unconstrained `additionalProperties` ([#5238](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5238))
- Updated the playground to seed a sample's schema defaults, `ui:initialValue`s included, into its form data on load, since it now controls that data, and to mount a new form for every loaded sample
- Added a `themeTests` suite to `@rjsf/snapshot-tests`, run by `@rjsf/core` and every theme, asserting that `generateTemplates()`, `generateWidgets()` and `generateTheme()` build new maps on every call, checked recursively through every nested map, instead of returning the shared default exports, and that their `memo()`/`forwardRef()` components stay the same instance ([#5311](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5311))
- The playground calls `@rjsf/utils`'s new `resetLogOnce()` when it loads a sample or a shared link, so the uiSchema warning a sample exists to demonstrate is logged again rather than held back by an earlier sample that made the same mistake at the same field id. It deliberately does not re-arm while the schema or uiSchema editor is being typed in: Monaco reports a change per keystroke, so doing it there logs the warning again on every character ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- Upgraded `oxlint` to 1.85.0, `oxlint-tsgolint` to 7.0.2003 and `oxfmt` to 0.70.0, and `.oxlintrc.json` sets `denyWarnings: true`, so any warning fails lint. Removed the type assertions the upgraded `no-unnecessary-type-assertion` rule reports as redundant, and turned off the new React Compiler rules for now along with a few warning-level rules that flag deliberate patterns ([#5366](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5366))
- Documented what a schema with no usable type of its own does with an `anyOf`/`oneOf`, an `enum`, a `const` or a component `ui:widget`, in the multiple-types section of the single-schema docs, and noted in the `ui:fieldReplacesAnyOrOneOf` reference that the flag needs a `ui:field` that resolves ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333))
- Enabled pnpm's global virtual store (`virtualStoreType: global`), so a git worktree's `node_modules` links into one store shared by every checkout on the machine instead of building its own `node_modules/.pnpm`: a fresh worktree installs in about 2 s instead of 13 s. CI uses it too, since pnpm only turns it off in CI when the setting is left at its default. `.pnpmfile.mjs` gives every dependency that peers on `react` or `react-dom` an optional peer on the matching `@types` package, and a `packageExtensions` entry declares the `react-dom` that `@mantine/hooks` imports; without them the global store hides React's types from those libraries (their props typecheck as `any`) and `react-dom` from the playground's Vite build. Dropped pnpm settings that pnpm 11 replaced or that no longer did anything: `onlyBuiltDependencies` (duplicated `allowBuilds`), an expired `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` list, two scoped overrides already covered by unscoped ones, the `form-data`, `nx>tmp` and `http-proxy-middleware` overrides (their dependents now require a fixed version themselves), the `undici` override (it held jsdom 30 on undici 7, below the `^8.9.0` jsdom declares), and an `.npmrc` that restated the default registry
- Moved `@rjsf/core`'s theme snapshot tests to `packages/snapshot-tests/test`. `@rjsf/snapshot-tests` depends on `@rjsf/core`, so core's dev dependency on it for these tests was a workspace cycle that pnpm warned about on every install. The `getTestIds` mock the shared suites relied on moved from each suite into `@rjsf/snapshot-tests`'s `src/setup.ts`, which every package that runs them lists in its vitest `setupFiles`: registered inside a suite, it only reached components imported after that suite, so it silently stopped working whenever a test file imported its `Form` first. (see `@rjsf/snapshot-tests`)
- The playground builds its first render's state from a shared link up front. A link naming a theme or validator the playground no longer has opens with the default one, one naming a removed sample opens its own schema and data with the Simple sample selected, and a link that doesn't decode to an object shows the "Unable to load form setup data." alert after the page first renders and opens the Simple sample ([#5381](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5381))
- Turned on the React Compiler lint rules `react/immutability`, `react/preserve-manual-memoization`, `react/purity`, `react/globals` and `react/display-name` as errors, fixing every violation without disable comments or overrides ([#5410](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5410))
- The repo is type-checked as two `tsconfig.typecheck*.json` programs, one under `nodenext` and one under `bundler` resolution for chakra-ui and the playground, instead of one per package, and every package declares the `@rjsf/source` export condition so both resolve `@rjsf/*` to source without a build. Lint overrides keep the published packages' `src` off the Node and Vitest globals the programs share with the tests and off Node's built-in modules, and the validators' `src` off the DOM globals they list; comments in `.oxlintrc.json` say to copy a new root entry, or add a new published package, into those overrides. ([#5372](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5372))
- `pnpm run lint` runs `oxlint --type-check` on those two programs, so it reports TypeScript's errors along with the lint rules', and `pnpm run typecheck` and its CI step are gone. A cold run takes about 6 s and peaks around 7 GB, where lint alone took 27 s and the separate typecheck 24 s. Lint and editors pick a file's program from the nearest `tsconfig.json`, so the root `tsconfig.json` references the two programs and no package has a `tsconfig.json` of its own. Each package's build config is a `tsconfig.lib.json` that only extends `tsconfig.base.json` (chakra-ui adds bundler resolution), which tsdown reads for its declarations; `tsconfig.test.json`, the `test/` tsconfigs, the playground's tsconfig, the project references, and the chakra-ui `type-check` and validator-cfworker `typecheck` scripts that built them are gone. The published `lib/` is unchanged. `.oxlintrc.json` now ignores only `packages/*/lib`, rather than every `lib` folder, so `packages/shadcn/src/lib` is linted and type-checked ([#5372](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5372))
- CI now runs `pnpm run cs-check`, in a new `checks` job that also takes over `pnpm dedupe --check`, `lint` and `knip` from the build matrix ([#5476](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5476))
- Turned on the lint rules `react/todo`, `react/rule-suppression`, `react/hooks`, `react/jsx-no-literals`, `react/no-clone-element`, `react/no-react-children` and `react/prefer-function-component` as errors. The first two fail lint on what makes the React Compiler skip a component; `react/jsx-no-literals` is off for the playground, the tests and shadcn's `components/ui` primitives, and the last three for the tests. The playground's `DemoFrame` no longer clones `container` and `window` props onto the form, which nothing read ([#5482](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5482))
- Documented the `itemLabel` array option and the `itemButtonProps` prop of `ArrayFieldItemButtonsTemplate`, and added `itemLabel` examples to the playground's Arrays sample ([#5474](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5474))
- Removed the explicit `any`s in package `src` that stood on their own, 116 down to 61: theme handlers take their parameter types from the library's own prop, the react-bootstrap and shadcn `TextareaWidget`s drop their `options: any` override, and the playground types its editors, live settings (now picked from `FormProps`, which caught an initial `arrayMinItems` of `'populate'` the type never allowed, and two initial keys that named no prop), form ref and theme entry. Of the 61 left, 28 are declarations in `@rjsf/utils`' `types.ts`. The others take or pass on a value those leave as `any`: a widget's `value` and what its `onChange` reports (`ArrayField`, `DateWidget`, `TimeWidget`, `useAltDateWidgetProps()`, shadcn's `FancySelect` and the playground's widget samples), `rawValidation<Result = any>` in the validators, and the `data` parameter of `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` (its `oldData` and `oldDataForPairing` parameters, which held two more, were removed later) and the `formData` parameter of `getClosestMatchingOption()`. One stands on its own: the accumulator `mergeDefaultsWithFormData()` builds its generic `T` in ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- Fixed the playground crashing into its error boundary when a JSON editor held a value of the wrong kind, such as `5` or `[]` as the schema: each editor checks what it parsed and marks anything else invalid. The options drawer's `arrayMinItems.populate` defaults to `all`, where its `populate` was none of the values it offers, and a shared link's `validate`, `useFallbackField` and `arrayMinItems: 'populate'`, which the playground's initial settings carried through v6, are left out when it loads, as is an `arrayMinItems.populate` of `'populate'`, which a change in the drawer wrote into a link, except that a `validate` of `true`, the live validation toggle of an early v5 link, turns live validation on ([#5488](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5488))
- The docs teach a field's plan for its error state and messages, `presentation.errors.messages`, `hasWidgetErrors()` and `hasTemplateErrors()`, in place of `getVisibleErrors()`, `hasVisibleErrors()` and `rawErrors`: the custom `BaseInputTemplate` example, the field, widget and container template prop lists, the `ui:hideError` directive, the utility-function reference and the v7 upgrade guide ([#5325](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5325))
- The playground's programmatic Reset puts the form back to the data it was loaded with, the sample, the shared link or the formData editor's last edit, rather than to the latest edit, since the form is seeded apart from the copy `onChange` keeps for the editor and the share link; a theme switch, which remounts the form, seeds it with the data it holds then ([#5556](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5556))
- Fixed 58 doc examples that imported `Form` as a named export of `@rjsf/core`, which only has a default export. They now use `import Form from '@rjsf/core'`, and the examples that import `ThemeProps` or `FormProps` from it import them as types, fixing [#5568](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5568)
